# Reverse-engineering log — RE2-64

Chronological. Each entry states what was believed, what the measurement said, and what changed.
Detailed write-ups live in `docs/log/`.

| milestone | outcome | detail |
|---|---|---|
| M0 | MIPS big-endian decompiles through REA→Ghidra at all | `tools/mkmips.py` known-truth fixture |
| M1 | dump identified: 512 Mbit, z64, entry `0x80000480`, `NREE` rev 1 USA | `docs/log/M1-rom-identification.md` |
| M2 | splat splits code `0x1000`–`0x12140`, 243 functions, `unknown 67 MB (99.89 %)` | `docs/log/M2-code-data-boundaries.md`, `docs/log/M2-boot-code-decompiles.md` (the partial that first reached the decompiler) |
| M3 | naming the entry's callees gained **no** coverage — labels do not discover code | `docs/log/M3-libultra-naming-and-research.md` |
| M4 | the ROM is banked (30 687 pointers over all 64 RDRAM buckets); the `0x128B8` table is a libultra I/O dispatch table, not a load table | `docs/log/M4-bank-structure.md` |
| M5 | two PI DMA programmers located by behaviour | `docs/log/M5-pi-loader-found.md` |
| M6 | the ROM→VRAM map is *not* a stored `(rom, vram)` table — three hypotheses eliminated | `docs/log/M6-mapping-not-a-table.md` |
| M7 | **the mapping was wrong** (`0x480` low); every earlier address repaired | `docs/log/M7-mapping-correction.md` |
| M8 | **the map found**: 16-byte cart descriptor table at vram `0x80012150`, 66 records | `docs/log/M8-file-table-found.md` |
| M9 | **the cartridge unpacked**: 4 761 zlib members, 1 227 JPEG frames, an MPEG-1 movie | `docs/log/M9-unpacked.md` |
| M30 | k0 trace from the vector to the fault: the handler loads k0 with `lw k0,0x2950(k0)` at `0x80000768`, reading vram `0x80012950`, which is **0 in the cartridge** and never written in this run (watchpoint: no hits). Not a register bug — a **missing initialisation dependency**. Execution then faults on `sd t1,0x20(k0)` at `0x80000770` | `format_notes.md` §5b |
| M29 | **Two mapping corrections applied**: `0x80000180` is RDRAM (phys `0x180`), not cartridge ROM; `0xBFC00380` is PIF boot ROM; `Cause 0x2000` is IP6, and the RCP interrupt is **IP3 = 0x400**. D1: MI model (`0xA4300008`/`0xA430000C`) asserts IP3 only when pending&mask — live run shows Cause `0x400`. D2: **the game installs all four vectors itself** (`lui k0,0x8000`), `E_VEC` jumps to **`0x800006d0`**. D3: dispatch reaches `0x800006d0`, runs ~0xa0 bytes, then faults at `0x80000770` on `sd t1,0x20(k0)` with k0=0 | `format_notes.md` §5b |
| M28 | **CP0 implemented and validated 5/5** (BadVAddr/Count/Compare/Status/Cause/EPC; acceptance IE&!EXL&!ERL&(IP&IM); BD; ERET restores EPC). Vector selection now uses Status.BEV: `0x80000180` (BEV=0) / `0xBFC00380` (BEV=1), and `0x80000000` is the TLB-refill vector. **Live run: the VI interrupt fires at `0x80088668` with Cause `0x2000` and EPC correct — then the CPU executes uninitialised RAM at the vector, because `0x80000180` maps to ROM `0xd00` (IPL3), 0x300 bytes before the game image.** Two CP0 bugs found by the tests (pending-bit convention, ERET delay slot) | `format_notes.md` §5b |
| M27 | Send-site census: **51 osSendMesg sites, none with a literal queue argument** (17 loaded, 21 reg+offset, 11 copied) — so the producer is indirect by design and a constant scan could not find it. PI/VI references concentrate in the boot bank (82/19) vs 1/1 in stage-2 and 0 in overlays. Event-delivery trace: exactly **two message ops, both blocking receives**, zero sends before deadlock. Harness limit recorded: `mfc0` returns 0 so CP0 Status=0 and **no interrupt can be recognised** | `format_notes.md` §5b |
| M25 | Constant scan: `0x80139f10` is drained by **thread 1** and registered by thread 7's callee; `0x8012bd30` is owned by **thread 9** and **no mapped code ever sends to it**. The missing edge is a producer for `0x8012bd30`, which explains why the M24 ticks woke nobody. Commit A/B/C all confirmed done | `format_notes.md` §5b |
| M26 | Execution checkpoints: `checkpoint/` with the 7 requested files plus `run_digest.json`; two independent runs give the **identical digest**, so reproducibility is checked, not asserted | `format_notes.md` §5b |
| `docs/log/M245-both-toolchains-run.md`, `tools/toolchain/provision.sh`, `docs/TOOLCHAIN.md` |
| M245 | **both toolchains run: IDO 5.3 and 7.1 native, GNU as/ld for mips** | Stage 4's prerequisite is a compiler **and** an assembler; both exist now. Installed: `build/toolchain/binutils/bin/mips-linux-gnu-{as,ld}` (**GNU Binutils 2.42**), and **IDO 5.3 and 7.1** (`cc as0 as1 ugen uopt ujoin usplit upas uld`) by static recompilation of the original IRIX binaries -- the IRIX binaries are **not fetched by this repository** (a decompilation that redistributes its compiler has a licensing problem rather than a shortcut), so `tools/toolchain/provision.sh` takes the owner's `ido-static-recomp-main.zip` via `IDO_RECOMP_ZIP`/`IDO_RECOMP_DIR` and recompiles locally. **The proof is a probe, not a directory listing**: `int f(int a,int b){return a+b;}` at `-O2 -non_shared -G 0 -mips2` gives `.text = 03e00008 00851021` (`jr ra; addu v0,a0,a1`), the N64-era form with the add in the delay slot. **Six failures were found by running it, not by reading about it**: `--disable-gas=no` is not a flag (my invention); configure reported FAIL with no reason because it was run by a relative path from the build dir with output to `/dev/null`; the bundled zlib collides with the macOS 14 SDK because `zutil.h` defines `fdopen(fd,mode) NULL` when undetected (three outside overrides failed; a verified `sed` removing all three copies asserts the count); a `grep -c` assertion printed `0` **and** exited 1, so `\|\| echo 0` yielded `"0\n0"`; `info-recursive` aborts with no texinfo on the host, and `MAKEINFO` must be passed as a **make command-line variable** because the Makefile defines it; and IDO said "cannot execute binary file" because `USR_LIB` pointed at `ido/<ver>/usr/lib`, so `cc` exec'd the IRIX `cfe` -- flatten the native tools plus `err.english.cc` into `build/toolchain/ido-<ver>/`. **What it does not unlock**: a linker script per image and a working `uld` invocation -- `as`/`ld` existing is not a build that reproduces an image, which is stage 5 and starts with the boot image whose map is already verified. Also recorded from `command-tab/awesome-n64-development`: `m2c`/`m3c`, `asm-differ`, `y64_linker`, `ultralib`/`libreultra`, `n64sym`, `f3dex2` | `docs/log/M245-both-toolchains-run.md`, `tools/toolchain/provision.sh` |
| M246 | **the compile-probe corpus: IDO is refuted, and 5.3 and 7.1 are the same compiler** | Stage 4's question -- what produced these bytes -- answered by a corpus of five C snippets compiled with every available profile and diffed against the image. **IDO 5.3 and IDO 7.1 emit byte-identical code on every probe**, so they were one hypothesis, not two. And **neither matches**: the empty function matches all three profiles (it has no code to get wrong) and every probe with a body differs -- p2 shows IDO **homing** both arguments (`sw a0,0(sp)`) where the image has none, p4 a register allocation (`addu v1,a0,a2` against IDO's `addu v0,a0,a2`). The diff is not the argument though, because a wrong C guess and a wrong compiler look identical in a byte diff: **p3 differs at its first word only because an object holds relocations, not addresses** (`lui t6,0x0` against `lui v0,0x8013`). The load-bearing evidence is a **census that no C guess can spoil**, because a move is the least ambiguous C there is: over 1 938 stage-2 ranges the cartridge writes `addu rd,rs,$zero` **6 560** times and `or rd,rs,$zero` **zero** times, while IDO asked directly for `int f(int a){return a;}` emits `03e00008 00801025` = `jr ra; or v0,a0,zero` against the image's `jr ra; addu v0,a1,zero`. A compiler that emits `or` for every move did not produce this cartridge. **IDO is refuted, both versions**, and the next candidate is the **GNU MIPS toolchain** (`mipse-ultra-gcc`, gcc 2.7.2/2.8.1) -- what the N64 SDK disc in the owner's Downloads carries; the corpus already takes another profile as one tuple. This is the outcome the stage was designed to produce: a refutation with a number instead of a hundred functions translated against the wrong compiler. Named, not hidden: **no probe has been linked yet**, so p3 says nothing until `uld` is in the loop | `docs/log/M246-the-compile-probe-corpus.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M247 | **gcc 2.7.2 matches: three of four probes byte-for-byte, against IDO's one** | M246 refuted IDO on the move idiom and the SDK's makefiles named `CC = gcc`; this builds **gcc 2.7.2.3 for mips-elf** (the `mipse` target) and runs the same corpus. **p1 (empty), p2 (return an argument) and p4 (byte-copy loop) are byte-identical**; p3 is the relocation-bearing probe and is unfalsifiable at object level. IDO reaches **1 of 4** — the empty function — and fails p2 structurally, because it homes both arguments to the stack and no C fixes that. Per profile: `gcc -O` **3/4**, `gcc -O2` **3/4**, `ido-5.3` 1/4, `ido-7.1` 1/4. **Two corrections to M246 fell out of it.** First, `move` is a pseudo-instruction the **assembler** expands: gcc emits `move $2,$4` under `.set nomacro`, modern gas picks `or`, the 1998 SDK's `as` picked `addu` — so the 6 560 : 0 census was measuring an assembler, and still refutes IDO (whose `as1` emits `or`) but does not by itself refute a compiler; the corpus now performs that expansion explicitly. Second, the **C's loop shape is part of the probe**: p4's `while` form gets its test hoisted, and `do { … } while` reproduces the 36 bytes exactly. Building a 1996 compiler on a 2026 host took **six patches, none of which touch codegen**: a `<varargs.h>` shim, `const` on `sys_nerr`/`sys_errlist` in four files, force `HAVE_VPRINTF` so the ANSI branch compiles, `CLIB=-lm` instead of the SGI `-lmld`, build with **gcc-16** rather than clang (lvalue casts, and the `obstack_*_grow` macros rewritten as two statements), and skip `deduced.h`/`enquire.o`, which want `.stabs`. The resulting toolchain is `cc1` (1996) → `as` (2.42). Left before a matching build: a **linked** comparison for any probe that names a symbol, a flag sweep on a probe where `-O` and `-O2` actually differ, and p1's 16-byte object padding, which a linker section removes | `docs/log/M247-gcc-272-matches.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py`, `tools/toolchain/provision.sh` |
| M248 | **the linked comparison: p3 resolves and the residue is two words, in the assembler** | M247 left p3 "unfalsifiable until linked"; this runs `cc1` -> `as` -> **`ld` with a generated script placing `.text` at `0x80025D60`** -> compare. The script is generated **from the symbol names**, because this project already names globals by address: every undefined `D_XXXXXXXX` becomes `D_XXXXXXXX = 0xXXXXXXXX;`, and a name without an address fails the link rather than placing it at zero. Two things had to be right, both measured: **`as` needs `-G 0` as well as `cc1`** (otherwise the definition lands in `.sbss` with `R_MIPS_GPREL16` and the link dies "small-data section too large"), and **the source condition was inverted from my first guess** -- the image branches `beq v0,zero,+3`, i.e. *skip the store when zero*, so the C is `if (D != 0) D2 = 1;`, and a byte diff cannot tell a wrong branch polarity from a wrong compiler. **Result: 6 of 8 words match.** Both sides do the same three operations, but the image hoists the address `lui v1` into the **`beq` delay slot** and stores through `v1`, while ours fills the slot with the `addiu` and stores through the assembler temp `$at` -- that is the **assembler's expansion of `sw rt,symbol` plus its delay-slot fill**, the same class as M247's `move` finding, not a compiler difference and not fixable in the C. Two ways forward, both cheap: build the SDK-era `as` (binutils ~2.6) and re-link, or model the two expansion rules explicitly as the corpus already models `move`; the first is more faithful and is now the only missing piece of the toolchain. Corpus now 6 cases, the last asserting p3 is *compared after linking* -- the point being that its verdict means something, not that it passes | `docs/log/M248-the-linked-comparison.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M249 | **the SDK-era assembler: `move` explained, and p3's residue is not the assembler** | M248 left two candidate explanations for p3's two-word residue and this builds the SDK-era tool to settle it: **binutils 2.7 (1996) `as`** expands `int f(int a){return a;}` to `00801021` **`addu`** where 2.42's gas and IDO's `as1` both give `or` -- so the `move` -> `addu` rewrite the corpus had been doing by hand **is what the real tool does**, and with 2.7 wired in the corpus rewrites nothing and p1/p2/p4 are byte-exact on their own. **It does not close p3, and that is the useful part**: re-linked through the SDK-era `as` at `0x80025D60` the result is **identical to the 2.42 one** -- 6 of 8 words, both gas versions expanding `sw $2,symbol` into `lui $at` + `sw $2,lo($at)` and both filling the `beq` delay slot with the preceding `addiu`, while the image has `lui $3` -- a **general** register, not the assembler temp -- placed *before* the `addiu` and stored through. So the residue is **compiler-side**: gcc must have materialised the address into `$3` itself, and two source idioms tested (`volatile` on the target; an address in a pointer local) do not reproduce it -- the first keeps the schedule, the second emits `li $3,0x800130000` + `addiu`, four instructions for a two-instruction store. That leaves one specific next test: **gcc 2.8.1** (1998, what a 1999 SDK would ship), because p1/p2/p4 are three-instruction functions that cannot distinguish 2.7.2 from 2.8.1 while a store's address materialisation is exactly what changed between them. Two host problems recorded not hidden: **2.7's `ld` writes an empty file here** (linking therefore uses 2.42's `ld` plus `objcopy`, because 2.7's `.symtab` `sh_info` is rejected by modern `ld`), and **2.7's `configure` needs a modern `config.sub`** (it calls it with old-style multiple arguments). `provision.sh` now builds all five toolchains and its doctor reports each | `docs/log/M249-the-sdk-era-assembler.md`, `tools/toolchain/provision.sh`, `tools/compile_probe.py`, `docs/TOOLCHAIN.md` |
| M250 | **gcc 2.8.1: four of four probes byte-exact, and the compiler is identified** | M249 localised p3's residue to the compiler, so this builds **gcc 2.8.1** -- the 1998 release a 1999 SDK would ship -- and it closes the corpus: **p1, p2, p3 (linked) and p4 are all byte-identical**, where gcc 2.7.2 gets 3/4 and IDO 1/4. `p3 want = got = 3c028013 8c4214cc 10400003 3c038013 24020001 ac6244c0 03e00008 00000000`. **The two gccs differ exactly where M249 predicted**: 2.8.1 materialises the store's address into a **general register** (`lui $3`) and its delay-slot filler hoists that `lui` into the `beq` slot, while 2.7.2 hands the address to the assembler, which uses `$at` and fills the slot with the `addiu` -- one instruction's scheduling, two words in the image, and the difference between a compiler that reproduces this cartridge and one that does not. So the toolchain is **`cc1` (gcc 2.8.1, mips-elf) -> `as` (binutils 2.7, `move` -> `addu`) -> `ld` (2.42, with `objcopy`)**, and `-O` vs `-O2` remain indistinguishable on this corpus (both 4/4) -- that is the next probe's job. Building 2.8.1 on a modern host took **five more patches, none of them codegen**: 2.8.1's `config.guess` predates aarch64-darwin (2.42's copies); its host case ends in `exit 1`, so a `*-*-darwin*` branch uses `xm-linux.h` **plus** `xm-std32.h` (no 64-bit host config exists, and the exit codes live there); `xm_file` wants bare names because the Makefile prefixes `config/`; **`config.h` is generated with host macros only** and only `crtstuff.c` includes `tm.h`, so appending `#include "tm.h"` is what lets cc1 compile at all; and the `obstack` grow macros need the same two-statement rewrite as 2.7.2. `make cc1` also skips the standalone `cccp`, which is not in this pipeline and which cannot see the target macros. **Named so it is not assumed**: four probes are not a compiler proof -- they cover a leaf, an argument move, a linked global store and a delayed loop, four functions of 3 638; the next step extends the corpus with a `switch` (86 dispatch heads to draw from), a struct copy, an integer divide and a floating-point expression | `docs/log/M250-gcc-281-four-of-four.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py`, `tools/toolchain/provision.sh` |
| M251 | **the four extra classes: the cartridge has no cheap probe for any of them** | M250 asked for a `switch`, a struct copy, an integer divide and an FP expression. All four are **attempted and refused, each with a measurement**. First route: compile a snippet with gcc 2.8.1 and **search all 36 images for the exact bytes** (padding cut at `jr ra`) -- **0 occurrences for all six snippets**, and the informative one is `float f(float a,float b){return a*b;}` at **8 bytes**: this cartridge contains **no function that returns the product of its two float arguments**, so a snippet cannot become a probe by search. (The first attempt searched the *whole* `.text`, which the assembler pads to 16 or 48 bytes, so every pattern missed for a reason unrelated to the compiler.) Second route: reconstruct the C of the smallest real function per class -- and **two of the four are not functions**: the divide candidate `0x8006e8bc` is a **fragment** (`lh v0,0(s1)`, `div v0,s0`, the full zero/overflow checks, `mflo`, then **`j 0x8006b...`**, a tail jump, so the range ends inside a larger function), and the FP candidate `0x80083e60` computes with **`f2`, which no o32 argument register carries**. The switch class is the 86 dispatch heads, whose arms are the table's **relocations** -- comparable only with every arm placed, i.e. a per-function link, not a snippet -- and no function in the cartridge copies three consecutive words between two pointers. The divide near-miss is worth keeping: `void f(short *p,int q){*p=*p/q;}` compiles to the same 64-byte sequence with `a0`/`a1` where the image has `s1`/`s0` -- exactly what a fragment looks like. **The compiler identification stands where M250 left it** (gcc 2.8.1, 4/4, including a linked probe 2.7.2 cannot reproduce) but is now bounded honestly: the cheap classes are exhausted and the next four need **source reconstruction** (stage 3), which the tool records in `CLASSES_WITHOUT_A_PROBE` rather than leaving invisible | `docs/log/M251-the-four-classes-refused.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M252 | **the linked switch head: the dispatch sequence is exact, and the one differing instruction is the arms'** | Route 2 from M251. Head `0x8004fd1c` -- bound 5, index an incoming argument, no transform -- is compiled as a five-case switch with gcc 2.8.1, assembled with binutils 2.7, `objcopy`-rewritten (2.7's `.symtab`), and linked with `.text` at the head **and `.rodata` at `0x800b8bd0`**, the image's own table address -- placing the table is what makes the `lui`/`addiu` pair comparable at all. **Two of the ten instructions cannot be compared and the tool names them**: the `beq` offset (`1040005e` vs `10400011`) is the distance to the default arm, i.e. the arms' total size, and **the delay slot** -- the image schedules the **arm prologue** `addu a3,a0,zero` into it (`00803821`) while ours had nothing to fill it with, so the `lui` took the slot (`3c02800c`). **Everything else is byte-identical**, and as a sequence the seven-word table dispatch matches exactly: `3c02800c 24428bd0 00061880 00621821 8c620000 00400008 00000000` -- so the compiler reproduces the `sltiu` bound and the register it tests, the `lui`/`addiu` split of the table address, the `sll` scale, the `addu` index, the `lw`, the `jr` and its `nop`. **The delay slot is a result, not noise**: `addu a3,a0,zero` means the arm bodies read the first argument out of `a3`, and my five cases (`a`, `b`, `a+b`, `a-b`, `a^b`) keep it in `a0`, so there is no move to schedule -- recovering those arms is source reconstruction, while the head itself is now measured and matched. Corpus: **p1-p4 byte-exact, p6 = bound check + 7-word dispatch exact**, and the refused classes drop from four to three | `docs/log/M252-the-linked-switch-head.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M253 | **three more dispatch heads: bounds 5/6/7/7, four index sources, and two layout facts** | M252 built the machinery for one head; this adds three chosen for variety -- **p7 bound 7 on the byte at `ctx+6`** (table `0x800b8f78`), **p8 bound 6 on a global byte** (`0x800b69b0`) and **p9 bound 7 on a command byte after `andi 0xff`** (`0x800b96a8`), alongside p6's bound 5 on an incoming argument (`0x800b8bd0`). Each is compiled as a switch with that head's case count and linked with `.rodata` at that head's own table address. **All four reproduce the bound and the `sll 2`**, with four different index registers (`v1` twice, `a0`, and one reachable only after an `andi`) -- the construction is the same and the numbers in it are the image's. **The delay slot absorbs an instruction and which one depends on the head**: the image puts the **arm prologue** (`addu a3,a0,zero`) in p6's slot, the **table's first half** (`lui`) in p7's, a **frame store** (`sw s0,0(sp)`) in p8's and the **index scale** (`sll`) in p9's, while ours is always the `lui` -- so the slot compares the *arms*, not the compiler, and the signature compares bound, shift and table address instead, saying plainly that the op sequence is not compared. **Two layout facts, reported not smoothed**: the image's tables are **8-byte aligned** (`0x800b8f78`, `0x800b96a8` are 8-aligned but not 16), and gcc's `.align 3` is satisfied by any 16-aligned section start, so a script placing `.rodata` at an 8-aligned address gets the section rounded up and the table lands **+8**; `ALIGN(8)` fixes one head and not another because what precedes the table inside `.rodata` differs, and **p6 and p8 land exactly**. Not established: the arms (untranslated), the index *expressions* (documented from `head_index.json`, not byte-compared -- the load precedes the `sltiu` and its position depends on the prologue), and any `-O`/`-O2` difference (none seen yet). Corpus now **12 cases** | `docs/log/M253-three-more-dispatch-heads.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M254 | **p6's arms recovered: the delay slot matched, and the rest is a reconstruction in progress** | M253 argued the delay slot is the *arms'* business; this tests it by recovering p6's five arms from the disassembly and re-compiling the whole 392-byte function. Read from the instructions, the arms are a **four-byte state machine** (`s[0]` a mode byte, `s[1..3]` a counter and two limits, `a1` a `short *` output, `a2` the index): arm0 `*p = s[3] << 4`; arm1 `*p = (s[1]/s[2]) << 4` with a `s[1] == s[2]*s[3]-1` early return; arm2 the same with a wrap to 0; arm3 the same with `s[1] -= s[2]` and `s[0] = 132`; arm4 decrements first and fires `s[0] = 131`. Four of the five carry gcc's `divu` zero check, which dominates the byte count. **The delay slot matched**: my compiled head contains `addu a3,a0,zero` exactly as the image does, because the recovered arms read the pointer through `a3` and the allocator put it there — before the arms existed there was nothing to fill the slot and the `lui` took it (M252), so M253's claim holds in its strongest form. **The head is 9 of 10 words and its one difference is the `beq` offset** (`0x5e` vs `0x62`) — the distance to the default arm, i.e. the arms' total size, mine being 24 bytes too large. Per region: **arm0 exact (4/4)**, **arm1 13 of 19** (first difference the operand order of the `mult` that computes `s[2]*s[3]`), **arms 2-4 wrong** and their first differences are telling — the image loads where mine stores (`lbu v0,1(a3)` vs `sb v0,1(a3)`), so I increment the counter *before* the division and the original divides first. **27 of 98 words, 416 B against 392 B**: a reconstruction in progress, said in those words rather than rounded up. The delay-slot question is closed; what it leaves is the ordinary stage-3 question of writing the arms with the original's order of operations | `docs/log/M254-recovering-p6s-arms.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M284 | **the arm-shaped family opens with `0x8006fc5c`: 53 of 53, on the second measurement** | Both partials are parked as pins (`0x80024558` `>= 136`, `0x8007e590` `>= 226` with `case3 == 113`) and the next target was chosen by rule -- **the family `-g` unlocked, smallest first**. A scan for the dispatch idiom over the whole image returns **five** sites: `0x8006e6b8` (29 words of arms but **not a function start**, its enclosing code sits in an index gap), `0x8006fc5c` (**53 words**, with `0x8006fca0`'s seven-way dispatch inside it -- the smallest real member), `0x8006ff48` (98, bound 44), `0x800704e0` (123, bound 44), and `0x800701d8` (already done). `FUN_8006fc5c` comes out **53 of 53 byte-exact with its 7-word table 7 of 7**, and it is a *different arm shape* from `0x800701d8`: these arms are **stores**, not loads. It reads `p = ctx->f1c`, takes `a = *(s8 *)(p+2)`, `idx = *(s8 *)(p+3)` as the key and `v = *(u16 *)(p+4)`, computes `rec = *(u32 *)(ctx->f154 + 0x234) + a * 376` (visible as `(a*3)<<4 - a` then `<<3`), writes `v` into one of seven offsets -- `rec+0x60` plus `rec+0xcc = FUN_800844cc((s16)v)` with the sign extension readable as `sll`/`sra`, `rec+0x62`/`rec+0xce` as bytes, then `rec+2`, `rec+0`, `rec+0x58`, `rec+0x5a`, `rec+0x5c` as halfwords -- advances `ctx->f1c` by 6 and returns 1. **The one thing wrong on the first compile (41 of 53) was the arm order**: ascending is what a human writes, and the image's arm addresses say the source wrote **2, 3, 0, 1, 4, 5, 6** -- since cc1 emits case bodies in source order (M278's fixture) and the table maps index to arm, **the arm order in the image is a fact about the source and can be read straight off the jump table** by sorting `table[k]`; ascending is merely the common case, not the default. That reorder took 41 -> **53 of 53**. `0x8006fc5c` enters the probe as two **exact** pins, corpus **36 cases, all passing**, suite **72 suites / 1005 cases** with **725** needing the cartridge. Recovered functions are now **ten**, of which **eight are byte-exact** (46, 53, 53, 84, 98, 141, 167, 206 words), the two partials unchanged at 136/139 and 226/229 and both pinned. The family still has three unmapped members -- `0x8006ff48` (98), `0x800704e0` (123) and the embedded `0x8006e6b8`, whose enclosing function has to be found first -- so the next two targets are already sized and identified, which is the point of choosing by rule | `docs/log/M284-the-arm-shaped-family-opens-with-0x8006fc5c.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py`, `tools/progress_map.py` |

| M282 | **the gas flag ladder, and a correction to M281: `-O0` cannot work, `-O10` can** | M281 said `-g` stops the assembler's delay-slot swap and that "`-O0` sets `mips_optimize` to 1". The second half is **wrong**. Measured on the 44-arm discriminator: no flag / `-O0` / `-O1` / `-O2` all give **100 object words and 28 of 141**, while **`-O10` and `-g` give 144 words and 141 of 141** (`--gdwarf2` is rejected by this as outright). The reason is a one-character bug in `binutils-2.7/gas/config/tc-mips.c`: the handler is `if (arg && arg[1] == '0') mips_optimize = 1; else mips_optimize = 2;` -- it tests **`arg[1]`, not `arg[0]`**, so for `-O0` the argument text is `"0"`, `arg[1]` is the NUL terminator, the condition is false and `mips_optimize` stays at the default **2** with the swap still running; `-O10` has `arg = "10"` and works by accident. The supported route is the other one in the same file (`if (mips_debug == 2) mips_optimize = 0;`), i.e. **`-g`**, which is what the corpus pins and what all nine assembler calls now pass -- it does not depend on the bug, and its `.mdebug` section is discarded by every link script. **Corrected, not edited**: M281's note stands as written and this row is the correction, per the project's rule (M262, M266, M270, M275). Nothing that depended on the pin moves: `0x800701d8` stays **141/141 byte-exact** with jump table **44/44**, corpus **34 cases, all passing**, seven of nine recovered functions byte-exact (`46`, `53`, `84`, `98`, `141`, `167`, `206`), suite **72 suites / 1003 cases**, **723** needing the cartridge. The generalisable part is sharp: **an assembler flag that looks like a no-op may be a misfeature, so measure the ladder rather than reason from the manual**, and when one `char` index decides whether a pipeline reproduces the cartridge, use the spelling the tool documents rather than the one that works by coincidence | `docs/log/M282-the-gas-flag-ladder-and-a-correction.md` |
| M281 | **`as -g`: the missing assembler flag, and `0x800701d8` byte-exact at 141 of 141** | M280's two experiments were run in order. **(a) The compiler flags are uniquely pinned**: rebuilding each recovered source with `-O1`, `-fno-delayed-branch`, and both, destroys every match -- `0x8004fd1c` 98 -> 26/0/2, `0x80059868` 167 -> 48/6/5, `0x80059530` 206 -> 36/8/13, `0x800acae4` 53 -> 7/3/2, `0x8007e590` 226 -> 31/7/3 -- so `-O2` with the delayed-branch pass on is the only setting that reproduces anything, and the arm shape is not reachable by relaxing a compiler flag. (The negative result pointed the way: `-fno-delayed-branch` makes every function *bigger*, because cc1 then emits its own slot `nop`s.) **(b) The discriminator is the assembler, and it is image-wide**: M280's framing -- "the image's cc1 scheduled each return" -- is **wrong**, and one scan refutes it: **of the 2 237 `jr ra` in the image, not one has a load in its delay slot** (the population is `addiu sp` 1 371, `nop` 365, stores 265, alu 160, `jr` 160), so the original's assembler *never* swapped a branch with the preceding instruction in 1.9 MB of code. binutils 2.7's `tc-mips.c` states why ours did: the swap is gated on `mips_optimize >= 2`, guarded by a label on the branch, by any emitted `nop`, and by `.set noreorder`, and `mips_optimize` **defaults to 2** -- only gas's own `-g` (0) and `-O0` (1, not 0) lower it. cc1's bare `j $31` per arm was correct all along; gas moved the preceding load into the slot, which is still *correct* code (the slot runs before the jump) and one word shorter per arm, which is why it survived 30 probe cases and seven exact functions. **With `-g` on the assembler the function is byte-exact**: 28/141 at 100 words without it, **141/141 at 144 words (141 + alignment padding) with it**, and `-O0`/`-O1` behave like no flag because `-O0` maps to 1. All nine assembler invocations in the corpus now pass `-EB -mips2 -mcpu=r4300 -g -G 0`, and the seven previously exact functions were re-measured under `-g` and are **unchanged** (`98`, `84`, `46`, `167`, `206`, `53`, and `0x8007e590` still 226/229) because none has a `load; return` arm for the swap to fire on; the `.mdebug` section `-g` asks for is already discarded by every link script. Coverage is **seven byte-exact of nine recovered** (46, 53, 84, 98, 141, 167, 206), both failures unchanged and both allocation residue (`0x80024558` 136/139, `0x8007e590` 226/229), `0x800701d8` in the probe as two **exact** pins (the 141-word function and its 44-word table), corpus **34 cases, all passing**, suite **72 suites / 1003 cases** with **723** needing the cartridge. **The lasting artifact is the diagnostic ladder**: when a function is short by exactly one word per arm the question is not which statement is wrong but *who emitted the missing word* -- the compiler, visible in the `.s`, or the assembler, visible only in the object; dumping the `.s` showed `lhu; j $31` was already right, and it took a milestone to think of because every previous failure in this project had been a compiler failure | `docs/log/M281-as-g-the-missing-assembler-flag.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py`, `docs/TOOLCHAIN.md` |

The 44 cases are written and measured: **28 of 141 words**, the **9-word head byte-exact** (`sltiu a1,44` bound, table `0x800b96c8`), and the **case 0/1 swap predicted from M278's source-order fact is confirmed** -- table order scores 27, swapping case 1 before case 0 gives 28 and puts `table[0] = 0x80070208` and `table[1] = 0x800701fc` on the image's own entries, because cc1 emits case bodies in source order and the image's source wrote case 1 first. Not registered in the probe: a partial pinned at 28 of 141 would assert a build fact that is really a toolchain gap, which is what M279's pin deliberately avoided. | M279 | **0x8007e590 enters the probe at 226 of 229, as a partial pin with a regional assertion** | Case 4's three words are parked and the function is registered anyway: `P7E590_SOURCE` plus two cases in `tools/compile_probe.py`, the acceptance pin **30 -> 32**, the corpus at **32 cases, all passing**, and a second `▓` on the progress map (**6 reproduced byte-for-byte, 2 recovered with the residue itemised**). The rule the corpus runs on is that a case asserts a fact about the **build**, not that a function is finished -- `p8` set that precedent at 136 of 139 -- and leaving this one out until it was exact has already cost something nameable: M275's 95/229 and M277's 176/229 were both measured by a harness in the gitignored `build/`, so neither could be re-derived by CI. **Three deliberate choices in the pins**: `same_offset >= 226` rather than `== 226`, because a pin must fail on a regression and **pass on an improvement** (p8's `>= 136` is the precedent) -- an equality pin would turn the next session's progress into a gate failure; `case3 == 113` is the assertion that matters, since a whole-function pin at 226 is satisfied by *any* arrangement of 226 identical words, including one where the 113-word arm loses ten and the head gains ten, so the arm is pinned exactly and the structure this session verified cannot decay into a different 226; and the jump table is its own case, 6 of 6 words at `0x800b9c78` with the sixth zero because the bound is 5, which is a claim about the linker script rather than the source and would fail independently. | M278 | **0x8007e590 at 226 of 229: the copy block, both table reads, and a switch with no `default`** | M277 left this function at **176 of 229** with 19 hunks; this pass took it to **226 of 229 by offset and by content, one real hunk** (the three words of case 4's select, a register tie-break) plus the `as` padding past the extent -- body 229, every region matching in size and nearly in content: head **14/14**, case 0 **21/21**, case 1 **30/30**, case 2 **5/5**, case 3 **113/113**, case 4 19/22, tail **24/24**. **The copy block** (17 words, duplicated in both `f8` arms) is now **17/17 twice**, and the lever was that **the source order is the reverse of the emitted order**: the image emits `f2c`, `f25e`, `f32`-pair, `f1a2++`, `f2a`-pair, `f36`, but writing *that* order scores 4/17 -- the source that reproduces it puts the counter first, then the `f2a` pair, then the `f32` pair, because the scheduler hoists `f32`'s load above `f2a`'s once the increment is out of the way; taking emitted order for source order is what kept 31 words wrong (194 -> 206), on top of **chained assignment** (`s->f38 = s->f1b6 = s->f32` emits one `lhu` feeding two `sh`s where two statements emit an extra `move` per pair). **The table read wants two `if`/`else` statements, not a ternary** (206 -> 220): with a ternary cc1 keeps the symbol address in one register and computes only the index conditionally, while the image materialises the base in each arm (`lui` + `lbu` + `addiu`, and in the else copy the `lui` even serves both arms from the `beqz` delay slot); and **the else copy is not a copy** -- its test uses the `c` local (`sltiu v0,a0,16`) while its index arms re-read `s->f8`, and it has no `s->f1e8.b[2] = 0` at all, so both differences had to be made together (220 -> **225**). **The last structural discovery is that the switch has no `default:`**: the dispatch's out-of-range `beqz` lands on the code after the switch (`0x8007e8c4`), case 3's exit lands there too, and case 1's three exits plus case 4's go to the epilogue -- so case 3 ends with `break` while case 1 and case 4 end with **`return;`**, and **in a `void` function those are the same behaviour and different code**: keeping `default:` inside sends case 3 to the epilogue (3 words), dropping it but leaving case 1's `break`s sends those three to the tail (3 words, 223), and only all-`return` is exact (226). Supporting fact, measured on a four-line fixture rather than guessed: **cc1 emits case bodies in source order, not jump-table order** (a `default` between `case 0` and `case 1` emits A, D, B, C), so the image's block layout *is* the source order of the arms. What remains is three words: the image reuses `v0` (which held `22`) for `5`/`4` and stores from it, mine uses the dead `a0`, and **fifteen variants did not move it** -- commuted operands, `0x16`, `4U`/`5U`, `(u8)` casts, `u8`/`u32` temporaries with and without braces, `(u32)` on the condition, a `u8` local holding `f5`, `+ 4` arithmetic, character literals, double parentheses, `if`/`else` assignment (194, two stores -- the value-select is confirmed) -- so it is the allocator's ordering alone, the same class as `0x80024558`'s three words, and M270's rule holds: a tie-break that survives fifteen shapes is not steerable from the source. Corpus unchanged at **30 cases, all passing**; `0x8007e590` is not in it, since 226 of 229 is not exact | `docs/log/M278-0x8007e590-226-of-229-transposition-and-exit-kind.md` |
| M277 | **0x8007e590 second pass: every region the right size, the default arm exact, and two errors that were reading rather than reconstruction** | M275 left this function at **95 of 229** words by offset; this pass took it to **176 of 229 by offset, 195 by content, 19 hunks**, with **every region now exactly the image's size** (case 3 113, case 4 22, default 20 + 4-word epilogue) and the object **3 words over, all of them the `as` alignment padding past the extent** -- so no statement is missing and none is invented, and what differs is where the allocator put things. Per region: head **14/14**, case 0 **21/21**, case 1 **30/30**, case 2 **5/5**, case 3 74/113, case 4 18/22, default **24/24**. **Two of the four fixes were reading errors, not reconstruction**: the device-window base must stay **detached from the symbol** -- `D_800C76B0 + 0x18000 + 0x1a7a` in one expression folds into a single `%hi`/`%lo` (two words), while the image materialises `0x18000` in a register and `addu`s it to the symbol (five words), which is gcc's `mips_add_large_offset_to_address` and is reached only when the base is already a **register**; `build/p7/` already held the idiom from the exact `0x80057e1c`, which is where the fix came from -- a matched function is the cheapest documentation of a shape rule in the repository. And the table is **`0x8010BA78`, not `0x8011BA78`**: `lui $v0,0x8011` + `addiu $v0,$v0,-17800` adds as printed to `0x8011BA78` but reassembles to `0x8010BA78`, because `%hi` rounds up when bit 15 of the low half is set -- **read a printed `lui` as `%hi`, never as the address's high half**. **Two discriminator mechanisms, both measured**: `beql` is **not a flag** -- this cc1 rejects `-mbranch-likely` and gcc 2.8.1 sets `mips_branch_likely` from `INSN_ANNULLED_BRANCH_P`, so the likely form appears when reorg puts a store in an *annulled* delay slot (the image's one `beql` carries `sb zero,5(s0)`), and the source shape that produces it is the **inverted** `if (!(A && B)) sel; else f5 = 0;` -- the natural form compiles to `bne` `+ j` and scores **10 of 22** against **18 of 22** for the inverted one, same program, four words apart; and case 4's test masks **bit 28**, not bit 12 -- `lui $v1,0x1000` + `and` is `0x10000000`, and `& 0x1000` compiles to a one-word `andi`, so **an `and` with a `lui`-built operand is a mask above bit 15, always**. **M275's duplicate-block claim was wrong**: the *field-copy* block is duplicated, but the table read before it is not -- the then-branch re-reads `s->f8` with `lbu` (20 words) and carries `s->f1e8.b[2] = 0` in the `beqz` slot, the else-branch uses the `a0` still holding `f8` from the outer test and has **no `b[2] = 0` at all** (17 words), so the source holds `f8` in a local for the outer test *and* the else branch while re-reading the field inside the then branch -- written that way the score went **95 -> 174 in one edit**, the largest gain of the pass, whereas the same hoist in M275's shape scored 86, which is a context fact and not a contradiction. The copy block needs **chained assignment** (`s->f38 = s->f1b6 = s->f32`) for one `lhu` to feed two `sh`s (two separate statements emit an extra `move` per pair, +2 words) plus **named temporaries** (`u16 p, q;`), which took 174 -> 176 and content 169 -> 195. **The 53 words that remain are all allocation or schedule, none structural**: 31 in the copy block twice (mine hoists the `f32`/`f2a` loads above an earlier store; the image issues the same 17 instructions in source order, with `a0`/`v1`/`a1` against my `v1`/`v1`/`a0`), 16 in the table read twice (base and index registers swapped, including the shared `lui` the image parks in the `beqz` delay slot), 3 in case 4's select (the image reuses `v0` for `22`/`5`/`4`, mine uses the dead `a0`, a pure tie-break that case 3's register pressure will likely decide). **Not claimed**: the function is not exact, it is not in the probe, and the progress map still does not list it -- corpus unchanged at **30 cases, all passing**, and M276's coverage reading stands: everything at or below 206 words has come out exact, and both failures are the two largest | `docs/log/M277-0x8007e590-regions-sized-and-two-found-by-wrong-address.md` |
| M276 | **0x800acae4: the streak held, and four shape facts a 53-word function can pin** | After M275's half-done 229-word function the next target was chosen deliberately small -- 53 words -- and came out **byte-exact: 53 of 53 by offset and by content**, jump table **7 of 7** at `0x800ba890`, with only the `as` alignment padding past the extent. It is a **clamp-then-dispatch predicate** and the first target whose dispatch is on an **offset domain** -- the index is `(cmd & 0xff) - 128`, so the cases are `0x80..0x85` and the table's 7th word is never read: the head clamps the third argument into `[0, 0x3fff]` before the switch, six arms are two words each, and one 18-word arm carries the default and the epilogue (head 25/25, arms 2/2 each, tail 16/16, measured separately before the total was believed). **Four shape facts pinned, each a discriminator**: a clamp written as a **ternary** reproduces `bltz … slti … beqzl` where an if/else-if inverts the first branch; a **truncation `(u8)x` is not a mask `x & 0xff`** -- cc1 reuses an already-live `li v0,255` and emits `and rd,rs,v0` instead of an `andi`, which is the most portable of the four; two separate `slti`s mean **nested ifs**, since `x >= A \|\| x < B` would fold into the `addiu`/`sltiu` range idiom; and a **switch phi landing in `a0` rather than `t0`** signals that an incoming parameter is still live to a later call -- here param0 is the call's own first argument, which is why the head parks it in `t1`. **Six of eight recovered functions are now exact** (46, 53, 84, 98, 167, 206 words), and the two failures are the two largest, failing differently: `0x80024558` is off by three words of allocation, `0x8007e590` is half-done at 95/229 because its 113-word arm's statement order is unsolved. **Everything at or below 206 words has come out exact**, which is now the honest predictor, with a mechanism: below roughly 200 words the data flow pins the allocation tightly enough that the compiler has no freedom left to disagree, and above it one wrong local width or statement order has room to move the whole body. Per region, measured at the image's own addresses: **head 14/14**, **case 0 21/21**, **case 2 5/5**, case 1 27/30, **case 3 21/113**, case 4 6/22, **default 1/24** -- so the dispatch, the first arm and the five-word arm are byte-exact and the difficulty is entirely in three named places, which a bare 95/229 could not say. **What is pinned and worth keeping**: the struct is solved, every offset taken from the image's own accesses and verified by a host offsetof check (`f0` 0, `f5`-`f8` at 5-8, `f2a` 0x2a, `f2c` 0x2c, `f32`-`f38` at 0x32-0x38, `f66` 0x66, `f1a2`, `f1b4`, `f1b6`, `f1e0` 0x1e0, the `f1e8` union at 0x1e8, `f214`, `f21c`, `f220`, `f22c`, `f230`, `f25e`, `f26f`, `f2b0` 0x2b0); **one access is not a field** -- the image reads a halfword at **0x2e inside the word at 0x2c**, so that read must be a cast, and declaring a field there shifts every later offset by two (an entire iteration was spent finding that); and **the control flow is resolved** -- `switch (s->f7)` bound 5, **case 0 falls through into case 1** and **case 2 into case 3**, case 3 duplicates an identical 47-word block in both arms of its `f8` test (so the source has those statements twice), and the default is a *real* 24-word block with a device-window test, a pointer comparison against a device register and a `FUN_80077f30(b, b * 3 + 4, s)` call whose multiplier is visible in the `sllv`/`addu` pair. **What is not pinned**: the shape of that duplicated block (the field copies `f1b6`/`f38` from `f32`, `f1b4`/`f34` from `f2a`, `f36` from the 0x2e halfword, the two `-= 1800` decrements and the `f1a2 + 1` counter are identified but not their order -- 92 of the 113 missing words are there), case 4's nested device test, and the default block's if structure. **Recorded rather than parked** on M254's precedent (which landed 27 of 98, said in those words rather than rounded up): the map is a durable artifact, the missing part is one arm's statement order plus two small blocks, the probe corpus is untouched at **28 cases**, and the progress map does not list this function. Two process notes: a subagent was cancelled mid-run and its replacement **died with an internal error at nine turns**, both before writing any C -- so on a target this size the failure mode to plan for is *the agent never starts*, not *the agent gets it wrong*, and a direct takeover with a per-region harness is the fallback that worked | `docs/log/M275-0x8007e590-an-honest-partial.md` |
| M274 | **the 206-word twelve-arm machine: the widest dispatch, byte-exact, and four single-word discriminators** | The next candidate tested a shape nothing else had -- **`0x80059530`**, a **twelve-arm** dispatch of **206 words**, the widest and largest yet -- and it turns out to be the state machine that runs *immediately before* `0x80059868` from M272 (this one's last word is `0x80059864`, the other begins at `0x80059868`), so the two share a `Ctx` layout and a device idiom and the recovery reused them. **Result: 206 of 206 by offset and by content** -- head 15/15, every arm exact (23, 20, 9, 18, 9, 20, 9, 32, 19, 6, 12, 9), the 5-word shared tail 5/5, **jump table 12/12 at `0x800b9010`** with `.rodata` exactly 48 bytes; the only difference is two zero words past the extent, the fifth instance of the `as` alignment-padding class. **And the extent was one word bigger than I said**: I computed 205 from the head plus the arm list, but `0x80059868 - 0x80059530 = 824 B = 206 words`, the missing word being the epilogue's `addiu sp,sp,32` -- the `jr ra` delay slot, which a `nop`-terminated arm list hides, and the list summed to 206 only because arm 11's size already contained the shared tail; **derive a word count from two addresses, not from a sum of parts**, since a sum cannot tell you it has double-counted a tail. **Four discriminators, each worth a single word**: a negative constant stored into a `u16` field must arrive **signed** (`s->f1e0 = -800` on a `u16` emits `ori rt,zero,0xfce0` and scores 205/206 with 2 hunks, while the image's `addiu rt,zero,-800` needs a signed RHS -- check the opcode, never the printed value); two structurally identical clamps need **opposite** comparisons (case 3 is `blez`, case 8 is `bgez`, eight cases apart -- a clamp's direction is a per-arm fact); a `* k` is visible in the **shamt** of its `sll` (`* 5` here against the neighbour's `* 3`); and **a call is a scheduling barrier**, so keeping a `jal` after a group of stores means the stores precede it *in the source*, while the arguments' `lui`/`ori` is the scheduler's job. The first is the sharpest: `-800` as a `u16` field assignment and as a signed store are the same value and different instructions, and only the `addiu`/`ori` bit tells them apart. **Coverage: five of six exact** (46, 84, 98, 167, 206 words; dispatch bounds 5, 6, 7, 7, 12 and 12), the single failure still the one whose residue is a register-allocation tie-break, so the method's remaining limit is unchanged and specific. **The map made the landing cheap**: one entry in `progress_map.py`'s table plus `--write` re-rendered the README block with a fifth bright cell, the gate passed, and the phase row moved to M251-M274 -- a milestone now costs one table line and a regeneration instead of a hand-edited chart. Corpus **28 cases, all passing**, pin `same_offset == 206` | `docs/log/M274-the-206-word-twelve-arm-machine.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py`, `tools/progress_map.py` |

Two panels in one fenced block wrapped in `progress-map` markers so it regenerates in place: **the cartridge**, one row per image and one cell per group of functions 58 wide, shaded by the **matching** question rather than "has a body" -- all 3 638 functions have had a body since the corpus was generated (M209's point), so a cell is bright only where a function was reproduced and **recompiled to the byte**, which makes the map's most useful property that it is honest about being mostly grey: **4 bright cells out of 3 638, all in `stage2`** (`0x8004fd1c` 98 words, `0x80057e1c` 84, `0x80070120` 46, `0x80059868` 167) plus one `▓` for `0x80024558` at 136 of 139; and **the record**, one bar per phase whose bars are read from **the README's own phase table**, so the picture cannot drift from the table beside it. **Why generated**: `tools/progress_map.py` reads `manifests/code_index.json` and holds one table of recovered functions whose *evidence* is the probe -- each entry names the `compile_probe.py` case that asserts it -- and `--check` fails both when the README's block differs from the fixtures **and when a claimed function is not in the corpus**, so the map cannot invent progress nor silently fall behind it; | M272 | **the 167-word state machine: byte-exact, and three shape levers that are discriminators** | The next dispatch-head candidate was chosen by a different rule -- not the smallest but the one that answers M271's open question, **does the `-mcpu=r4300` profile reproduce large functions as reliably as the 46-word one**: **`0x80059868`**, **167 words, double p7**, with a prologue, a `lbu 6(ctx)` index (p7's index source), bound 7, and seven arms whose sizes are wildly uneven (41 words against 6). **Result: 167 of 167 by offset and by content**, head 14/14, cases 23/41/32/19/6/12 and case 6 plus epilogue 20/20 all exact, **jump table 8/8 at `0x800b9040` with `.rodata` exactly 32 bytes** -- the table and nothing else, because **the function contains no pool literal at all** (every constant is `lui`/`ori` or an immediate), so the pool pathologies of p6 and p8 cannot arise in it: the residues cluster where the code shape gives the compiler a choice, not uniformly. The only difference is one `nop` past the image's extent, the fourth instance of the `as` 16-byte padding class (p6, p8, p9). **Three shape levers, each a measured discriminator**: a word field and the bytes inside it must be **one union member** (sibling `u8` fields score **136/167** with 25 hunks, because gcc then proves the byte reads disjoint from the word store and re-schedules the whole body); fields written `lhu`/`sh` are **`u16` with an explicit `(s16)` cast only in the predicates** (`s16` fields score 160/167, since the image pairs `lhu`/`addiu`/`sh` with `lh` for the test); a compound test is **nested `if`s not `&&`** (a flat chain scores 157/167 and loses 10 words in that arm, because the pointer assignment *between* the tests is what stops the symbol address being kept live); and the device pointer needs a **local** (the direct cast scores 40/167). The union lever is the same *kind* of discriminator as p9's variable width and p7's struct-vs-pointer rule -- **an access-kind fact, not a statement-order fact** -- and it is the first case where the fix is about *struct decomposition*. M271's test also generalises: no `default:` and no trailing `return`, the out-of-range path falling to the epilogue on the `sltiu`. **The method's coverage, which was the actual question**: 46 exact, 84 exact, 98 exact, **167 exact**, and the one failure (139 words) is the *only* one whose residue is a register-allocation tie-break rather than an access-kind fact -- so **the profile scales, and what does not scale is a reconstruction that leaves the allocator free to choose**. Corpus **26 cases, all passing**, pin `same_offset == 167` | `docs/log/M272-the-167-word-state-machine-byte-exact.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M271 | **p9 recovered byte-exact on the second attempt, and the shape facts that made it so** | The next function from the dispatch-head queue is **p9 at `0x80070120`** -- the fourth of M253's heads and the only one never recovered as a whole function (signature-compared only), and the smallest at **46 words**. It is a **predicate dispatcher**, not a state machine: advance a cursor by 6, read a command word, call `FUN_800701d8(ctx->f154, command >> 8)`, then switch on the low byte to `==`, `>`, `>=`, `<`, `<=`, `!=` and `(x & v) != 0` on the callee's return value. **Result: 46 of 46 by offset, 46 of 46 content-aligned** -- head 23/23, cases 0-6 18/18, epilogue 5/5 -- and the **jump table 8/8 at `0x800b96a8`**, with the single "hunk" being two zero words **past the image's extent** (`0x800701d8` is the next function): binutils 2.7's `as` pads `.text` to 16-byte alignment and `--set-section-alignment .text=4` rewrites `sh_addralign` without shrinking the section, the same residue class as p6's and p8's padding. **Independently re-measured after the recovery, not taken on report.** **Two shape facts are measured, and one is provable**: (a) **variable width, not statement order** -- `w` must be held in a **32-bit** local, because a `u16` local makes gcc keep an HImode pseudo and re-emit `andi $s0,0xffff` before the `srl` and the `sll`/`sra` re-sign-extension in all seven arms (**9 of 46**), and every other shape lever this arc has tried was about ordering; (b) **the source has no `default:` and no trailing `return`** -- the out-of-range path lands on the epilogue with `v0` still 0 from the bound's `sltiu`, so adding either costs **16 words** (30 of 46), which gives a cheap general test: *does the image contain the `li v0,0`?*; and (c) **the tail arm falls into the epilogue** rather than jumping, which is why the last arm is 2 words and the others 3. p9 joins the probe as a fourth whole-function case plus a table case, so the corpus is **24 cases, all passing**, with pins asserting exactness (`same_offset == 46`) rather than a threshold. Coverage is now **`0x8004fd1c` exact (98), `0x80057e1c` exact (84), `0x80070120` exact (46), `0x80024558` 136 of 139** -- **three of four byte-exact, and the failures cluster in one place**: the 139-word function whose residue is allocation (M270), while the 46-word one came out exact on the second attempt with the first already at 30 of 46. That is the method working as designed: **small functions with unambiguous data flow come out exact, and the cost is reconstruction time rather than compiler archaeology** -- the archaeology (M257-M265) was paid once and now applies to every function. README's *The matching build* table and the P16 phase row updated to four recovered and three exact, and the case counts moved with the two new gates | `docs/log/M271-p9-byte-exact-predicate-dispatcher.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M270 | **case 3/4 in the image's statement order: measured, and the current expression is the best** | M269's hypothesis -- the pool's order `[2^32][4.0f]` against ours reveals the original's *statement order*, the conversion written before the multiply -- is tested in four shapes, all semantically identical: **the current single expression scores 136/139, and every restructure is far worse** -- two locals with C89 declarations first **72/139** (19 hunks), two stores `w1 = ...; w1 \|= ...` **74/139** and the **arm addresses move by 4 bytes** (`0x80024774` against `0x80024770`, i.e. the code grew), operands swapped inside the `\|` **78/139**, and M269's hoisted `4.0f` 115/139. A fifth failed to compile at all: declarations after statements, because **cc1 is a C89 compiler** (`u32 a = ...` below `Gfx *_g = p;` gives `parse error before (null)`, the mojibake M257 documented as pointing at an earlier undefined thing). **Why reshaping costs so much**: p8 is 139 words, keeps its argument in `s1`, the save area in `s0` and runs a four-register packet cursor through `t6..t9`, so changing *where* a value is computed changes which pseudo gets `t6` against `t7` and that propagates through every packet address -- one register decision, 14 words apart in the diff (`move a2,t7; addiu t7,t7,8` where the image has `t6`). That is the same effect M262 measured from the other direction: **in a function this size only a rewrite that leaves allocation untouched can improve the score, and every restructure expressible does not**. So the pool's order is a *consequence* of the source and the shapes that would produce the image's order cost far more than they gain: **p8's three words are not reachable from the C** -- `$at` against `$v0` is allocation (`-mno-split-addresses` costs 95 words on p6), the pool entry `0x69c8` against `0x69d8` is the original's one-entry-per-use (M268) whose reproduction costs 21 words of layout (M269), and the third is `.text` alignment padding. **p8 is functionally identical and byte-different in 3 of 139 words**, both causes compiler-side, both measured, each more expensive than the three words it would win -- an honest stopping point for this function, not a claim that it is impossible. **Five hypotheses have now been tested and refuted for p8's residue** (flag, pool placement, dedup, hoist, source order), and the pattern across them is consistent: the residue is allocation, and allocation in a 139-word function is not steerable from the source. The productive direction is therefore M264's unfinished one -- another function of this size whose C *is* fully recoverable, to test whether the `-mcpu=r4300` profile reproduces big functions as reliably as it did p7, a question about the method's coverage rather than about p8's last three words. No tool change; **p6 exact, p7 exact, p8 136/139**, corpus 22 cases | `docs/log/M270-case34-statement-order-measured-and-refuted.md` |
| M269 | **the hoist and the no-dedup, tried together, and the docs brought up to date** | M268 established the original's pool is **per use** and that no-dedup is the right model, so both changes were applied -- no dedup on `immed_double_const`'s chain search and `force_const_mem`'s descriptor hash for `SFmode`/`DFmode`, and `float k = 4.0f;` hoisted into case 3/4 -- and p8 got **worse: 115/139 offset (134 content, 6 hunks) against 136/139**, for **two separate causes visible in the diff**: the **pool's order flipped** to `[table][4.0f][2^32]` where the image is `[table][2^32][4.0f]`, because slots are emitted in *use* order and the hoisted local uses the float first; and **the hoist made the load unconditional** -- the image loads `4.0f` **after** the `bgez`, inside the fall-through path, while a `k` declared at the top of the block made gcc emit `lui %hi` + `lwc1` **before** the branch, three extra words. So the image's single `4.0f` slot comes from **CSE, not from a hoisted local**: the original wrote the multiplication twice and gcc loaded the constant once, where the image loads it -- which our original source already did. Both changes **reverted**, p8 back at 136/139, toolchain back to the M265 patch set; the lesson is M264's and M265's again -- with a coupled pair, measure after each half, and here the *second* half broke a layout property the first was supposed to fix. **The docs were larger than "outdated":** the README phase table ran **P11 \| M192-M199** and stopped while the log is at M268 -- 69 milestones with no row in the table a reader sees first -- so it gains **P12** (pool record classes, two published corpus figures corrected), **P13** (the VM's opcode registry, an evidence-carrying call graph, verification tiers, `TYRANT` as codename, the three work packages that closed the sound family), **P14** (the RSP microcode, the vector unit 252 of 254, the dispatch-head queue), **P15** (**the toolchain identified**: binutils 2.7 and IDO built natively, IDO refuted, gcc 2.7.2 then gcc 2.8.1 reproducing every probe) and **P16** (**whole functions reproduced**: three recovered, two byte-exact, and the build facts behind them), and p8's row in *The matching build* was corrected to M268's finding -- the pool entry is one the original keeps **per use** where ours deduplicates, not "a duplicate the original emits", which was my misreading. **`docs/TOOLCHAIN.md` was worse**: its section 2 was headed *"The compiler ... still not runnable here"* and section 4 *"The compile probe, ready to run"*, both written before either was true, so the heading now says **runnable and proven**, section 4 is rewritten from plan to result, and it records a real disagreement with the disc -- the kit's makefile says `CFLAGS = -c -O -G 0` but **`-O2` is what reproduces the image** (`-O` misses by 10 words on the two byte-exact functions), so either that makefile is the libraries' contract rather than the game's or the game was built with different flags; the SDK's own text is evidence but not a measurement of the game's build. **p6 exact, p7 exact, p8 136/139**, corpus 22 cases; next for p8 is not "make the pool match" but the source's **statement order** -- the pool's order follows use order, so the image's `[2^32][4.0f]` means its source wrote the conversion before the multiply | `docs/log/M269-the-hoist-and-the-no-dedup-and-docs-brought-up-to-date.md`, `README.md`, `docs/TOOLCHAIN.md` |
| M268 | **the mixed-constant test: duplication is uniform, and "the image dedups floats" was wrong** | M267 proposed a discriminating test -- a function that uses one double twice and one float twice, to see whether the double is duplicated while the float is shared. It comes back **negative**, and why withdraws two of M267's claims. **The scan**: 387 float pool loads resolved (`lui R,HI` then `lwc1`/`ldc1` on base `R`, restricted to the `.rdata` window `0x800b0000..0x800c0000` -- the unrestricted version picked up `lwc1` from stale registers pointing into RAM and produced two false "mixed" functions). **No function contains both behaviours**, and the aggregate is unambiguous: of 86 distinct **float** values **38** appear at 2+ pool addresses, of 20 **doubles** **10** do, and **zero** constants of either size are **shared by two loads from one address**. The duplicated floats are not marginal -- `4f000000` (2^31 f) at **52** addresses, `3f800000` (1.0 f) at **41**, `45800000` at **13**, `40800000` (4.0 f) at **12**; the double `41f0000000000000` (2^32) at **60**. Functions duplicate every constant they use: `FUN_80070708` has three doubles each at 2 addresses, `FUN_8009ef50` four at 3. **Two claims withdrawn**: (a) *"the image does dedup `4.0f` -- both uses in p8 share one slot"* is **wrong** -- p8's image pool is `[2^32][4.0f][2^32]`, the float appearing once because p8 **loads it once** and keeps it in `$f4` for both multiplies, not because the pool was deduplicated, i.e. I inferred a pool property from a register-allocation outcome; (b) *"the original's behaviour is mode-asymmetric"* is **not supported** -- duplication is uniform across both sizes, so there is one rule, not two. The corrected statement is simpler and stronger: **the original never shares a pool entry between two loads -- the pool is per use, for floats and doubles alike** -- and the test as designed could never have discriminated, because "shared by two loads" occurs **nowhere** in 387 resolved sites: the only way a constant is reused is by keeping it in a register. That also re-reads M267's no-dedup result: since no-dedup is the *right* model, the **46-word loss (p8 136 to 90) was not a semantic error but a layout consequence** -- a bigger pool moves which entries sit at which offsets and hence every `%lo` immediate -- possibly compounded by our source loading `4.0f` twice where the original loads it once; not re-run here, because it needs the source and the compiler changed together, the kind of coupled change that produced M264's and M265's false starts when attempted one at a time. **p6 exact (98/98), p7 exact (84/84), p8 136/139**, corpus 22 cases, no tool change; **this is the third self-correction in the arc** (M261 a pool reading, M264 a pool word, this one a dedup inference), each caught by a one-scan measurement and recorded rather than quietly replaced. Next, precisely: make p8's source load `4.0f` **once** (hoist it into a local) and, *with* dedup off, re-measure -- if the pool layout then matches, only the `$at` tie-break and the padding remain | `docs/log/M268-the-mixed-constant-test-and-two-withdrawals.md` |
| M267 | **the duplicate pool entry is systematic, and removing dedup is not how to match it** | M266 left one question: p8's pool holds the same 2^32 double twice, ours dedups to one, and the image also dedups its single-precision `4.0f`. **The third-function test says the duplication is systematic**: a scan of the image for `lui R,HI` followed by an `lwc1`/`ldc1` on base `R` resolves **430 float pool loads**, and grouping them by (function, value) with two or more distinct addresses gives `FUN_800a3094` loading **2^32 from seven slots** (`0x800ba630, 638, 640, 648, 650, 658, 660`), `FUN_800a5da0` from six, `FUN_8009ef50` from five, three functions loading the float `4f000000` from nine, nine and eight addresses, and p8's own pool region holding 2^32 at `0x800b69c8/69d8/69f8/6a08` -- so p8's two copies are an ordinary instance and **the duplicate should be reproduced** if byte-exactness is the goal. **Applying it wholesale is wrong, and measurably so**: removing the dedup on both paths that do it (`immed_double_const`'s `const_double_chain` search and `force_const_mem`'s descriptor hash) gives p6 **98/98** and p7 **84/84** unchanged but p8 **90/139 against 136/139**, 11 hunks against 3, and the jump table no longer findable in `.rodata` -- worse by **46 words**, because the image *does* dedup `4.0f` (both uses in p8 share one slot) and a uniform no-dedup floods the pool with a fresh `4.0f` per use and shifts every `%lo` immediate after it, the same coupling that made "pool everything" alone worse in M264. So **the original's behaviour is mode-asymmetric** -- one shared entry for the single-precision constant, several for the double -- and since `force_const_mem` has a single hash and a single comparison, no uniform setting produces that, which means the model of *why* the original duplicated is still wrong and is not "the comparison was broken" (that would duplicate the float too). The experiment was **reverted and recorded rather than pinned**: p8 stays at 136/139, its three words being the `$at`/`$v0` allocation tie-break (unaffected by the pool change, which rules out "the pool grew and the allocator moved"), the duplicated entry, and one padding word. **README upgraded**: the headline *"This is not a matching build"* is true of the whole cartridge but was incomplete, so it now says so and gains a **The matching build** section with the identified profile (gcc 2.8.1 / binutils 2.7 / `-mcpu=r4300` on both / the two patched behaviours) and a table of the three reconstructed functions with their results -- prose only, no new gated number, `counts.py` green at 25/25. Next test, now well specified: a function that uses **one double twice and one float twice** -- if the double is duplicated and the float is not, the asymmetry is a property of the original compiler's comparison and can be characterised exactly | `docs/log/M267-the-duplicate-pool-entry-is-systematic.md`, `README.md` |
| M266 | **`-msplit-addresses` is refuted, and case 3/4 is an unsigned conversion** | Two asks. **(1)** `-msplit-addresses` is **already on**: `mips.c:3703` sets it from `TARGET_GAS && TARGET_SPLIT_ADDRESSES && optimize && !flag_pic && Pmode==SImode`, and the emitted forms prove it (`lui $2,%hi($LC0)` + `l.s %lo($LC0)($2)`, and `lui $2,%hi(g)` for globals). `-mno-split-addresses` *does* give the image's `$at` form -- by handing the bare symbol to the assembler -- but it changes every address materialisation and costs **95 words on p6 (98 to 3) and 77 on p7 (84 to 7)**, while p3's own image (`lui v0,0x8013; lw v0,0x14cc(v0)`) proves the image's globals went through gcc-allocated registers, so the image's build had split addresses on too: **the `$at` is not a flag**, it is one allocated register inside the same split form (same `lui` immediate, same `lwc1` offset), i.e. an allocation tie-break. **(2)** Case 3/4 decoded: `mtc1/cvt.d.w` then `bgez` guarding an `ldc1` of **4294967296.0** and an `add.d` is gcc's **`floatunsidf`** expansion, so the source converts an **unsigned** value -- and M264's reading of that pool word as a float 30.0 was **wrong**: `41f00000 00000000` is the double 2^32, which is why the pool word sits next to no 30.0 anywhere. Our source already types those globals `u32`, so the instruction sequence matches and the arithmetic was never the problem. p8's last three words are therefore: the **allocation tie-break** (`$at` against `$v0`), **a duplicated pool entry** -- the image's `.rdata` for p8 is `[table 6][41f00000 0][40800000 0][41f00000 0]`, **two copies of the same 2^32 double**, and the second conversion loads the second copy while ours dedups to one -- and **one word of trailing `.text` padding**. The duplicate is **not a blanket difference**: a two-call function compiled here pools 2^32 and 4.0f once each, and the image dedups its *single-precision* constant across both uses, so the image collapses one class and duplicates another, and `force_const_mem` dedups by descriptor hash plus `CONST_DOUBLE_MEM`, meaning the two `CONST_DOUBLE`s reaching it were not equal by its own test although their 8 bytes are -- the same family of failure M261 fixed on our side, and reproducing it would mean **deliberately re-breaking a comparison our build now gets right**. **Deliberately not done**: p8 is three words from exact and **functionally identical**, and unlike the `-mcpu` and pool-placement findings there is no independent evidence of what the original defect was, only that its pool did not collapse an identical double; the honest next step is a third function that uses the same double twice -- if the image duplicates it there too the defect is systematic and worth characterising, if not the duplicate comes from this function's own expression tree. | M265 | **both patches together: the pool lands where the image's does, and p8's residue changes kind** | M264 predicted that pooling every float constant *alone* makes p8 worse and needs the pool emitted after the function body too; both are now applied and the prediction held in the way that matters -- p8 still reports 136/139, but **the three remaining words are a different question**. | M261 | **the FP pool bug is a 64-bit-host bug, and fixing it changed codegen, not just data** | M260 found two defects in one 8-word window; this fixes the one that gates FP. `varasm.c`'s `immed_real_const_1` stores a real into a CONST_DOUBLE -- two 32-bit words that every reader decodes as the **target** bit pattern -- guarded by `if (sizeof u == 2 * sizeof (HOST_WIDE_INT))`: **8 == 8 on the 32-bit host the code was written for, 8 == 16 here**, so SFmode and DFmode fell through to the path for reals *wider* than two words, where a raw `bcopy` drops the emulator's packed internal form into the rtx and every reader then decodes it as a pattern. Measured: `return 30.0f` gave `.word 0x00000000`, `1.0f/3.0f` gave a denormal, while `static float arr[1] = {30.0f}` gave `0x41f00000` -- the tree->`assemble_real` path was always fine, so the emulator and parser were never the problem, only the rtx round-trip. **Three coordinated edits** in `tools/toolchain/patch_gcc281_fp.py`, re-applied by `provision.sh` and idempotent per file: the writer builds the pattern with `REAL_VALUE_TO_TARGET_SINGLE`/`_DOUBLE` and returns through `immed_double_const`, `REAL_VALUE_FROM_CONST_DOUBLE` becomes its exact inverse via `ereal_from_float`/`ereal_from_double`, and the pool reads through that accessor. Knock-on: with no pool needed, our p8 `.rodata` starts with the jump table, which now lands at the image's own `0x800b69b0` instead of 16 bytes late. **The `30.0f` retry changes nothing and that is the expected result**: `4.0f` and `30.0f` both score **118/139 offset, 128 content, 12 hunks**, because a float constant comes from a pool or an immediate either way, so its value cannot move an instruction; the image's `41f00000` still proves the original source's constant, so the source should say `30.0f` even though no code moves. **What the image does and we do not**: it loads `lwc1 $f4,27088(at)` from a **pool** where our fixed compiler emits `li.s`, and every flag tried (`-mips2`, `-mips3`, `-mabi=eabi`, `-mabi=o64`, `-msoft-float`, `-mno-abicalls`) keeps `li.s` -- so whatever made the image's build take the pool path is still unexplained, and is now a precise question. File exists`), so the base "could not be compiled" and the run died before iteration 1 -- `/tmp/perm.XXXXXX.s` plus a `trap` is the fix -- and the M256 `compile.sh` convention (`<in.c> -o <out.o>`, the output named after a flag) still applies. **What 1 780 buys is nothing in the matching metric**: the best two candidates compiled through the real pipeline and scored the way the probe scores p6 (linked `.text` at `0x8004fd1c`, `.rodata` at the table) give **28 of 98 offset-identical, 82 of 98 content-aligned, 12 hunks -- identical to the base in every column**, and the gap between those two numbers is p6's first differing word (`0x8004fd20`, the head's `beq` offset) shifting the other 96.
