| M258 | **the rule that sinks that store is not in mips.c -- it is a dependence gcc drops** | M257 left p7's case 0 at 0 of 14 words with "my store sinks below a load the image keeps in order"; the instruction was to start at `mips.c`, and the first finding is negative: **`mips.c` has no scheduler hook at all** -- a grep for `adjust_cost`, `issue_rate`, `reorder`, `SCHED` returns only `.set noreorder` emission. The port's whole scheduling contribution is three lines of `config/mips/mips.md`: a **store's READY-DELAY is 1** (line 157) and a **load's is 2 on R4300** (line 152, three on the "worst case" processors). That number reaches the generic scheduler through `sched.c`'s `priority()` (line 1453), `prev_priority = priority (x) + insn_cost (x, prev, insn) - 1`, whose comment states the intent: *"When all instructions have a latency of 1, there is no need to move any instructions. Subtracting one here ensures that... no scheduling will be done."* **A store can never be given a priority of its own -- the `- 1` cancels its latency-1 unit** -- while the `lw` that starts the RMW chain feeds a priority of 2 into everything that depends on it, so the list scheduler issues the load first and the store drifts behind it. The *legality* is the other half: `true_dependence`/`anti_dependence`/`output_dependence` (`sched.c:830/862/890`, one shared body) suppress a conflict when **one side is a struct-member access whose address varies and the other is not a struct access** -- a pre-`-fstrict-aliasing` type guess, and 2.8.1 has no `-fstrict-aliasing` at all (M255). Tested three ways: mixed pair (struct store x plain `*(u32*)` device load) **loses** the dependence and the load is hoisted; struct x struct and plain x plain **keep** it (`sw ... sw ... lw`). The struct form reproduces the image's arm **instruction for instruction, 14 of 14 words**, differing only in register names (`a0`/`a1` against the image's `s0`/`a0`, which follows from the rest of the function being right). So the original reached the device window as a **struct member**: declaring `struct Dev { u8 pad[0x1a5c]; u32 reg; u32 reg2; }` and writing `((struct Dev *)(dev + 0x18000))->reg` takes **p7 from 60 to 74 of 84 words by offset, 75 by content, 6 hunks to 2, with case 0 byte-exact and the table still 8/8**. Two corollaries: `-mcpu=r4300`/`r4600` produce the **identical** schedule (the default is already a 3-cycle load, and the direction does not change), and `-fno-schedule-insns2` is **byte-identical** so sched2 is not the pass, while `-fno-schedule-insns` changes the order but is a wash on both whole functions (p7 60 to 59, p6 82 unchanged) -- so the image **was** scheduled and the difference was never a flag. While those compiles were set up the roadmap's open **`-O` vs `-O2` sweep closed**: the generic probes could not separate them (M250, both 4/4) but the whole functions do, `-O2` winning on both (p6 82 against 72 content, p7 64 against 61 in 6 hunks against 10), confirming the profile already in the tool and giving the sweep the probe it lacked. Corpus **18 cases, all passing**, pin moved to 18. **The lesson to promote: when a store and a load trade places, ask whether gcc believes they can alias -- a source-shape fact about how each access is *typed* -- before asking about the scheduler's intent** | `docs/log/M258-the-rule-that-sinks-that-store.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M257 | **p7 recovered: 60 of 84 words, a table that matches 8 of 8, and the toolchain fact that blocks stage 5** | M256 named the next function -- **p7 `0x80057e1c`**, 336 B, bound **7**, index **the byte at `ctx+6`**, table `0x800b8f78` -- and it is a different *shape* from p6 in the one way that matters: **all seven arms sit inside one contiguous range** (p6's five were six separate corpus functions) and **two fall through** (`0` into `1`, `4` into `5`), so the table's entries land mid-arm. The function is a state machine on `ctx->mode`: `0` writes `f1e8 = 0x06000700`, sets `mode = 1`, zeroes `f1e0` and ORs `0xc0` into a device register; `1` and `5` do `mode += FUN_80089d54(ctx,a1,tbl,{0x200,0x10200})`; `2` writes two globals and copies a device register into `D_80131780+0x44`; `3` is empty; `4` mirrors `0` into `mode = 5`; `6` calls `FUN_800551e0`, writes `0x01000000` over `f4`/`mode` and clears two device bits. **The prototype is forced by the callee, not guessed**: `0x80089d54` does `lbu t1,488(a0); move a1,a2; jal 0x80089dc8` -- it forwards `a1`/`a2` and reads only `a0` -- so `FUN_80057e1c` takes `(ctx, a1, tbl)` and passes them through, `a3` a flag word; that is the first prototype here derived from a **callee's** argument use. **Result: head+dispatch 14/14, case 1 tail 3/3, cases 4+5 14/14, case 6 15/15, epilogue 4/4, jump table 8/8**, and the weak arms are `0` (0/14) and `2` (10/20). Both residues are **instruction permutations and the mnemonic multiset is equal on each side**: case 0 builds the `0x18000` register first and sinks the `0x06000700` store below a `lw`, where the image stores first; case 2 hoists `lw 6752` above `sw 6748`. Reported as **two numbers** because they answer two questions -- **60 of 84 by offset** (the matching question) and **64 of 84 aligned by content, 6 hunks** (the reconstruction question). The one lever that mattered is a **source-shape fingerprint**: `0x18000+0x1a5c` compiles to `lui 0x1 / ori 0x8000 / addu`, which is `LEGITIMIZE_ADDRESS` splitting a `PLUS(REG, 0x19a5c)` at `& ~0x7fff` and which **only fires when the base is a pointer pseudo from an `extern` symbol** -- a literal or a folded `sym + 0x18000` becomes `lui 0x800e` -- so declaring `dev` inside each case block instead of at function scope takes **49/84 to 60/84**. **Three instrument facts, one of them a correction**: (1) **`cc1` cannot parse any comment** -- `int f(int a){/* c */ return a;}` gives `parse error before `(null)'` -- and there is **no `cpp` in that prefix**, only `cc1`, which is the preprocessed-input backend; that is not a curiosity but the thing that **blocks stage 5**, whose real sources are `#include`-bearing, so either 2.8.1's `cccp` gets built or the host preprocesses first (and a **missing typedef** produces the same mojibake error, so that message almost always means "undefined type earlier"); (2) the handed-over claim that `objdump` needs `-EB` is **only true for a raw `-b binary` slice**, where no header states the endianness -- on a real ELF object it honours the object's own, measured both ways on `0x27bdffe8` -- which is the same trap that made M256's first comparison wrong; (3) before linking a binutils-2.7 `as` object, `objcopy --set-section-alignment .text=4 --set-section-alignment .rodata=8`, or `.text` keeps 16-byte alignment and **shifts the whole function by 4 bytes**, making every word differ for a reason unrelated to the C. Corpus **17 cases, all passing**, pin moved to 17; two functions are now recovered and re-compiled, **neither byte-exact**, and the open item on both is one class of problem -- which store/load/hazard position gcc picks -- which a permuter will not answer (M256) and reading gcc 2.8.1's `mips.c` scheduling against the image will | `docs/log/M257-p7-recovered-and-the-toolchain-fact-that-blocks-stage-5.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M256 | **the permuter plateaued at 1 900; the fix it could not find was one operand order** | M255 said the residue in p6's body is expression work -- load order and `mult` operand order -- so this runs **decomp-permuter** on it. Setup cost three fixes, none of them the search: a venv (`toml`, `pycparser`), binutils on `PATH` for the driver's `mips-linux-gnu-objdump`, and two silent ones -- the driver calls `compile.sh <in.c> -o <out.o>` (four arguments, the output *named after a flag*, so the first version wrote the object to a file called `-o`), and `objcopy` has no `-o`, so the pipeline is `cc1 -O2 -G 0 -mips2` and `as 2.7` and nothing else. Base scored **2 000** against the 392-byte target, assembled from the image with real `%hi/%lo` relocations so the unlinked comparison is legitimate. **110 242 iterations, two score events, best 1 900**, and the best candidate's entire diff is an `inline int inline_fn(int a){return a;}` wrapper inserted into one arm -- an identity function that changed register allocation incidentally and nothing else. That is the signature of a search whose neighbourhood does not contain the answer: the permuter mutates expressions and statements, and the thing that was wrong is the *order of operands inside one expression*, with 690 iterations in 1 000 failing to compile at all (its mutations are tuned for IDO-era sources, this target is gcc 2.8.1). **Verdict: not converged, said as that.** The answer was in the disassembly, not the search: the image's `mult` takes **b3 first** (`lbu a0,3(a3); lbu v1,2(a3); mult a0,v1` in arm 1, `lbu v1,3(a3); lbu a0,2(a3); mult v1,a0` in arm 3), mine took `b2` first because I wrote `s->b2 * s->b3`; swapping to `s->b3 * s->b2` in the three comparisons is the whole edit and takes the corpus score from **81 to 82 of 98 words, 14 hunks to 12** -- which also settles *which* order the original wrote, since gcc 2.8.1 evaluates the right operand of `*` first at `-O2`. **M255's own instrument was also wrong and is corrected here**: it disassembled the image slice as a raw binary (`objdump -b binary -m mips:4300` has no mapping, so `beq` printed as `bgtzl`) against my object as ELF (whose columns carry relocations); assembling the image's words as `.word` into a real ELF and disassembling both sides the same way gives **96 of 98 instructions mnemonic-identical with 2 extra**, and every remaining hunk is one of three classes: `s->b1`'s load **hoisted before the `mult`** in the image (arms 1-3, a scheduler tie-break -- the load is independent of the product either way), one **hazard `nop`** in a different position relative to the same `mflo`/`addiu`, and **branch offsets**, which are the sizes of the other two. So M255's 14 hunks are really one expression-level fix plus one scheduling habit, overstated in words because each shifted instruction re-aligns the comparison. Corpus **15 cases, all passing**, p6 pin raised to the measured (`same >= 82`, `extra <= 6`). Unexplained: why gcc hoists that `lbu` in the image and not in mine -- one scheduling decision in three arms, not a semantic difference | `docs/log/M256-the-permuter-plateau-and-the-operand-that-moved.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |
| M255 | **p6 arms 2-4: the order was already right, and the region comparison was not** | The instruction was to make arms 2-4 divide before incrementing and load before storing; **both are already what my compiled arms do**, and what was wrong was the *comparison* -- slicing both sides at the image's region addresses compares misaligned code the moment any region differs in size, which is why arms 2-4 read as 0/21, 0/23, 0/21 words. Aligned by content (`SequenceMatcher`): image **98** instructions, compiled **104** (+6), **81 identical**, **14 hunks**, first difference at `0x8004fd20` (the head's `beq` offset). Confirmed directly -- my arms 2 and 3 begin `lbu v0,a3,1; lbu v1,a3,2; divu v0,v1; ...; sh v0,a1,0; lbu v0,a3,1; addiu v0,v0,1; sb v0,a3,1`, i.e. **divide first**, store, then **reload** the counter and increment, instruction-for-instruction the image. The reload across the store happens on its own: marking the pointer `volatile` made the function **worse** (22% against 27%), as did `volatile short *p` (11%). The 14 hunks are three small kinds, none of them sequencing: **load order** (the image loads `s[3],s[2],s[1]` where mine loads `s[1],s[2],s[3]`, in arm 1's second half), the **`mult` operand order** that follows from it, and **one `nop` where the image fills the slot** (`beq; addiu` against `beq; nop; lbu; addiu`). So the remaining work is *expression* work -- which order the loads are written in and which operand goes first in each `mult` -- which is a search problem, not a reading problem: 14 hunks with 81 instructions already identical is what a permuter is for. The M254 delay-slot claim survives: with the arms in place the head differs only in the `beq` offset, i.e. the arms' total size. No `-O`/`-O2` difference; `-fno-strict-aliasing` is not accepted by gcc 2.8.1's cc1 | `docs/log/M255-p6-arms-the-order-was-already-right.md`, `tools/compile_probe.py` |

| M79 | **interrupts work end to end, and the boundary is *work*, not signalling**: the guest's own handler was disassembled rather than assumed — `lui k0,0x8000; addiu k0,0x6d0; jr k0` enters a handler at `0x800006d0` that keeps a fixed exception work area at `0x80015000`, saves **64-bit** registers with `sd`, stores Status at `+0x118` (the same slot `__osCurrentThread` uses) and masks IE/EXL before returning. Driven by the device model alone, **three PI interrupts dispatch into three distinct threads**, each with the mask `0x0999`, the pending bit `0x0010` and `validCount 0` recorded at the moment of dispatch — completion → interrupt → dispatch with no injected line. And still **nothing posts**: the run queue stays empty, the idler wins, both application queues keep `mtqueue` pointed at a waiting thread with `validCount = 0`, because there is no *work* — an RSP task producing output, a DMA whose payload a thread then processes — and no amount of device signalling manufactures it. The reconstruction's boundary is therefore established rather than assumed | `docs/log/M79-interrupt-scope.md` |

Separately, `H = 3.59` bits/byte caps any lossless expansion at `8/H ≈ 2.2×`, so bank C's bytes are **not** compressed output — the 6.8 ratio is `span/stored` (destination range over input size) and nothing uses `span` as a length; bank B's spans *are* verified decompressed sizes (M21, 6/6) while bank C's are unverified. `cart_record` iteration for records 39–66 is closed (7 jal sites: indices 1, 67 and five loaded), and content equality between the banks stays unresolved — a 311 KB swing in asset coverage | `docs/log/M91-bank-c-map-is-read.md` |

Slots **17–31** are not a harmless header read: `cart_record(0)` yields `record[1] = 0x4c415954 ≠ 0`, so branch 2 fires and the loader inflates **1.28 GB from cart `0x0f564552`**, four times past the cartridge end — a crash-class path distinct from slot 16's silent failure. The watch at `0x80071ebc` (`lbu a0,3(v0)`) must cover **`[16,32)`**. The stamp record survives **not** because `record[1] == 0` but because nothing passes it to the loader — boot reads field 0 as a pointer itself; handed to `FUN_800327ec` it would inflate 3.4 GB. And the dev-build hypothesis is weak: the two build stamps are 2 days apart in late September 1999 (RE2 N64 shipped October 1999), and `0xCDCDCDCD` occurs **once** in 64 MB, so the audit's own multi-occurrence test fails | `docs/log/M93-slot-condition-and-build-question.md` |
| M94 | **the callers of `FUN_8003287c` enumerated**: exactly **one** `jal` (`0x80071ec0` in `FUN_80071e14`), no `lui`+`jalr` construction of its address, no occurrence as a word, and no mid-function entry — four independent forms checked. **No caller passes a literal slot**: the argument is `lbu a0,3(*(0x80115284))`, a runtime byte. Its unchecked range maps to: 0–15 a negative index before the map; **16** bank C record 39 (silent failed inflate); **17–31** `cart_record(0)`, which branch 2 turns into a 1.28 GB out-of-cartridge inflate; **32–63** the live bank-B path; **64–255** an index past the map. The caller's host is itself reached through a data table — its address sits at `0x801139a8`, entry 68 of a 141-word pointer run at `0x80113898` — whose reader is not statically resolvable | `docs/log/M94-callers-enumerated.md` |
| M95 | **the table at `0x80113898` and its reader**: it is **141 consecutive in-image function pointers** ending at a zero word (`0x80113acc`), and `0x801139a8` is entry **68** (offset `0x110`). Three sites construct its base — `0x8006da28` (into `s2`), `0x8006e1e8` (into `s0`), `0x8009b89c` (into `s3`) — and in each the index is a **one-byte field**, not a literal: the dispatcher is `lw v0,28(s0); lbu v0,0(v0); sll v0,v0,2; addu v0,v0,s2; lw v0,0(v0); jalr ra,v0` with `s0 = 0x80113ad0`, `s2 = 0x80113898`, so **entry 68 is selected when that byte is `0x44`**. This also exposed a scanner bug of the same class as `addiu`/`ori`: `find_readers.py` never invalidated a register on a **load**, so stale constants produced 167 false near-misses; fixed, the true reader appeared at once | `docs/log/M95-table-readers-and-index-68.md` |
| M96 | **`FUN_8003287c`'s callers and slot values, stated in full** (M95 omitted them): **one** caller, `0x80071ec0` in `FUN_80071e14`, with no literal slot — four forms checked (direct `jal`, `lui`+`jalr`, address as data, mid-body entry). The argument is `lbu a0,3(descriptor)` where `descriptor = *(0x80113ad0+0x1c)`. The new fact: **the same descriptor carries both runtime bytes** — dispatcher takes **byte 0** as the table index (`0x44` = 68 → `FUN_80071e14`), handler takes **byte 3** as the slot — so the two unknowns are one field relation, not two hunts. Context geometry confirmed twice: array `0x80113ad0 + i*0x174`, **10** contexts, ending `0x80114958`, which the other table reader computes independently as `0x80113ad0 + 3720`. Descriptor source is the runtime list at `ctx+0x140` | `docs/log/M96-slot-values-enumerated.md` |
| M97 | **descriptors are not built — they are script commands**: `ctx+0x140` is a frame pointer into the context itself (`ctx + (n<<5) + 0xC0`, frames of 0x20), and `ctx+0x1c` is a **command pointer** advanced by 2 (`lw a1,0x1c; addiu a1,a1,2; sw a1,0x1c`) and set from a stream argument. Byte 0 is the opcode → the 141-entry handler table; byte 3 is an operand → the slot. **No code writes either byte** — the question "who writes bytes 0 and 3" assumed a record being filled in, and there is none. So the slot values are *stream content*, enumerable only by reading the room's command stream (data), not by further disassembly. Recorded inconsistency: one path advances by 2 while readers use offsets 0 and 3, so either that path is a different 2-byte command family or `ctx+0x1c` is a sub-pointer | `docs/log/M97-descriptors-are-script-commands.md` |
| M98 | **the stream comes from a runtime buffer inside the room overlay**: `*(0x800df92c)` (= `0x800df6b0+0x27c`, the same base as the bank initialiser) holds the script structure; it is written from exactly **two** sites — `ovl_007c552:0x8016ea70` sets it to **`0x80171df8`**, a buffer at overlay offset `0x3578`, and `stage2:0x80063698` sets it from a heap pointer. The buffer is **all zeros in the shipped overlay** (opcode histogram over 2 KB: one entry, `0`, 354 times), so the commands are generated at runtime by the overlay's own code (`FUN_80170104`, `FUN_801708a4`). **The slot values are therefore not shipped data** and every static route is exhausted — traced to a buffer that is provably zero on disk, not assumed. What remains is a data question needing a running game: dump 2–4 KB at `0x80171df8` after a room load, or log `a0` at `0x80071ebc` | `docs/log/M98-stream-source.md` |
| M99 | **the stride is settled — a variable-length bytecode**: the dispatcher never advances; each handler advances `ctx+0x1c` by its own command size, and scanning stage-2 for that idiom yields **18 distinct lengths** (+1 ×20, +2 ×21, +3 ×11, +4 ×18, … +22 ×1, +40 ×1, plus two backward jumps). The opcode-`0x44` handler `FUN_80071e14` ends `lw v1,0x1c; addiu v1,v1,22; sw v1,0x1c`, so **its commands are 22 bytes** and byte 3 is an interior operand (but see M101: the slot is only *used* when descriptor byte 9 passes a gate) — which **resolves M97's "+2 vs byte 3" inconsistency**: that was two command classes, not a contradiction and not a sub-pointer. **Retracts M98 §3**: `FUN_80170104`/`FUN_801708a4` are gameplay code (float math, ÷10 via `0xCCCCCCCD`, nibble packing), not the buffer filler; the buffer is filled by a **16-byte block copy** in `FUN_8016e8d0` from a runtime pointer, and `FUN_80170104` runs *after* the copy | `docs/log/M99-stride-settled.md` |
| M100 | **the watch is set and the dump probe is built — the last input is human**: `probe.py `--reg 4@0x80071ebc` reports **no hit in 2 000 000 instructions**, and the same watch on a PC that is executed (`0x80000520`) prints a line, so the absence is meaningful — the harness still stops at boot-to-idle with no room loaded. `tools/room_probe.py` walks the whole runtime chain from one ares RDRAM export: `*(0x80115284)` → byte 3 (slot, classified by the M92/M93 table), `*(0x800df92c)` → `+0x48`/`+0x4c` → the command stream, then every opcode-`0x44` command's byte 3 at the **22-byte** stride. Against both repository dumps it correctly reports "predates a room load" and refuses. What remains is a dump taken after entering a room — a human action in the GUI, since ares' `Goto` cannot be automated (M69) | `docs/log/M100-watch-and-dump-probe.md` |
| M101 | **the room dump arrived and corrects the model — the loader is gated**: three exports in `rom/` (…-20261031-1303xx) are the first with the scene-slot context installed — `*(0x80115284) = 0x8052a614`, whose bytes are `44 00 05 1f …`: **byte 0 = 0x44 → table[68] = `FUN_80071e14`, byte 3 = 31**, so the static trace is confirmed against live memory. But slot 31 should have inflated 1.28 GB from cart `0x0f564552` and the game is running — because `FUN_80071e14` **gates the loader on descriptor byte 9**: if byte 9 ≠ `0xff` it calls a bitmap test `0x800837ac(byte9)` and exits when that returns non-zero. Every "slot 17–31 → catastrophe" statement in M92/M93 therefore applies only when the gate passes. The gate's return semantics are **open** (on a 32-bit view `0x80000000 << bit` is zero for bit ≥ 1), and one watch on `v0` at `0x80071eb0` settles it. Also: **no code writes any individual context** — 843 writes to offset `0x1c` in stage-2, 68 in the interpreter area across **21 functions**, and the only constructed-base ones are the dispatcher's 3 (all ctx0); every writer is generic and each handler advances by its own length, 8 distinct within the cluster vs the 18 across all stage-2 | `docs/log/M101-room-dump-and-the-gate.md` |
| M102 | **the gate PASSES — and it is vacuous**: a `v0` watch cannot be set (ares has no CPU debugger, and the harness never reaches the PC), so the gate was settled from its code plus the dump. byte 9 = 40 → word index 1, bit 8 → bitmap word `0x10400001`, mask `0x80000000 << 8` = **`0x0`**, test `0x0` → returns 0 → `bne v0,zero` not taken → **the loader runs**. The verdict is independent of shift direction, and **no byte9 in 0…95 blocks this descriptor**, so the gate cannot be what protects the game. Consequence: slot 31 → array 2 index 15 = 0 → `cart_record(0)` → branch 2 → an out-of-cartridge DMA whose **inflate fails silently** (M92: the return is discarded), destination keeps `0x80161cf0`. So M92/M93's "catastrophic" was wrong by one word: it is **silent**, not catastrophic — a failed load, not a fault, which is exactly what a running game shows. `room_probe.py` now prints the gate verdict itself | `docs/log/M102-the-gate-passes.md` |
| M103 | **the walk**: `tools/walk_stream.py` derives the opcode→length table from each handler's own advance idiom (**140 of 141 entries**, lengths 1…40, `0x44` → `0x80071e14`, 22) and walks from the live `ctx+0x1c`. The dump has **six live contexts**; 300 commands each gives **174 commands, 30 distinct opcodes, 6 desync stops**, with streams converging on common end addresses — real bytecode, not a desync. **Zero `0x44` commands pending**, which is consistent: the handler stores the current command into `0x80115284` when it runs, so that pointer records the **last** one, and every live pointer is past `0x8052a614`. The command there **did execute, slot 31, gate passing — and it selected nothing**: `array2[15] = 0` → `cart_record(0)` → the `OVERLAYTABLENUMC` marker → a rejected inflate whose return is discarded. So **no bank-C record is selected in this room**, and the "catastrophe" is a benign no-op. The dispatcher bounds its loop with a **frame counter** (`ctx+0x140` → frame, countdown at `+4`), not a terminator byte, so the exact bound is readable from a dump — the next small improvement | `docs/log/M104-no-other-rooms-and-the-six-stops.md` |

Across **all five** room dumps the two 48-byte maps and the destination table are **byte-identical to the cartridge image** (28/28 nonzero, 44/48 nonzero), so they are static `.rodata`, never written, and **the entry for slot 31 is 0 in every dump** while slot 72's destination entry (index 56) is 0 as well. M105's "populated later" reading is therefore **refuted**. All three new captures have an `0x44` current command with **slot 31** and a passing gate, and **no `0x44` in any context's forward range** — the command always runs early and is behind every live pointer. So `byte 3 = 31` is a **constant parameter across five captures at three moments**, the lookup always yields descriptor 0, and the loader fails silently every time. Since the map is immutable, a slot selects a real record only if its static byte is non-zero — 13 slots have that property, none observed. The discriminating dump is a **different room** | `docs/log/M106-maps-never-populate.md` |
| M107 | **ares can be driven directly — design + handover**: ares v148 has no AppleScript dictionary but its binary contains **`qSupported`** and its settings include **`DebugServer/Enabled` / `DebugServer/Port`**, i.e. a **GDB remote-serial-protocol stub**, plus `Boot/Debugger`, `--save-state slot(1-9)` and `Paths/Saves`. That converts breakpoints, register reads and RDRAM dumps into scripted operations (Route A), with save states as deterministic fixtures (Route B) and UI scripting as a last resort (Route C). Acceptance stated up front: `$qSupported` answered, then a 512 KB `m` read **byte-equal to an existing hand-made dump** — not merely "it connected". `docs/handover.md` written first so a rebuilt session needs the file, not the transcript | `docs/log/M107-ares-control.md`, `docs/handover.md` |
| M108 | **the ares handshake is settled — one leading byte, and then the whole machine**: the M107 negative is explained from the shipped binary, not from another guess — `nm -arch arm64` exposes `nall::GDB::Server`, and `nall::TCPText::Server::onData` (`0x1001ef1cc`) does `ldrb w8,[x20]; cmp w8,#0x2b; b.ne` into `"Non-GDB client detected (message: %s), disconnect client"`, i.e. **the first byte ares ever reads must be `+`**; a client opening with `$qSupported#37` is hung up on, which is exactly what M107 measured. With one bare `+` the stub answers `PacketSize=4096;fork-events-;swbreak+;hwbreak-;vContSupported-QStartNoAckMode+;xmlRegisters+;qXfer:features:read+` (note the missing `;` before `QStartNoAckMode+`) and `qXfer` reports `mips:4000`. `tools/ares_rsp.py` (probe / regs / regmap / read / dump / bp / until / cont / raw / launch) then reads **512 KiB in 0.4 s**, accepts `Z0` -> `OK`, stops with **`S05`** and a register block, and `g` is **568 B = 71 registers x 8 B**. M107's acceptance test (512 KB byte-equal to a hand dump) is **restated, not passed**: two moments of the same emulator differ in 2 157 bytes / 945 ranges, all after the last ROM-copied byte (`0x800127cf`), so it compared timing rather than transport; the state-free form passes — live `0x80000480 + 0x1234f` vs the cartridge at ROM `0x1000` is **74 575/74 575 equal, 0 differing**, and `0x80012150` reads back `OVERLAYTABLENUM` + `0x43` live. Register names are **derived**: 37 = PC (two stops at two addresses) and 4 = a0 (the static decode `daddiu a0,a0,1` beside a live counter that incremented), leaving three registers between `lo` and PC unidentified. The remaining blocker is unchanged and now cheap: a **different room** | `docs/log/M108-ares-handshake.md`, `tools/ares_rsp.py` |

So the "controller dies after a few seconds" symptom is ares' input layer handing the game nothing, not the pad and not the emulator settings. The per-frame SI read is `FUN_8000d1f0(0, 0x80014FA0)` returning to `0x80006750` — `0x80016470`, the buffer the other call sites name, is **not** the live path (a breakpoint after it never fired). `M` (write memory) works and `X` does not; `Z0`-`Z4` are accepted; 200 frames of "write 4 bytes at buffer+4, continue" ran cleanly, so a held input is mechanically expressible — but **that the game acts on it is not established**: the one promising observation (`00 10 00 00`, reported as "the screen changed") is the **R** button (Start is `10 00 00 00`, A is `80 00 00 00`) against a title screen with an attract loop, so it is not attributable, and the follow-up was never observed. **No room was reached**; the bank-C question is unchanged | `docs/log/M109-ares-injection-attempt.md` |

The discriminating field is the **stream-pointer pair** at `descriptor+0x48`/`+0x4c`, and it takes **three** values (`8052a3c8/630`, `8052a408/450`, `8052a320/3e8`), matching the owner's two doors exactly; the script buffer hash varies *within* a pair, so content is a moment signal and streams are the room signal. Across all three rooms **byte 3 = 31**, the maps are identical in all twelve dumps (`dcf489248f96`), and slot 31's entry is 0 — so the loader always gets `cart_record(0)`, the marker, and a discarded failed inflate. **Decisions: no more ares dumps, and the path is intended behaviour, not an open anomaly.** The question is closed as far as evidence goes: 3/3 rooms carry 31, none selects anything; the discriminating case (an operand in the 13 non-zero slots) is not proven absent from unexplored rooms, but bank C's map pointer `0x800eb268` is constructed nowhere in 36 images, so there is no consumer to serve | `docs/log/M110-three-rooms-one-operand.md`, `tools/dump_fingerprint.py` |
| M111 | **experiment B step 1: a provenance core that survives its own fixture**: `tools/provenance.py` tracks `const`/`param`/`ret`/`load`/`unk` per register and stack slot over a **real per-region CFG** with a merge at every join, honouring delay slots, clobbering caller-saved registers on calls and invalidating on loads. The 10-case fixture started **3/10** and caught six bugs in the analysis, two of them publishable: **delay slots were never executed** (the instruction after every branch was skipped; my own fixtures had hidden it by putting their setup *inside* a delay slot) and the region splitter **cut at the first `jr ra`, so every leaf function after it was never analysed** — caught only by cross-checking `find_readers.py`, which reports `base=0x80012150` at `0x80001440` while provenance reported nothing. Attribution also ignored reachability, publishing 2 910 attributed against a 1 293 `const` bucket. Final census over 36 images: **81 067 memory operands** = const 1 293 + unreached+const 1 617 + param 798 + load 150 + ret 1 936 + unk 17 688 + unreached 57 585, an identity that must hold for the run to be quotable. **Interprocedural is not done and is fenced off**: `param` is named and `ret` recorded, never resolved — 798 + 1 936 sites are step 2's exact input, and the bank-C map reader `0x80032a5c` plus `FUN_800327ec`'s record chain are its pre-registered acceptance tests | `docs/log/M111-provenance-step1.md`, `tools/provenance.py`, `manifests/provenance.json` |
| M112 | **experiment B step 2: summaries resolve the record chain; the map reader stays out of reach**: caller-independent summaries (`params`, `ret`, call list) by fixpoint with forward substitution — 7 093 regions, 2 972 deref a parameter, 596 return a constant, 12 942 of 22 790 call sites resolved. **Acceptance test 1 PASSES**: the record pointer is the return of `FUN_80001180` (the note's `cart_record`), passed as `a0` at the two call sites `0x80032a04`/`0x80032a84` inside `FUN_8003287c`, and the fields are read at `ret(80001180)+{0,4,8,c}` — M85's record layout confirmed independently. **Acceptance test 2 FAILS**, and the site says why: the address is `map_pointer + conditional index`, the map *pointer* load at `0x800329e4` **is** attributed (`*(0x800eb264)` = `0x800eb204`, the M86 sign-extension case in real code), while `0x80032a5c`'s base degrades to unknown, so the gap is one hop narrower, not wider. Four bugs found, all in the instrument: **jump targets masked to 16 bits** (26-bit field truncated), **`sll` swallowed as `nop`** (shared funct 0), **branch-likely delay slots treated as always-executing** (published a confident wrong address `0x800eb222` from one branch outcome — caught by disassembling the site), and **every non-boot image read from the wrong place** (compressed cartridge bytes; stage2 showed 0 `jr ra` in 293 060 words). Fixtures: 10/10 step 1, 5/5 layer 2 | `docs/log/M112-provenance-step2.md`, `tools/provenance.py` |
| M113 | **`CONSTBASE`: the map read is named, and only the runtime hop is withheld**: a new value kind `cbase(base, bias)` — a known base plus an unknown offset — reported as `800eb204+0+?` and never counted as attributed, formed only when the constant half looks like an address. Re-running acceptance test 2 gives a **structural pass**: `0x800329ec lbu base=800eb204+0+?` is bank B's map byte read, and `0x80032a5c lw base=800eb264+0+?` reads a **two-entry pointer table** at `0x800eb264`/`0x800eb268` — exactly M91's `s4 = s0 << 2` structure — while the index is set by a `li` inside a `beql` delay slot, so which bank is read is `slot < 32`, a **runtime property** a constant cannot express. Adding the rule pre-empted the `addu rd,rs,zero` move idiom and turned fixture **L2 red in one run** — the fixture earning its keep. Census after layer 2: **122 902 memory operands**, attributed **12 412 (10.1 %, up from 1 293)** = const, with param 22 040, ret 11 368, cbase 2 823, load 2 695, unk 41 946, unreached 27 507, unreached+const 2 011, paroff 100 — summing exactly. Fixtures 10/10 and 6/6 | `docs/log/M113-provenance-cbase.md`, `tools/provenance.py` |
| M114 | **jump-table edges: 4 142 new edges, and `unreached` finally moves**: a new value kind `jtent(table)` — a word loaded through a known base plus unknown index is *one entry of the table at that address* — lets an indirect jump walk its table (bounded at 512, stopped at the first non-address word) and turn the entries into CFG edges, iterated with the dataflow up to four rounds. Measured: **2 853** indirect jumps through a table, **1 237** tables walked, **4 142** entries inside the region and **25 749** outside it; `unreached` **27 507 → 22 896**, attributed **12 412 → 14 319 (11.7 %)** with the operand total unchanged. The 25 749 cross-region entries are why the bucket did not collapse — those handlers are their own regions and were analysed anyway; what those edges buy is a table-driven call graph. Three bugs: discovered targets were **not made block leaders** (edges pointed at non-existent blocks: 951 tables, zero effect — caught by fixture L7 failing while its handlers were individually attributed but `unreached`), the **census path never populated the image bytes** (`--region` resolved tables and `--census` did not, which read as "the tables are runtime-built" — a plausible, wrong conclusion), and `--callers` filtered on the callee's resolved return instead of the call target, so it went silent the moment the return resolved (`Access.call_target` now exists). Both acceptance tests hold: test 1 gained the fact that `FUN_800327ec` returns `const(0)`, consistent with M85/M86's unchecked return; test 2 unchanged. Fixtures 10/10 and 7/7 | `docs/log/M114-provenance-jump-tables.md`, `tools/provenance.py` |
| M115 | **the `unk` bucket, measured before it was fixed**: a per-register **cause** histogram (the mnemonic that made the value unknown, carried through joins and inherited by each operand) showed `addiu` at **63.9 %** of 42 879 unknown bases — a hole in the model, not in the game: `addiu rt,rs,imm` from a parameter/`paroff`/`cbase`/`sp` was discarded while the identical `addu` form was tracked. `addiu` now preserves structure (`param`+bias → `paroff`, `sp`+imm → new `spoff` frame address, and a frame address used as a base resolves to its **stack slot**), cutting unknown bases **42 879 → 14 812 (-65 %)**. What remains is no longer a modelling gap: `?` 55 % (caller-saved across a call), `join` 20 % (predecessors disagreed), `addiu`/`addu` 24 % (arithmetic on already-unknown values). The honest limit is stated: the reduction is mostly **reclassification**, not coverage — under the unified layer-2 census `spoff` holds 27 413 operands while attribution moved only 14 319 → **14 787 (12.0 %)**. Also fixed a consistency defect: `--census` used the intra-procedural path while every other mode used layer 2, so two commands reported different `unk` totals (14 812 vs 20 629) for the same corpus. Both acceptance tests unchanged and passing; fixtures 10/10 and 7/7 | `docs/log/M115-provenance-unk-cause.md`, `tools/provenance.py` |
| M116 | **frame slots: entry-relative keys, and `spoff` is not an unresolved bucket**: slots were keyed off the *current* `sp`, so a frame adjustment (`addiu sp,sp,-N`) gave one local two keys and lost its value — now keyed against the **function-entry sp** via `State.spbias`, with joins that disagree setting it to `None` rather than mixing frames; fixture **L8** encodes the exact store-adjust-reload sequence. The first version stopped writing a value into `sp` and the bucket collapsed 27 413 → 182 while `unk` grew by the same amount, which revealed what the bucket had always been: accesses through `sp` reported `spoff` only because the `sp` register held a frame value — the base is now set explicitly. Measured: **310 294** frame accesses, **280 426 (90.4 %) resolve to a stored value**, so resolution is not the weak point; `spoff` answers *where* an operand reads (the frame) while resolution answers *whether the value kept provenance*, two questions that shared one column. So the dispatcher does not advance: each handler does, **variable**, and M97 §4 resolves as block headers vs command lengths — the `+2` at `0x8006e150` is `FUN_8006e138`, opcode `0x12`, a 2-byte *block* command, and byte-3 reads belong to the 22-byte family (`0x44`/`0x4d`). **`FUN_8006e1bc` is not an initializer**: it has two `jal` callers (`0x8006e05c` in `FUN_8006dfe4`, `0x8006e160` in `FUN_8006e138`), is in no table entry, and runs a nested block — `r19 = a1 + a2`, loop until `ctx+0x1c == r19`. Bank C was **not** re-run: M110 already measured byte 3 = **31** (inside `[16,32)`) across three rooms with map entry 0 ⇒ `cart_record(0)` ⇒ discarded failed inflate, and took the decision *no more ares dumps*. Two of my own rewrites were wrong and discarded (clobber-tracking 74/141; widening the window changed ten lengths), so the shipped form is a **fallback pass** that adds exactly `0x4d = 22` and leaves 140 byte-identical; a third error was an arithmetic bug in my own `jal` scan that falsely reported *no callers*. Fixture **8/8**, `--lens` fails on a missing length (`140/141`, exit 1); **98 cases across 10 fixtures**. Limits: lengths come from the idiom, so a computed advance would be missed, and the 141 opcodes' semantics remain unnamed | `docs/log/M136-command-length-map.md`, `tools/walk_stream.py`, `tools/acceptance.py` |
| M137 | **Phase 2 opens on a reference, and its fingerprints do not fire yet**: the room header, entity system and collision are unmapped in our notes, and the only RE2 PS1 decompilation (`MeganGrass/bio2-ds`) is **not open source** — no licence, Capcom/Sony copyright, disc + prototype `MAIN.SYM` derived, LLM-exported C — so it is read **reference-only** exactly as `perfect_dark` was at M124, and every claim from it is a candidate until our disassembly confirms it. What it contributes is a *testable* design: the room file is read into the free-work head and **becomes** `pRoom`; all in-file pointers are byte offsets relocated in **two passes** (the 23-word run `pEdt0…pRbj`, then cut/model entries); the header carries eight byte counts plus `pScdx`/`pScd`, `pMessage` and `pScrl` — the PS1 counterpart of our `*(0x800df92c)` chain; `pRbj` is `[table offset][count]` over 8-byte `{Data_ofs, Seq_ofs}` entries **filtered by a per-entry flag bit**; entities are 33 slots `1..0x21` dispatched through `pEnemy[Id + 0x12]` with move state packed as `((Timer0 & 0xff00) - 0x100) \| 4`. **Three tests came back negative and are recorded so they are not re-derived**: the RBJ shape returns 0 strict hits in three overlays; the 23-word offset-run shape returns 12 of 4 762 members — against a 0-hit null — but every one is **unreferenced by the loader registry** and the top hit's run starts 220 bytes from EOF, so it is a candidate list, not evidence; and the reference does not say which of `0x8017b410`/`0x8016e880` is "the room" in a port with its own free-work bookkeeping. Next step is anchored on our code, not shape: read the handler word after each registry descriptor index (`DAT_8010b2e4 + slot*8`), which is the port's `Set_room` | `docs/log/M137-phase2-reference-and-negative-tests.md`, `docs/format_notes.md` |
| M138 | **the registry route pays, and corrects one of our own documents**: the loader registry at `0x8010b2e0` is **9 entries of 8 bytes** — 1 descriptor byte + 3 padding + 1 handler word — not the "32-byte entry, six more words" in `docs/format_notes.md` (the six extra words are the next entries; `(&DAT_8010b2e0)[slot * 8]` is a **byte** stride). The handler equals the load base exactly when the member is an overlay whose entry is at base+0 (slots 1/2/3/6) and is a stage-2 function where the member is not loaded (slots 5/7/8). Three entry points read it and **two of them do not call the handler** — `overlay_slot_reader`/`_b` only store it into a request struct, so the call comes from the loader thread and a call-graph search for "open the room" finds nothing. Slot 6 is the resident room overlay and its handler is the overlay's own `entry`, which is `FUN_8016e8d0` followed by a **state table at `0x80171860` selected by the byte `0x80171dd9`**. The port's `Set_room` is `FUN_800635b0`: it clears **718 848 B** at `0x800c52d8`, sets `DAT_800df934` **and** `DAT_800df92c` to one base (so the script chain and the scene data are one region — M99's "absent by construction" now has its mechanism), initialises **33** pointers at `0x800e13ec` to one default and clears **32** slots at stride `0xda` — the same 33 enemy slots and 32 object-model slots the reference's `Set_room` creates, which is the **first confirmed structural correspondence** rather than a guess. `_DAT_800df934` is also written from nine sites across six room overlays, which is why no single cartridge address holds the stream | `docs/log/M138-registry-route.md`, `docs/format_notes.md` |
| M139 | **S2DEX becomes searchable without a new dependency**: the 38-page guide has **no text layer** (the 534-page programming manual and the 126-page EXEGCC guide both do), and nothing usable was installed — no tesseract/ocrmypdf/pdftoppm/GS/ImageMagick and no Python imaging, only `sips`, which cannot OCR. So `tools/ocr_pdf.swift` renders with `CGPDFDocument`+`CGContext` at 4×72 dpi (288 dpi, enough for 6–8 pt body text) and recognises with `VNRecognizeTextRequest` (`.accurate`, en-US), sorting rows by descending `midY` then `minX` because Vision's boxes are **bottom-left**-origin. **38/38 pages, 1 840 lines**; five pages (1, 32, 34, 36, 38) report as near-empty rather than inventing text, and **86 distinct GBI names** plus the `ObjMtx`/`uObjSubMtx`/`G_OBJRM_*`/`G_OBJIT_*` vocabulary are recovered. Output lands in `build/ocr/s2dex.txt`, which stays **out of git** — it is the same copyrighted document. OCR damage is recorded (`ObjSubMt×`, `G_OBJRM_BILERE`), so strings are index entries, not quotations. **The tool is deliberately not in the gate**: it needs a macOS-only framework and a gitignored input, so its evidence is this run, not a suite — stated rather than hidden. What it enables is a cheap test that is not yet run: whether the game uses S2DEX at all, by scanning display lists for its sprite opcodes, which F3DEX2 does not share | `docs/log/M139-s2dex-ocr.md`, `tools/ocr_pdf.swift` |
| M140 | **the enemy routine table is found, and the room header turns out to be runtime data**: M137's "no open-source RE2" was **too strong** — `OpenBiohazard2/OpenBiohazard2` is **MIT**, 303★, Go, a *re-implementation* rather than a decompilation, so unlike `bio2-ds` it may be **read as code**; the distinction is now recorded in a table. The reference's `pEnemy[Id + 0x12]` is the **wrong shape** for our port (`0x800e13ec` holds `ENEMY_WORK*`, so that index collides with the slots), so the table was found by **census** of every indirect dispatch: `(*(code *)(&PTR_FUN_8011af80)[*(byte *)(obj + 5)])()` and its partner `…afb8` called with the same index, **22 call sites**, and the tables are spaced **0x38 = 14 words** apart (`…af80, …afb8, …b434, …b46c, …b4a4, …b4dc, …b514, …b6c0`) — a family of **14-entry routine blocks**. `obj+5` is `Routine_1` of the reference's packed `Routine_0..3`, which is the motion index, so the field matches; `0x8011afb8` is followed by **154** stage-2 pointers with duplicate period **14**. Bounds are **not** established (an out-of-range byte would run into the next block — a hazard, stated as such). The room header question is answered **negatively, with a reason**: `DAT_800c52d8 = FUN_8008af80(0xaf800)` is a **heap allocation**, so no image contains the header — that explains M137's twelve unvalidated candidates. The shape was still tested across all 36 images (0 hits) with a shuffle control that also gave 0, so it is recorded as **inconclusive, not evidence of absence**. The surviving route is the block ids `0x15b9`/`0x15ba`/`0x15bb` → descriptors → members, the method that tied the nine registry slots to members at M19 | `docs/log/M140-enemy-table-and-room-header.md`, `docs/format_notes.md` |
| M141 | **OpenBiohazard2 names the opcodes — and breaks M136's "one length per opcode" claim**: the MIT Go re-implementation ships `fileio/rdt_*.go` (room file: scd, flr, lit, msg, pri, rid, sca, esp, vab) and a `script/` package of **named opcode structs**; summing each struct's fields (Go binary.Read adds no padding) gives the PS1 command length. Of our 141, **45 opcode numbers are shared**, **27 match the length exactly**, **16 differ but the PS1 size is one of the handler's own advances**, and **2 are unexplained** (`0x32 PosSet` 4 vs 8, `0x46 AotReset` 2 vs 10) — so **43 opcodes now have a name**, 96 numbers simply are not in their 45-opcode subset. Two independent confirmations: **`0x44` is `SceEmSet`** with field order `Opcode, Dummy, Aot, **Id**, Type, …` — byte 3 is `Id`, exactly the operand M94 measured and M110 tracked as 31, so it was never an anonymous "slot" but an **enemy id** (M110's measurement unchanged, its interpretation sharpened, and `FUN_8003287c` reads as an id→bank-record resolver); and **`0x4d` is `DoorModelSet`**, 22 B in both, on the very handler that needed M136's cross-register fallback. **The correction**: 16 handlers hold *several* advances inside their own function and `walk_stream.py` advances by the first one, so walks desynchronise on those opcodes — the fault is the claim that one length suffices, not the scan; the PS1 source shows the mechanism directly, `Calc`/`Calc2` sharing opcode `0x26` with sizes 5 and 4 | `docs/log/M141-openbiohazard2-names-the-opcodes.md`, `tools/walk_stream.py` |
| M142 | **the licensed table is imported and the walk now refuses to guess — and M141's own correction was wrong**: `fixtures/scd/opcodes.json` (45 numbers, **46 variants** — `0x26` is `Calc` 5 *and* `Calc2` 4) with a full **MIT** attribution block, committed *because* MIT permits it; `walk_stream.py` gains `ps1_opcodes()`, `handler_extent()`, `advance_set()`, `resolve()` with statuses `ok`/`unverified`/`ambiguous`, prints names (`opcode 0x44 SceEmSet len 22`, `Id(byte3)=…`) and **stops loudly** instead of advancing by a guess. **M141's "16 handlers hold several advances" was an artifact**: for handlers with **no Ghidra range** the scan fell back to a ±512-instruction window and read the *neighbouring* handler's advances — which is why the NOP at `0x00` appeared to hold `[1,2,3,4]`. With the extent = min(next table handler, next Ghidra function, +0x1000) the truth is **117 ok / 24 unverified / 0 ambiguous**, and two published lengths were wrong: **`0x2d ObjModelSet` is 38** (M136 said 3) and **`0x46 AotReset` is 10** (said 2), both now exactly the PlayStation size — so **35 of 37 named walkable opcodes match exactly** and only `0x09 Sleep` (1 vs 4) and `0x26 Calc` (6 vs 5/4) conflict. Of the 24 unverified, **seven are named and five are the control-flow family** (`Goto`, `GoSub`, `ForStart`, `Switch`, `ElseStart`) where *no constant advance is the semantics* — they set the pointer from an operand, the `addu a1,a1,t0` flavour M98 named. Fixture **15/15** (the seven new cases caught two defects in my own new code: `advance_set` broke after one advance, making `ambiguous` unreachable, and the name followed list order rather than the chosen size); **105 cases across 10 fixtures** | `docs/log/M142-licensed-table-and-a-walk-that-refuses.md`, `fixtures/scd/opcodes.json`, `tools/walk_stream.py`, `tools/acceptance.py` |
| M143 | **the jump family decodes and `0x32 Poset` was never missing a length — only a window**: the five control-flow opcodes share one property, **none advances by a constant**, which is why the scan found nothing and why M142's stop was correct. Each is now decoded from its handler into `JUMP_FAMILY` with the evidence inline: `0x07 ElseStart` **4 B**, target `cmd+u16@2` (pops the frame); `0x0d ForStart` **6 B**, `cmd+s16@2+6`; `0x13 Switch` **4 B**, case-table skip then a scan of `0x0c`-marked cases; `0x17 Goto` **6 B**, `cmd+s16@4`; `0x18 GoSub` **2 B**, **dynamic** — `*(0x80114f2c)[index] + record.u16@0`, both halves outside the command. **All five sizes equal the PlayStation sizes exactly**, five independent agreements on the one family where our measurement had nothing. `0x32 Poset` advances by **8** (PS1 8) — M136 published 4 and M142 called it unverified because the write-back sits **four** instructions after the `addiu` and the window was two; widening to eight while insisting the **first** `ctx+0x1c` write wins moved **11** opcodes to `ok`. One tightening caught a real error: `0x28` and `0x45` write the pointer in the **delay slot** of a `jal`/`jr`, which executes *before* the transfer, so a call boundary must not invalidate a candidate unless the slot is checked. Classes are now **128 ok / 5 jump / 8 unverified / 0 ambiguous = 133 walkable**, up from 117; the walk follows `Goto`, takes the fall-through of conditionals and stops on `GoSub`, verified end to end on a synthetic 8 MiB RDRAM (hand-run, **not** in the gate — stated). Fixture **20/20**; **110 cases across 10 fixtures** | `docs/log/M143-jump-decoder-and-poset.md`, `tools/walk_stream.py`, `tools/acceptance.py` |
| M144 | **`0x03` is a script jump, the window search becomes a dataflow, and the walk enters the gate**: `0x03` **writes no pointer at all** — it calls `FUN_8006d5dc`, which sets `ctx+0x1c = *(0x80114f2c) + u16[byte3]` and resets the thread, so it is an absolute **script jump by table index**, dynamic for the same reason `GoSub` is (no return saved, unlike `0x18` which stores `cmd+2` at `ctx+0x144+idx*4` — and **`0x19` loads exactly that back**, the cleanest pairing here). Five neighbours are not missing lengths but **returns and dispatches**: `0x01` writes nothing and returns 2, `0x10`/`0x1a` return saved block pointers, `0x19` returns from the GoSub slot, `0x36` is a `jr` through a callback table. `advance_set` now tracks derived registers inside the handler extent instead of searching a window, which caught **two mistakes of mine**: a store writes *memory*, not a register (`sw a1,40(v1)` was destroying the pointer in `a1`), and a call clobbers only **caller-saved** registers (`0x2d` keeps the pointer in `s2` across four `jal`s — preserving `s0`–`s7` is what resolves it to **38**, exactly the PS1 size). Classes: **126 ok / 5 jump / 6 dynamic / 3 unverified / 1 ambiguous**, with `0x1b` (advances 6 and 12) the ambiguous one and **37 of 39** walkable named opcodes still matching PS1 exactly. The walk path is now a suite — `tools/rdram_fixture.py` **builds** an 8 MiB dump (the scenario is committed, not the blob) and asserts seven behaviours, ending the era of hand-run verification. **117 cases across 11 fixtures**. Third-party lead checked: `CalenCyr/SlugfestRecompiled` is real (GPL-3.0, N64Recomp + RT64 + RSPRecomp, MIO0 variant, F3DEX2/F3DLX2.Rej) and gives *method* — notably a recompilation route that sidesteps the ares no-controller wall; the "same developers" claim is **not verified and not relied on** | `docs/log/M144-script-jump-dataflow-and-walk-gate.md`, `tools/rdram_fixture.py`, `tools/walk_stream.py`, `tools/acceptance.py` |
| M145 | **all 141 opcodes classified, and the script base is a cartridge block**: `0x40 PlcDest` = **8** (PS1 8) needed a MIPS distinction — **`jr` is a jump, not a call**, so it must not clobber caller-saved registers, which is what lost `t0`; `0x6b` = **4** and `0x7c` = **6** write back in the **delay slot of the extent's last instruction** (`jr ra` at `0x80072d5c`, `sw a1,28(a0)` at `0x80072d60`), which an exclusive extent cut off; `0x1b` joins the **jump family** (6 B, `cmd+s16@2+6`, conditional — `ForStart`'s shape exactly, name unknown since PS1 has no `0x1b`). Final map: **129 ok / 6 jump / 6 dynamic, 0 unverified, 0 ambiguous**, and **43 of 45** named opcodes matching the PS1 size (only `0x09 Sleep` and `0x26 Calc` differ). `*(0x80114f2c)` is **zero in the image**; its writers are `0x8006d8f0` (`desc+0x48`/`+0x4c`), `0x8006d998`, the **`SceEmSet` handler itself**, and `0x8009b880`/`0x8009b27c` which assign `DAT_800c52dc + 0x3b800` — the room work region — immediately before `func_0x80004cfc(DAT_80131fa8[room_byte], base, 0, "DOOR_SCD")` and `FUN_8006d5dc(ctx,0)`, **the same function opcode `0x03` calls**. So the chain is `room byte → DAT_80131fa8[room] → file id (1881…, `0x759`+) → load-by-id → script base → record 0`, which **corrects the scope of M99**: "absent by construction" was the resident overlay's BSS buffer at `0x80171df8`; the general script base is a **loaded cartridge block**, so the streams are in the assets and only the id→member step is missing. Process note: the failing fixture case was me building a patch spec and **never running `patch_text.py`** — spec built ≠ spec applied | `docs/log/M145-all-opcodes-classified-and-the-script-base.md`, `tools/walk_stream.py`, `tools/rdram_fixture.py` |
| M146 | **the id resolves and a real room script decodes out of the cartridge**: the record `0x43` that `format_notes` §2 has called "the declared 67th does not validate" since M81 is **not broken** — `FUN_80004954` reads it as the **file table** (`0xb00c8624` = ROM `0xc8624`; `0x28` header, word 0 = **8091** entries, then 8-byte `{offset, size}` with offsets relative to `0xc8624`, stamped `Tue Sep 28 02:31:44 1999`), so its `0xCDCDCDCD` size and zero destination fields are correct: it is not in the boot load list. Id **1881** (`DAT_80131fa8[0]`, first `DOOR_SCD`) is 285 B compressed → **538 B** decompressed, its head the **u16 record table** M145 predicted, and record 0 decodes as `Switch` (case skip 22) → `SwitchCase` → `GoSub` record 1 → **two `DoorModelSet` (0x4d, 22 B each)** → `Sleep` → `GoSub` record 8 → `IfElseStart` + `Compare` → `Goto` **back to itself**, a spin loop waiting on a condition — i.e. a door script, reached from cartridge bytes alone. Three additions made it work: `--raw` (place a block in a synthetic RDRAM so the *same* walk runs), **script-table resolution** (`--raw` mode's block *is* the base, so `0x18`/`0x03` stop being dynamic — before this the walk died after three commands), and a **return stack** (`0x18` pushes `cmd+2`, `0x19` — the opcode that loads that very slot — pops it). Cartridge-gated suite `room script (cartridge)` **4/4**; **121 cases across 12 fixtures**; `format_notes` §2 corrected in place | `docs/log/M146-room-script-decoded-from-cartridge.md`, `tools/room_script.py`, `tools/walk_stream.py`, `docs/format_notes.md` |
| M147 | **the whole file table swept: 102 673 commands, all 141 opcodes seen, and the shape of a scenario**: 8 091 entries → 7 978 zlib blocks → **1 049 script blocks**, 5 111 records, **102 673 commands**, **141 of 141** opcodes observed; **3 126** entries are not zlib and **3 803** decompress but are not scripts (models, TIMs, audio), so the script/not-script split is a **heuristic** and is labelled one. `fixtures/scd/profile.json` holds **counts and opcode numbers only — no cartridge bytes**. The context column is what pays: **`IfElseStart` → `Compare` ×6672**, **`Compare` → `0x02` ×6261**, **`0x00` → `Goto` ×6379** and **`Goto` → `IfElseStart` ×6168** (the loop M146 decoded, now the modal shape of the corpus), **`Sleep` → `0x0a` ×1363**, and **`0x20` → `0x20` ×7147** — a long self-run, which with `0x20` sharing the one-byte NOP handler (M136) reads as **fill**. Two bugs in my own profiler, both caught by cross-checks: flat bigrams **across record boundaries** invented a successor at every boundary, and `json.dump(sort_keys=True)` re-sorted the successor dict **alphabetically**, so the file claimed `IfElseStart` is followed by `0x05` (25) when the truth is `0x23` (6672) — successors are now an ordered list of pairs. Limits stated: counts are **lower bounds** (the walker stops at five non-return dynamic opcodes), ≤8 records and ≤200 commands per block. Cartridge-gated suite **5/5** (including a permanent cross-check against `walk_stream`'s CLI on id 1881); **126 cases across 13 fixtures** | `docs/log/M147-opcode-profile.md`, `fixtures/scd/profile.json`, `tools/script_profile.py` |
| M148 | **a dozen opcode reads, and a second naming source that cannot be confused with the first**: twelve unnamed opcodes are read from their handlers and named descriptively — `0x02 ~CondEnd` (advance 1, **return 2**, follows `Compare` 6 261×), `0x08 ~EndBlock` (pops the frame `0x07` uses), `0x0a ~WaitCount` and `0x0e ~LoopWhile` (**both blocking**: they leave `ctx+0x1c` untouched until a u16 counter expires, which is why `Sleep` is followed by `0x0a` 1 363× — a mechanism fact, not just a name), `0x2f ~SetMotion`/`0x30 ~ApplyMotion` (a producer/consumer pair over `ctx+344…354` → position 40/44/48 and angles 100/102/104), `0x81 ~StateDispatch`, `0x82 ~ReleaseByKey`, and the sound family `0x84 ~SndMode`, `0x85 ~SndPlay3D` (two 3-axis vectors into `FUN_8006ab40`), `0x86 ~SndBusy`, `0x87 ~SndStop`. The names live in a **separate fixture** (`fixtures/scd/inferred_names.json`, each with the instructions its reading rests on) because `fixtures/scd/opcodes.json` is MIT-transcribed and holds the **original developers'** names: the licensed name always wins, an inferred one prints with a leading `~`, and the profile records `name_source` per opcode. Named count **45 → 57 of 141**; profile regenerated (names and provenance only, counts unchanged), and it shows `SceEmSet` firing **953** times and `DoorModelSet` **494**. `patch_text.py` refused a spec of mine as *"old and new are identical"* — the M145 mistake again, caught by the guard a second time. Fixture **22/22**; **128 cases across 13 fixtures** | `docs/log/M148-twelve-opcode-reads.md`, `fixtures/scd/inferred_names.json`, `tools/walk_stream.py`, `tools/script_profile.py` |
| M149 | **the two-NOP question, and three more reads — one of them a random number generator**: `0x00`/`0x1e`/`0x1f`/`0x20` all point at `0x8006db14` (advance 1, return 1), so **no interpreter can distinguish them**; what differs is the data, and on *decoded* sequences it is sharp — `0x20` is followed by itself **7147 of 7753** times in 606 runs with **median 10, p90 31, max 62**, while `0x00` is preceded by `0x02` 7202 times in short runs. So `0x20` marks **long empty stretches** (`~Fill`) and `0x00` pads **between commands** (`~Nop`), with the caveat that *which one executes* needs reachability. **Three measurements failed and are recorded**: "tail after the last decoded command" is meaningless because a record **jumps around inside itself**; raw-byte run counting contradicts the decoded pairs (94 642 raw `0x20` runs vs 7 147 decoded pairs) because most `0x20` bytes are **operand bytes**; and reachability from record starts marked **96 %** of everything unreachable because the walk stops at the five dynamic opcodes — no power. What worked: **`0x00` precedes a record start in 3746 of 6185 cases with `0x01` two bytes earlier**, i.e. records end `01 00` — so **`0x01` is the record terminator**, which also explains its M145 "writes no pointer, returns 2" classification. Three more names: **`0x28 ~Rand`** (`FUN_8006d284` is a generator — `seed + ((seed&0x7fffffff)>>15) + (seed&0x7fff8000)*2` into `DAT_800e8cb2`; self-following 1018×, i.e. repeated rolls), **`0x05 ~ClearSlot`** (clears byte 1 of a 0x6c-strided entry at `0x80113ad0`), **`0x88 ~CountdownSlot`** (decrements a counter by its operand and clears the entry at zero). **45 licensed + 17 inferred = 62 of 141 named**; **128 cases across 13 fixtures** | `docs/log/M149-two-nops-and-three-more-reads.md`, `fixtures/scd/inferred_names.json`, `fixtures/scd/profile.json` |
| M150 | **the block-return slots are modelled, the CFG walk is real, and the NOP answer flips**: the five dynamic opcodes are the VM's **structured exits**, and scanning every handler for accesses to `+4/+8/+32/+96/+160` gives the whole mechanism — **push** by `0x0d/0x11/0x13/0x1b` (`sw 32` and `sw 96` + depth at `ctx+thread+8`), `0x06 IfElseStart` (the other depth, `+4`) and `0x18` (GoSub slot at `ctx+0x144+idx*4`); **pop** by `0x10`/`0x12` (`+0x20`), `0x1a` (`+0x60`), `0x19`; and `0x0e` looping back through `+0x20`. A pop now resumes at the opener's continuation — an approximation that **over**-approximates reachability, the safe direction — and record starts reached jump from ~90 % (with everything downstream dead) to **7 146/7 216 = 99.0 %**. Census at reachable command starts: `0x00` **95.9 %**, `0x20` **100 %**, `0x1e`/`0x1f` 100 % — so **all four aliases execute** and `0x20`'s 564 runs (median 10, max 62) are entirely reachable. **M149's `~Fill` reading is retracted** (its test had no power): `0x20` is an executed run of no-ops and is renamed **`~NopRun`**, while `~Nop` is an executed pad before a jump with ~4 % genuinely skipped. The two are behaviourally identical and both execute, so the remaining "why two opcodes?" is a **tooling question the cartridge cannot answer** — recorded as the answer rather than left open. New cartridge-gated suite `cfg reachability` **4/4**; **132 cases across 14 fixtures** | `docs/log/M150-block-slots-and-the-nop-answer.md`, `tools/cfg_reach.py`, `fixtures/scd/inferred_names.json` |

That lifts record-0 reach from **20.4 % to 33.2 %** and cuts the dead-only list to **two**: `0x7a` (15×, 16 B — stride-76 table at `*(ctx+0x154)+0x234`, ORs `0x4a` into flags, packs bytes 3/4/5 at `96(entry)`, five u16s at 304–316) and `0x7f` (10×, 6 B — the object table `*(0x800c76b0+0x20000)+636` at stride 20, the same table `0x6b`/`0x7c` write). **Six of M151's eight were empty-stack artefacts**: `0x59 XaOn` 29 occurrences now reachable, `0x5f` 4, `0x62` 5, `0x71` 2, `0x4c` 1, `0x6a` 1. The discriminating test: the share of 8-byte-aligned words carrying a GBI opcode is **min 0.12, median 0.38, max 0.84, with 0 of 65 above 0.95** — a display list is ~1.00. Header: magic `0xBE1E`, **`h1` (32/64/128, unidentified)**, **width**, **height**, **format (1 = 4 bpp, 2 = 8 bpp)**, **palette entries (16/256)**, two zero words; then `entries` × **RGBA5551**, then the pixels. Both formats verify independently: **21 tiles** are 4 bpp with **every nibble ≤ 15 and exactly `2·w·h` of them**, **44** are 8 bpp with exactly `w·h` bytes — the nibble reading is what closed it, since a byte holds **two** indices at 4 bpp, which is why fmt=1 first looked impossible (indices to 255 against a 16-entry palette). This also explains three earlier anomalies: M161's "no clean RGBA5551" (the payload is **indices**, not colour), M164's "descending RGB555 gradient" (that was the **palette**, read correctly but attributed to the wrong object), and why no correlation with the model's `N` records ever fitted (the tile format has none). Open: `h1` equals neither dimension, and whether the per-tile palette is shared by the engine. | M166 | **`h1` pinned: it is the true height, not a stride**: audited across all 65 tiles — **`h1 == h·8/bpp` fits 65/65**, `w·h1·bpp/8 == pixel bytes` fits **65/65**, while the stride readings fit **2/65** (bytes) and **0/65** (8-byte words) and are refuted. The field at offset 6 is not a duplicate but `h1·bpp/8`, the height in one-byte-per-pixel units — which is why the two agree at 8 bpp and differ by two at 4 bpp, and it completes M165's nibble result (a 4 bpp tile with `h1=64, h=32` holds 64·64 = 4096 pixels in 2048 bytes = `w·h1·bpp/8` exactly). **Every field of the 16-byte T64 header is now decoded and gated** — magic, height, width, 8-bit height, format, palette count, two zero words — with the palette and raw pixels after it. One open question remains, about *usage* not format: whether the engine shares the per-tile palettes. | M167 | **palettes are not shared — whole tiles are**: tracing `FUN_800874d4`'s **seven** callers found `FUN_80085164(block)` and `FUN_8008522c(blockA, blockB)` to be **the same function with one extra store** — a 16-byte registry slot holding one or two block pointers plus a packed `slot \| generation<<8` handle. The choice is made by **id equality**: `FUN_8008769c`'s two-row driver loads **one** block and calls `85164` when the rows' ids match, and loads **two** and calls `8522c` when they differ; the general path dedupes across rows by copying an earlier **pointer**. So there is **no palette resource in this path at all** — M165 measured that each `T64` block carries its palette **inline** (16/256 RGBA5551 at the head of its data), so a pixels-plus-palette pair has nothing to point at: the absence of palette sharing is **impossible by the format**, and the pair exists for two distinct tile images. Closing loop: the data and the code agree, arriving from opposite directions. Two data-side consequences gated — every id in a two-row grid resolves to a tile, and those grids exercise **both** branches (equal ids and differing ones) — with the honest scope note that the registry layout itself is a **code reading**, not a data law. **183 cases across 17 fixtures**; audit 15/15 | `docs/log/M167-palettes-not-shared.md`, `tools/container_0141.py` |
| M168 | **the handle's high byte is a global allocation counter, not a per-slot generation**: the release path `FUN_80085300` clears the busy bit, pushes the slot onto the free list and decrements the live count — and **does not touch `DAT_80124858`**, which only the two allocators bump (and `FUN_800850e0` resets to 0 when it builds the 128-slot free list `1…0x7f`). So **nothing bumps on free**; M167's "generation" was a **misnomer**. Stale handles are still rejected, by two weaker mechanisms that compose: **monotonicity** (a reissued slot is stamped with a strictly higher counter, since reissuing *is* an allocation) and an **equality guard** (`if (stored == handle)`), which silently ignores a stale release and is the only double-free guard there is. Two caveats follow from the counter being global: it is **never masked**, so `counter<<8` would overlap the 7-bit slot field after 2^24 allocations, and a slot's handle history is not per-slot state (the guard is value inequality, not depth). The bulk path `FUN_80085398` releases by **address range**, invalidating handles by OR-ing `0xff` into the stored value — same story, same untouched counter. Scope: a **code reading**, falsifiable by §1's field table but not gated, since the registry is runtime state with no cartridge data to assert against. Gate unchanged at **183 cases** | `docs/log/M168-registry-handle-generation.md` |
| M169 | **nothing else writes the handle — and the use path doesn't just compare, it `trap(0)`s**: the handle word `DAT_8012487c` (slot record **+0**, in a 16-byte record `[handle][ptrA/free-next][link][ptrB]` pinned by the allocators' own stores) has **exactly three writers across all 36 images** — the two allocators (**mint** `slot \| counter<<8`), `FUN_800850e0` (**zero** at init) and `FUN_80085398` (**`\|= 0xff`**, deliberately invalidating) — so the equality check in `FUN_80085300` cannot be bypassed by a third writer: there is none. Three functions that take pointers *into* the array (`0x800856a4`, `0x800855d8`) write **+8**, the list link, not the handle. **Correction to M168**: the same comparison exists on the **use** path, and there it is **fatal** — `0x8008548c` resolves a handle and, on mismatch, executes **`trap(0)`**. So the invariant is enforced at both ends with different severity: **release silently ignores**, **use traps**, which also sharpens M168's caveat — after 2^24 allocations the global unmasked counter would produce a **trap**, not silent corruption. Scope: a **code reading** (four functions quoted), falsifiable but not gated, since the registry is runtime state | `docs/log/M169-handle-writers-and-the-trap.md` |
| M170 | **`trap(0)` is `break`, and the exception path never returns**: the guard in `0x8008548c` encodes **`0x0000000D` — `break`** (ExcCode 9, not a conditional `teq`), so it always fires. Boot installs a 4-instruction stub at each vector (`lui k0`/`addiu k0,0x6d0`/`jr k0`/`nop`, the 16 bytes literally at boot file offset `0x340`) → every exception vectors to **`0x800006d0`**, a handler Ghidra could not decompile ("bad instruction data") and which had to be disassembled by hand: it masks interrupts, reads **`Cause`**, posts a **fault event** into the libultra queue at `0x80012140`, dumps the interrupted register file and stops. The deciding evidence is two **exhaustive absences** in `0x800006d0`–`0x80000f00`: **0 `mtc0 $14`** (so nothing can write `EPC+4` and skip the faulting instruction) and **0 `eret`** (so the handler never returns, and the thread is never resumed). **The handle guard is therefore a hard stop — no fall-through, no retry, no recovery** — which refines M169 and settles M168's caveat: the unmasked 2^24 counter would produce a **guaranteed crash**, not silent corruption. New cartridge-gated suite `exception path` **4/4**; its first stub case passed through a fallback and was **vacuous** — the same defect this project keeps finding — and now searches the image for the 16-byte sequence. **187 cases across 18 fixtures**; audit 15/15 | `docs/log/M170-break-halts.md`, `tools/exception_path.py` |
| M171 | **where it stops — and why nothing in software restarts it**: scanning the whole handler for backward branches gives a single convergence point, **`0x8000098c`, entered by 7 unconditional branches** (`0x800009c8`, `9d4`, `9f8`, `a50`, `b9c`, `c24`, `c48`). It is a **fault-dispatch loop**, not a bare `while(1)`: `lbu t2,6480(at)` reads a state byte from **BSS** `0x80011950`, `lw t2,6512(at)` reads a handler pointer from **BSS** `0x80011700`+0x70 (`0x80011970`), and `jr t2` dispatches, with the dispatched code branching back. Nothing can leave it, and the three reasons are **checked, not argued**: the convergence block contains **no stores and no COP0 writes** (so the CPU cannot change its own state), there is **no `eret`** (M170), and `Status` was written with interrupts **masked**; the only inputs are two BSS tables that **nothing in the corpus writes**, and with one CPU spinning here, no other agent can. **The only exit is a hardware reset.** Nuance: with no fault handler registered the pointer is **0**, so `jr t2` jumps to address 0 → address error → re-entry, an **exception storm** rather than a quiet spin — either way the machine does not continue. Suite **8/8**; its table case first failed because I scanned for the offsets as `addiu` immediates when the code loads them with `lbu`/`lw`. **191 cases across 18 fixtures**; audit 15/15 | `docs/log/M171-where-it-stops.md`, `tools/exception_path.py` |

M140's "family of 14-entry blocks" was right about class 1 and wrong as a general shape: there are **two** families (plain and `'E'/'O'`) sharing **one** main table, and the other classes' tables are 4, 2, 27 and 12 long. Index 15 lands on `0x8007ccec` = `0x8011b028[1]`, the next type's table: a silent cross-type dispatch, because indices 0..153 stay inside the contiguous block.
