| M179 | **`u16@+4` refuted three ways, and the directory becomes a reading**: the field (26..7 647 over 588 records) is **not a MIDI base note** (503/588 above 127), **not a duration in ms** for 16-bit mono (exact on 8/588), **not a sample count** (payload/2 and payload/4, 0/588), and **not plausibly a loop point** — it is in range on 588/588 but `field/payload` ∈ **[0.0307, 0.1143]** on 588/588, all of it in the first decile, so no sound loops past 11.5 % of its own length; a loop population has mass at 0 and near 1 and this has neither. Parity **272/588 odd** refutes a byte offset *in two-byte units* only and is **neutral otherwise** — random parity is what a count looks like, and recording it as more would be the sounds-like-a-gate pattern. So the field is **proportional to payload length with a 3.7× spread**: a description, not an assignment. **The yield is the directory**: M178's 23/23 correlation is now a **reading** — `FUN_800b3704` bounds the index by `_DAT_8015af80` (the 588 count), reads the word's top byte and takes **0 → 8000 Hz, else 16000**, and forms the record address as **`& 0xFFFFFF` + `_DAT_8015af78`** — so the word is `[rate byte][u24 offset]` by code, not by two numbers agreeing, with a second independent instance of the idiom in `FUN_80004a6c` (**bit `0x8000` → 8000, `& 0x7fff` → index**). Three cases pin the three expressions so a re-decompile that loses them **invalidates the claim instead of silently keeping it**. Also lands the **ratchet** (`tools/ratchet.py`), the first verification infrastructure here that is not about the cartridge: **match** (the registry must equal the baseline — added, deleted, raised *or* lowered all fail, so no change is silent), **decrease** (`--write` refuses to lower a count or drop a suite without `--allow-decrease`), **src** (the `src/` tree hash must match — a verifier may add cases, not edit the corpus to make them pass). Its `--self-test` found **three defects in the ratchet itself** while it was being written: `importlib` served a **stale `.pyc`** so it graded counts it never read; the `src/` case was **vacuous** (it ran with the registry still dirty and would have reported detection with the comparison deleted); and a **raised** count passed silently, so "requires `--write`" was never enforced. It also exposed a **loophole in `audit_gates.py`**: the verdict was `returncode != 0 or "FAIL" in out`, and a suite that deliberately prints `FAIL` lines was therefore detected unconditionally — one ratchet audit caught nothing and read PASS. The verdict now uses the `N/M` line and the exit status, the rule `acceptance.py` already used. **The ratchet's first customer is this milestone**: `file table` 20 → 28 and `gate audit` 35 → 37 were **refused until `--write`**. Three denominators reconciled: **126 is not 91** (4 852 → 4 726 distinct, so 126 repeats over 67 shared offsets, and **91 = 126 − 35**); the audit suite's **26/30/35/37** is growth, not contradiction. M5 untouched, `u16@+4` one open field, id 1983 still located-but-unparsed | `docs/log/M179-u16-at-plus-4-refuted-and-the-directory-read.md`, `tools/ratchet.py`, `tools/file_table.py`, `tools/audit_gates.py`, `fixtures/gates/ratchet.json` |
| M180 | **the counts are derived, not hand-edited**: seven numbers (total suites, total cases, the runnable pair, the gated pair, and the **audit suite's own count** — the one that actually drifts, **26 → 30 → 35 → 37 → 39** across five milestones) are quoted in **four files in twelve phrasings**, and one arc produced three propagation errors re-deriving them by hand (**273** left in the README at 297, **129** where the gated total was 133, **177** where the runnable tier was 178) — none a wrong claim about the cartridge, all clerical, none caught by a gate. `tools/counts.py` executes the same `SUITES` list the runner executes and checks all twelve sites. The rule that makes it a gate rather than a grep: **a phrase that disappears is a failure** — every pattern must be present exactly N times, so deleting the line cannot make the check pass, which is this project's signature defect in its purest form. `--self-test` runs on a **copy of the real files**, not a template, so it cannot drift from the phrasings it protects; it includes **one wrong value among the three identical `all N suites / M cases` lines** in the workflow, which a `count == 1` check would miss. **It paid for itself in the milestone that added it**: registering the suite failed eleven sites, and re-deriving after the audit grew caught the *same* clerical error a second time, made by the author of the gate within twenty minutes. Also the **seventh audit-environment gap** and the first silently counted as success: `README.md` was missing from the audit tree, the self-test raised `FileNotFoundError`, and the audit recorded it as **detection** because its verdict is an exit code — a missing input and a caught mutation are indistinguishable from one. Both new audits were hand-verified to fail on their intended case (7/12 checks; exactly `a phrase that disappears is caught`, 3/4), not by exception. Numbers in the milestone notes stay frozen and unchecked: M178's note says 297 because that was true at M178 | `docs/log/M180-counts-derived-and-asserted.md`, `tools/counts.py`, `tools/audit_gates.py`, `tools/acceptance.py` |
| M181 | **the verifier — one entry point, and admissibility is not a judgement call**: `tools/verify.py` runs the four procedures the project already maintains (`ci.sh`, `ratchet`, `counts`, `audit_gates`) in a fixed order and adjudicates. The rule it may not reinterpret is the **first branch** of `adjudicate`: if `audit_gates.py` reports any vacuous or broken entry, the verdict is **`INCONCLUSIVE`** no matter how green everything else is, because the checks that produced the evidence have not been shown to fail. `--self-test` feeds each branch synthetic results, including the inadmissible ones, so the rule is proven rather than asserted — **7 cases**, and the three audits aimed at the branches found a **vacuous case in the verifier's own self-test** (case 6 passed a procedure with exit 1 *and* a `(2, 3)` tally, so the "fewer cases than it ran" branch caught it even with the failure branch disabled; it now uses a clean `(3, 3)` tally). `docs/VERIFICATION.md` is the standing brief: what the verifier may decide, what it may not (no `src/` edits, no lowered counts, no commits, `INCONCLUSIVE` rather than a guess), and how a gate is finished — passing is not finishing, being shown to fail is. The contract's "do not invent a mechanism when one exists" is the **weak** restriction; the strong rule is the inverse, that a new claim class needs a new check and every check needs a mutation audit. First real run, by a sub-agent given only the contract, on `9e9118c`: **PASS**, admission `42/42`, 0 vacuous, coverage full, tree unchanged | `docs/VERIFICATION.md`, `tools/verify.py`, `tools/audit_gates.py` |
| M182 | **nothing in the corpus reads the record header**: M179 left `u16@+4` unassigned and named the route — the code that reads it. Three negatives, each pinned to a measurement. **(a)** No built image or unpacked member contains the literal **`MORT`** (4 798 files scanned; the bank is not among them because id 1979 is stored raw), so the tag is **never validated** and the ten-byte header is skipped at a **fixed offset**. **(b)** The audio cluster is **completely decompiled** — 26 functions in `0x800b3000`–`0x800b6000`, **0** missing — and the corpus census is **3 678** function ranges over 36 images with **74** lacking a `src` file, **0** of them in `0x800b0000`–`0x800bffff`. **(c)** `FUN_800b4a04` reads `+0x1a4` and `+0x1a8` to initialise stream state — a `u16` from `+0x1a4`, a **`short` from the same address as a `u32`**, `& 0xffffff` from `+0x1a8` — yet **exactly 4 writes to a literal `+0x1a4` exist in the whole corpus and none is in the audio family**, and no decompiled function addresses `0x80158c`–`0x80158e`. So the reader exists, is decompiled, and consumes fields **nothing writes**. Two readings, **stated as readings not findings**: the header is **copied** into the voice struct rather than parsed — under which `+0x1a4` holds `[u16 field][u16 rate]`, exactly the half-word pattern the code shows, and the rate in the low half is consistent with `(short)` yielding 16 000/8 000 — or the field is consumed by the **RSP microcode**, in which case no MIPS reading will ever assign it. **Honest limit**: `0x1a4` is a *literal offset in one rendering*; the same field can be reached through a different base expression, so the claim is "no write at that literal offset", not "nothing writes the field" — the same distinction as M179's parity sentence, and the gate is written to the weaker statement. What it buys: the skip is by fixed offset with **no sentinel check**, so a wrong header size will not be caught at runtime by a tag check; and `u16@+4` is **unassignable from the corpus at its literal offset**, so the remaining routes are a different base expression or the microcode, not more searching of the same kind | `docs/log/M182-nothing-in-the-corpus-reads-the-record-header.md`, `tools/file_table.py`, `fixtures/gates/ratchet.json` |
| M183 | **a milestone that exists only as a commit is refused**: landing M182 needed the `M181` row as an anchor and found none — the verifier had been committed with a tool, a suite, three audits and **no record of itself**, and nothing noticed, because `milestone_index` only requires every number **from 0 to the highest**, so a milestone *at or above* the highest is structurally invisible. Two-sided gate, because each leg catches the other's blind spot: **(a) accounting** — every `M<n>` in a commit subject must be a row, a note or a named gap (the M181 shape); **(b) freshness** — the commit fixture must be within **one** milestone of the record, because (a) is only as good as its input, and a fixture that stops being regenerated keeps passing over an ever-older list, which is the gate-that-cannot-fail defect again. The fixture is `fixtures/milestones/commits.json`, **committed and never read from git at check time**, for the reason already written in this tool — *a CI clone is shallow*, so a `git log` check would fail on a runner for an environmental reason and prove nothing. **The one-milestone lag is deliberate**: a row is written before its commit exists, so equality would fail on every milestone in progress; the cost, named rather than hidden, is that a skipped re-seed is caught at the **next** milestone. Measured: **109** distinct numbers across **210** commit subjects, highest **182**, none unaccounted — so it passes on the current record and **would have failed on M181 when it landed**. Synthesis is not here: it calls **FUN_800b62c0**, which transforms the 13 coefficients by `(param_1 & 7)` and scatters them at **stride 3** into a 42-short table — a polyphase kernel — beside **FUN_800b63b4** and **FUN_800b4dc0**, all three pinned by path. **The parse fails both ways**: MSB-first **362/588** overrun with **3** terminating within 8 bits of the end, LSB-first **339/588** with **0**. A correct transcription of a correct layout terminates within padding for essentially every clip; this terminates for three. **The blocker is now named and it is not another bit order**: the decoder does not read the record's bytes — it reads a **4 KiB ring** that `FUN_800b4a04` fills in `0x400`-byte chunks, and nothing here has established what that copy does. A byte swap, a halfword swap or a chunked re-layout would each leave the record bytes bit-incompatible with the reader **while leaving the widths correct** — consistent with all three measurements: widths summing to 260, the aggregate being 1.17× over budget, and no bit order parsing. **Next step is the ring-fill transform**, not a fourth guess. **No audio is decoded** — the block layout is transcribed and its widths confirmed, the stream's provenance is not | `docs/log/M195-the-voice-stream-transcribed-and-the-ring.md`, `tools/file_table.py` |

## Retractions carried by later milestones

| retracted | by | what changed |
|---|---|---|
| "**62.3 %** of code bytes claimed (1 212 984 of 1 947 632 B)" (M84, restated M177 and in the README/handover) | **M205** | **62.2 % of *image* bytes (1 210 600)** — `export_src.py` claimed one 4-byte word past every function body and the union absorbed most of it; the denominator was never code bytes, because nothing here classifies code independently. Announced figures in `docs/announcement-v1.0.md` are left as published |
| "for **16** opcodes the handler contains more than one advance constant" (M141 §3) | **M211** | **one** opcode-family survives measurement: `0x0d` and `0x1b` each carry one constant path (`6`) and one **variable** path (`6 + s16[cmd+2]`), which `walk_stream.advance_set()` folded into a bare `12`; M141's other rows were fixed-window over-reach into neighbouring handlers, the defect `handler_extent()` (M142) was written to remove. M141's own table also lists **17** opcodes under a heading that says 16, and misses `0x1b` |
| "the 30- and 38-byte advances belong to the handlers that take more than one" (M210) | **M211** | they arrived because M141 corrected `0x69 ItemAotSet4p` (2 → 30) and `0x2d ObjModelSet` (3 → 38) against the PS1 table — a size correction, not a second advance |
| "stream state at `+0x1a4`/`+0x1a8`" (M197, repeated in M222's brief) | **M222** | they are **ring bytes 4–11** — the MORT header, read once while `rom_offset == 0`. The ring is 4 KiB from `ctx+0x1a0`, so it spans `0x1a0…0x119f`; stream state begins at `+0x11a0` |
| "PROJ lists hold 5 136 shorts, 4 301 pool keys" (M185); "every list opens `[0,a,0,b]`" and "834 prologue zeros" (M186); "list[5] is the 142+148 pairing" and "list[2] holds 2 027" (M200/M202) | **M219** | the lists live at `record + 8 + offset`, not `record + 0`: the six spans overlap **417/417** records at `+0` and **0/417** at `+8`, and `+8` is where the offset fields themselves begin. Corrected: **7 388 shorts over lists 0–4 in [3 468, 3 316, 261, 4, 339], 7 369 pool keys (99.7 %)**, distinct `[2026, 1192, 242, 4, 327]`; there is no `[0,a,0,b]` prologue; **list 5 is a 12-byte row array** (3 822 rows) and reading it as terminated u16 yields **4** values in the whole file; list 2 holds **2 026**, not 2 027. **What survives the correction**: the 417-record chain, the `+4` key bijection less `11`, the rotation from 118, the tiling, and — re-measured — **531 still resolves to no pool record**, now as one of **4** distinct unresolved keys (`-32616, -16251, 531, 16385`). M188's "sections 1–3 never referenced" is retracted (the negative keys resolve) |
| "+24 is the **loop end**" (M193) | **M203** | it is a loop **length**: the reader loads it as a 32-bit word and the mixer compares **start + length** with the play position, so the record's declared length covers start + length + a remainder (`0x800a3cfc`, `0x800a5618`). Measured 187/187; the name came from a description, not from the cartridge |
| "`u16@+4` **is** the block count" (M193) | **M194** | **not established** — 260 bits per declared block would need **1.17×** the payload and **531 of 588** clips cannot hold it |
| "the `MORT` header is **16** bytes" (M193) | **M194** | **back to 12** — `u32@+8` ends at +12 and `FirstBitPosition = 0x60` is 96 bits = the header; `HeaderSize = 16` is the minimum clip size |
| "12 of 1 192 samples do not decode cleanly" (M193) | **M194** | my 0.7 threshold, not a failure set — correlation is continuous from +0.443 and the low tail is noise-like (ZCR 0.282 vs 0.127) |
| "the `MORT` header is 12 bytes" (M189) | **M193** | it is **16**: `u32 at +8` is the clip length in four-byte words, which the 12-byte reading had cut in half |
| "the 256-byte prelude is 128 raw int16 samples" (M190, M192) | **M193** | it is the **codebook**, and the book is **shared by the bank**, not per sample — decoding with a sample's own head gives 658 good, with the shared one 1 180 |
| "a 16-byte prologue of 8 samples" (M187) | **M190** | the prelude is **256 bytes / 128 samples**; M187 read only the first 16 bytes of it |
| "97.58 %, with a scattered 2.42 % residual" (M187) | **M190** | **100.00 %** at phase 256 — 0 failures in 217 944 groups; the residual was the misalignment |
| "bank C is slots 38–65", "bank B is slots 10–37" (M81, M82, M84, M85, M86, M88) | **M89/M90** | `cart_record(i) = 0x80012150 + i*16`, and index 0 is the `OVERLAYTABLENUM` header, so the parser numbers valid records from 0: **parser slot = cart_record index − 1**. Bank C is **records 39–66**; bank B is **records 11–38**. It went unnoticed because the two slot maps only *align* with the two banks once the offset is applied, and a misalignment reads like a wrong map rather than an off-by-one |
| "the 28 payloads are code by every signal available" (M81) | **M82** | an inference from matching sizes; refuted at the literal level, and M89/M90 settle it: bank C is dead data |
| "region coverage 0 % → 99.6 %" (M82) | **M83** | produced by a boundary that reads an undefined register; the honest figure with corrected entries was 5.3 %, and M84 recovered the region properly from a static jump table |
| "61.1 % → 61.6 % from discovery" (M82) | **M83** | the run was contaminated by a broadcast seed file; the clean differential was +25 functions / +0.2 points before the table class |
| "40-byte array, 30 distinct values, 2 in 38–65" (M86) | **M88/M90** | two 48-byte arrays, 28 nonzero slots each, values 11–38 and 39–66 |
| "there is no bank-C caller / bank C is dead data" (M89) | **M91** | the loader reads **both** maps, selecting with `s4 = s0 << 2`; `find_readers.py` resolved only base+immediate and was blind to base+register |
| "the slot map may be populated later in the room's life" (M105 §5) | **M106** | the maps are byte-identical static `.rodata` across all five room dumps spanning 130338→132952; slot 31's entry is 0 in every one |
| "the `ctx+0x140` frame holds a countdown that bounds the walk" (M104 §3) | **M105** | measured `0xff` in every context of both dumps; the frame is zeroed apart from a command pointer, and the dispatcher's only bound is `sltiu s1,10` per call |
| "record 67 survives because `record[1] == 0`" (audit suggestion, M92 §5 implied) | **M93** | its `record[1]` is `0xCDCDCDCD`; it is safe only because boot reads field 0 directly and never hands it to `FUN_800327ec` |
| "s0 is 1 iff slot ≤ 16" (M91) | **M92/M93** | `sltiu` tests `slot < 32`; `s0 = 1` selects bank C's map for slots below 32, and only slot 16 maps there |
| "27 bank-C slots" (M88/M90) | **M92** | both maps carry the same **28** nonzero slots; the 27-item list dropped 63 |
| "bank C is itself compressed 6–7×" (M90) | **M91** | `H = 3.59` bits/byte caps lossless at 2.2×, so the bytes are structured, not compressed; the ratio is `span/stored`, and `span` is a destination range for bank C but a verified decompressed size for bank B |
| "the five room dumps are five instants of one corridor" (M104, carried into M106) | **M110** | the resident overlay payload is byte-identical across all eight room captures, so it is not a room discriminator; the stream-pointer pairs take **three** distinct values, matching the owner's two door animations — the captures are three rooms, and the room signal was in the data all along |
| "the ares stub answers nothing to standard RSP, so it may not be RSP at all" (M107) | **M108** | it answers **full RSP**; `nall::TCPText::Server::onData` rejects the connection unless the **first byte** received is `+`, and `$qSupported#37` therefore closed the socket — one leading `+` and `qSupported`, `m`, `g`, `Z0`, `c` all work |
| "the DMA ring transforms the voice bytes" (M195) | **M197** | the copy is **verbatim** — a DMA callback into the 4 KiB ring — and the parse failed because bit *p* is the `p & 31` bit of a big-endian word, i.e. bytes reverse inside each 4-byte group; the record's payload **is** the stream |
| "`u16@+4` is **not established** as the block count" (M194) | **M197** | it **is** the block count: 307 038 declared blocks parse to the end of all 588 records, and M194's 260-bits-for-every-block premise was 1.17× too high because 14.4 % of blocks are silent |
| "**20 functions**, 10 gaps, 4 indirect exits" (M223) | **M224** | these are **19 call targets**: 9 start a routine, 9 are re-entry points into a body another target also enters, and one (`0xca4`) is a bare `jr ra` — a call that runs only its delay slot. **14 of the 19 share an exit**, in 3 groups. The "gaps" were a cap artefact of the greedy body rule; reachability from IMEM 0 gives **173 words reached by no edge, 172 of them code**. And the four `or fp,zero,ra` link-saves are **all unreachable** while three `jr fp` exits are reachable and no other instruction writes r30 |
| "the `lwc2`/`swc2` immediate is not a displacement, so the **125 vector transfers are excluded** from the DMEM statistics" (M223) | **M224** | the field split is base / vt / **row opcode 15:11** / element 10:7 / **offset 6:0, scaled by the access size**; corrected, all 125 are ordinary DMEM accesses and **8 of them fold** to `0x230–0x240` against a constant base, the other 117 being addressed through runtime pointers. `rsp_ucode.json` keeps the old field until `rsp_decode.py` is corrected in step 3, and the note says so rather than letting two artefacts disagree silently |
| "the SP register map is in no document this repository holds" (M223 E4); "register 11 is called the RSP status register by N64 documentation this repository does not hold" (M223 E0) | **M224** | the map is **cited from an external source** (N64brew Wiki, RSP interface, rev 5376, CC BY-SA-4.0): **c0 SP_DMA_SPADDR, c1 RAMADDR, c2 RDLEN, c3 WRLEN, c4 SP_STATUS, c5 DMA_FULL, c6 DMA_BUSY, c7 SEMAPHORE**. The documented status register is **c4**, and this program never reads it; its first instruction polls **c11**, which is in no map, and the artefact labels it `c11 (not in the cited map)` |
| "naming them would mean importing an encoding table from another source, **which is exactly what this project refuses to do**" (M223) | **M224** | policy narrowed, not abandoned: an external table may be **used as a cited source** — attributed, no text reproduced, every consequence re-measured here — which is the rule M142 applied to OpenBiohazard2 and M223 to libdragon. What the project refuses is an **uncited** table. The local manual's silence on the SP registers is now cited rather than asserted: p115 says the application "does not program the SP directly", and the index lists `rcp.h` against pp110–111 only (DP/VI/AI/PI status) |
| "the RSP's **254 vector words are identified and never named**" (M223), because "naming them would mean importing an encoding table" | **M225** | **252 of the 254 are named** from a cited table, and the 2 that are not are refused for a *measured* reason: `funct 0x11` has **no row** in it, and this cartridge uses it twice. `unreadable` drops from 31 groups / 254 words to **1 group / 2 words**. What the citation bought is a description of the units (a 16-bit fixed-point MAC pipeline, 116 of 211 words multiplying or accumulating), **not** what the mixer does with them |
| "the `lwc2`/`swc2` immediate is not a displacement, so the **125 vector transfers are excluded** from the DMEM statistics" (M223), corrected in M224's manifest but left standing in the decoder | **M225** | the correction now lives in `rsp_decode.py`, where the misread was made: base / vt / **row (15:11)** / element (10:7) / **offset (6:0) scaled by the access size**. All 125 carry a row (`ldv` 39, `sqv` 26, `ssv` 20, `lqv` 19, `lrv` 14, `sdv` 4, `lsv` 2, `llv` 1), and `dmem.excluded_cop2_transfers` is gone |
| "an RSP encoding table this repository could cite ... **and the `jal` fan-out would read as a command switch**" (M223, `what_would_decide` item 1) | **M225** | the table arrived and the prediction **failed**: the fan-out does not read as a command switch, because M224 measured that the program has **no computed transfer at all**. Recorded as evidence E8; candidate 1 (libaudio `aspMain`) is now **refuted on the shape it predicts** rather than merely unestablished, while the verdict stays `unresolved` |
| "`0x36` dispatches through `*(0x800c98e0 + ...)` — a **callback pointer, not a command address**" (M211, and restated in M213/M213b and `vm_refusals`) | **M226** | two errors in one sentence. The table is at **0x800b98e0**: `addiu v0,v0,-26400` sign-extends, so the encoded `0x98e0` is −0x6720 and the published address is 0x10000 too high (and holds 259 zeros, which is what made it look runtime-filled). And the targets **are** commands: five static pointers to five arms of one dispatch, each selecting a base object before a shared tail writes `ctx+0x1c = cmd+12`. The advance is the constant **12** and the index selects a sub-opcode |
| "`0x36` has no constant advance and `scd_vm` must refuse it" (M213, M213b, `registry.json`) | **M226** | the advance is **12** and the refusal is gone: `unresolved_advance` 2 → **0**, rooms 6 and 21 decode, clean rooms 107 → **109** of 256. What blocked it was never the decode but the handler's Ghidra **extent** — the store at 0x800712cc lies outside the body 0x80071180..0x800711cb — which is the M212 class of correction and is queued |
| "`0x36`'s advance needs a **queued extent correction** to be findable" (M226) | **M227** | it needs the queue only in the sense that one of three handlers does: attributed against each handler's own extent, **131 of 138** write their `ctx+0x1c` inside it and **3** (`0x01`, `0x0c`, `0x36`) only through a correction. M226 measured `0x36` from its dispatch arms instead, so nothing depended on the correction — and the six other handlers once thought to need it need nothing |
| "21 functions whose **extents must be extended**" (M212, `extent_corrections.json`) | **M227** | measured, **16 are extensions** (they swallow no function start) and **5 are merges** of 22 starts in all. Every swallowed start is classified by how it can be reached, and **`0x80071744 -> 0x80071b3b` would swallow `0x800717a0`, the target of a `jal`** — so the queue may be applied as 20 entries, not 21, and that one needs a decision first. `0x8007188c` in the same correction is referenced nowhere in the image |
| "`0x800717a0` is **a leaf function the walk sailed past**" (M227) | **M228** | it is a **dispatch head**: a corpus function start (`0x800717a0..0x800717db`), the target of **7 `jal`s**, and the owner of table `0x800b98f8` (5 entries). The queue entry was not clipping a leaf — it had **no valid extension at all**, because the next called head begins exactly at `current_end + 1`. Verdict `drop`, and it is the only one of the 21 |
| "`0x8007188c` is **referenced nowhere in this image**" (M227) | **M228** | it is the target of `bne v0,zero,0x8007188c` at `0x800717b0`. M227's audit counted `jal` targets and 4-byte pointers and not **branch targets**, so the `unreferenced` class was an artefact of an incomplete reference set; with all three it is **empty across all 22 swallowed starts** |
| "the extent queue's 5 merges are **5 merges**" (M227) | **M228** | they are 2 **arm-family** merges (`0x8006e534` swallows exactly table 0x800b9540's 7 entries, `0x80071180` exactly `0x36`'s 5), 2 **handler** merges, and 1 that swallows a **called dispatch head** — which is why only that one fails. A start that appears in one of the 86 tables is an arm, and an arm belongs to the head that owns the table |
| "**3 handlers are unresolved**" (M212, carried through M227 as "M212's three unresolved") | **M229** | **0**. All three are **dispatch heads** whose first `jr ra` sits inside arm 0, which falls through into the shared epilogue; the extent of a dispatch head ends at its `jr v0`. Opcodes `0x23`, `0x2e`, `0x81` — and the same rule reclassifies two more (`0x40`, `0x41`) that were filed as fragments |
| "**4 handlers write no `ctx+0x1c`**: `0x03` plus M212's three unresolved" (M227) | **M229** | **1**: only `0x03`, whose destination comes from a record table through a `jal`. The three formerly-unresolved heads write it inside their own (now bounded) extents, and `0x40`/`0x41` write it **in one of their own table's arms** — a class M227 could not have named |
| "`0x41`'s advance store is not findable" (my own working conclusion, mid-M229) | **M229** | it is at `0x80071c94`, in a **shared continuation** its seven arms `j` to, 0x5c bytes past the last arm. A fixed `+0x40` window reported nothing; the arm scan now follows control flow. Second window-vs-attribution defect in two milestones |
| "the **35 `local` heads** are inlined inside a larger function, so their index was set before the body boundary and resolving them needs the enclosing function's dataflow" (M230) | **M231** | **wrong on both counts.** Their definitions are inside the body already (0 function starts between def and use, 14 of 14 sampled), and no window change was needed: four **forms** were missing from the constant fold — a bare `lui`, two constants added, a four-link chain, and `sp` as a base. **35 → 4** |
| "the `addu rd,rs,zero` move propagation resolved six `local` heads" (M231) | **M232** | instrumented, the branch is taken **zero times** by all 86 heads and deleting it leaves the classification identical; the six were resolved by the other four widenings. Dead code, removed, not kept as a comment |
| "**127/127** audits, **0 vacuous**" (M226–M231, README, handover) | **M232** | **49 of 132 never ran their mutation**: the sandbox copied five of 26 `manifests/*.json`, so the tool died on `FileNotFoundError` and exit 1 read as a caught mutation. The number is 130/130 after the fix, and the old one described checks that could not start |
| "the single-lane lane rule's **borrow** is proven by a mutation" (M225) | **M232** | it cannot be: every single-lane word carries `vt_elem = 12`, so `msb = 3` and the trailing `& 7` masks away every bit the borrow contributes. Neither half of the ternary can change an answer here; the anchor is deleted and the arithmetic written in its place |
| "the per-sample prefix is **16 bytes** and `sound_decode.py` drops **six frames** (384 samples) per sample" (M238) | **M239** | **wrong, and the reverse is true**: `16 mod 40` is also `256 mod 40` (`256 = 6*40 + 16`), so the phase test cannot separate them. Legality by frame index steps from **39-55 %** illegal at frames 0-5 to **0 of 2384** at `+256`, the first six slots are raw `int16`, the block is not audio, and the shipped 256-byte skip is correct |
| "frame 0 carries an **illegal predictor** on 556-582 samples" (M238) | **M239** | there is no frame 0: bytes 16..255 are not nibble frames, so the reported anomaly was an artifact of numbering frames that the block occupies |
| "the game's private dispatch is **mostly over its own state**, not over a script" (M230, from ctx_field 19 vs command_field 5) | **M231** | sharper and differently weighted: with the fold fixed, **32 of 83 heads index a *global*** — a byte in a module-level array — so the dominant source is global state, then the context field (19), then a caller's argument (15). The command stream is the **rarest** named source at 5 |

## The errors worth remembering

1. **A guessed example poisoned fifteen files (M7).** `tools/wrap_mips_elf.py`'s usage line said `--base 0x80000000`, so every hand-computed vram address was `0x480` low. It survived five milestones because the wrong addresses still pointed at real code. *Check the base before using any address, and prove it numerically.*

2. **The `jal` sweep needed the 26-bit mask and the real function start.** Sweeping for the encoding of an interior address reports "no callers" no matter how many exist — that produced a nonexistent cross-bank foothold and a whole arc of dead ends.

3. **Three null results were filter artefacts.** `offset < 0x4000000` (the real offsets carry a `0xB` flag), adjacent `(rom, vram)` pairs (the record is `offset, size, vram, vram_end`), and `CMF == 0x78` (these streams use `0x68`). Each time the data was present and the search constant was invented.

4. **A constant's *mode* is part of the constant (M84).** `lui 0x800c` + `addiu v0,v0,-23464` is `0x800c0000 - 0x5ba8 = 0x800ba458`; the scanner beside the disassembler composed it with `ori` semantics and got `0x800ca458`. The wrong address was *plausible* — it pointed at the same .rodata bank — so the tables looked empty and a runtime writer was hunted for two sessions. The disassembler printed `-23464` on the same line as the bug: **when two tools disagree, check which one is being consumed, not which one looks trustworthy.**

5. **A hand-composed address is where every remaining error lives (M84/M86/M87 — four instances).** `ori` for `addiu` gave `0x800ca458` for `0x800ba458`; `+ 0xb264` for a sign-extended `lw` gave `0x800fb264` for `0x800eb264`; a 28-payload span was quoted as 81 852 instead of 74 716; and `0x80004550` was read out of the stage-2 image when it lives in the boot bank. **The fix is `tools/vaddr.py`, not discipline**: `sext16`/`compose(hi, imm, mode)` with no default mode, and `locate()` refusing when several images share the address.

6. **A register name is not a register value.** Grepping `sw …,4(v1)` gave 105 apparent PI writes; a small data-flow window that aborts on redefinition gave the true 2.

7. **An analysis rule under-invalidates as easily as it under-matches (M95).** Constant tracking that skips **loads** leaves stale values alive: `t4` kept `0x80113a34` across `lw`s and every access through it was reported as if it hit that address — 167 false near-misses around a table whose real reader sat one line away. The scan's *rule*, not just its arithmetic, decides what can be seen.

8. **A near-miss list is a candidate list, not evidence (M95/M99, three instances).** `find_readers`' base+register hits were taken as attributions twice — `0x80113a34` in M93 and `0x80171e80` in M98 — and both fell apart on reading the site (a loaded-over register; gameplay float code). The rule: a scanner output becomes a fact only after the instruction at that PC is read.

9. **Never convert an address in your head (sixth instance, M94).** Locating a pointer table I read `2148612520` as `0x80113a68`; it is `0x801139a8`, off by `0xC0`. The wrong address produced a table of *different* real function pointers — plausible, and therefore dangerous. `python3 tools/vaddr.py <addr>` and `tools/find_readers.py` answer the question without arithmetic; the mitigation for this class is asking them, not being careful.

10. **A file only a remote service parses is unverified (M174).** `.github/workflows/acceptance.yml` was committed in M128, rewritten in M130, and **never ran once**: an unquoted `: ` inside a plain scalar made it invalid YAML, so GitHub rejected it at load and nothing in the repo could notice — the file that makes CI run is the one file CI cannot test. Two milestones of prose about "51 of 54 cases" were describing scheduled work that did not exist. *Parse the artefact locally, and give it a gate that needs no remote.*

11. **`ls` hides the directory you are looking for (M174).** Asked to add a CI workflow, I wrote a second one: `.github` is a dotfile directory, so a plain `ls` of the root did not show it and I never checked. The answer was in the repo the whole time. *Ask git (`git ls-files .github`), not your eyes.*



## Working method that produced the answer

- **Change an execution model only under a fixture, and encode the *architecture*, not the current
  output (M87).** The harness passed for a year against a countdown loop that touched `addiu`,
  `addu`, `bnez` and `jr` — none of `LB`, `LH`, `SWL`, `SWR`. "Verified path" was true of the whole
  and false of the parts. Write the failing table first (5/15), fix, then require both the new table
  and the old smoke test to pass (15/15). The same form was used for CP0, which is why CP0 stayed
  fixed.
- **Read the cheap artefact first**: `rea decompile … --format json` puts pseudocode in
  `normalized_result` as a plain string; `rea instructions` returns a bounded array. Reserve
  `detail:"complete"` for fixtures.
- **A dispatcher states its own table length (M84).** `sltiu v0,v1,K` immediately before a
  `lw`/`jr` dispatch bounds the jump table at `K` entries. Checking `entries_found == K` is a
  falsifiable test, not an inspection: it caught a walk that read 27 entries where the table had 12,
  because it ran on through the padding into the next table. Assumption to state: a table with a
  default case has `K + 1` entries and will trip this test.
- **Prefer derivations over inspections**: 1 226 known frame transitions score a length field better
  than any amount of staring at a hexdump, and the score is falsifiable.
- **Self-consistency is not truth**: the trailer rule is only believed because an independent
  consumer (`ffprobe`) accepts the concatenated output.
- **Keep discovery and extraction separate** (`scan_headers.py` vs `extract_blocks.py`), so a wrong
  hypothesis cannot quietly produce wrong output files.
| M196 | **the suite was slow because it recomputed, not because it was Python** — the profile, before anything changed: `cProfile` over `file_table.py --check` put **26.26 s of 35.6 s (87.9 %)** in one per-bit closure (`get`, 37 795 369 calls, 130 564 153 `len()` calls) added in M195. That loop read one bit at a time **to throw it away** — `_parse` returns a bit position, and per-sample widths are constants — so the cursor now advances **O(1) per block, O(260) before**, and the restatement is exact because it reproduces both pinned verdicts unchanged: **362/588** overrun + 3 landed (MSB), **339/588** + 0 (LSB). `file_table.py --check` **31 s → 3.8 s**, 87/87. **It was also being computed 13 times per `verify.py`**: `ci.sh` ran the audit inside acceptance and `verify.py` ran it again as admission, so the same deterministic scan was paid twice per run — `--no-audit` now hands it off (printing **DEFERRED**, not SKIP, because a hand-off and a hole must not look alike) and the audit runs once, last, as its own header requires. **`JOBS`** (1, capped 4) runs suites concurrently under a contract — `JOBS=4` prints byte-identically to `JOBS=1` — graded by a new **6-case `acceptance.py --self-test`** and **3 mutations** for the three ways a pool can lie (dropped result, completion-order report, swallowed death); each was probed directly, and the first version of the cases **crashed instead of failing**, which the audit's exit-code rule would have recorded as a detection, so the cases now access results through a guarded helper. The M195 case had **no audit at all** — six file_table mutations, all predating it — so making it fast would have made it the least-audited case in the suite; the new mutation removes the block body and moves both verdicts (probed: 85/87, MSB 45/588, LSB 47/588), where aiming at the 512 KiB guard would have been **vacuous** (no clip reaches it). **The compiled-language question, answered with a measurement and declined**: a Python byte loop runs at 48 MB/s against 3 243 MB/s for a C-backed `bytes.count`, so a rewrite can buy 50-70× — but the winning change **removed the iterations rather than accelerating them**, and a compiled gate would still have to pay for the audit mutating source text, `counts.py`/`ratchet.py` `exec()`ing `acceptance.py`, and the README's "nothing to compile". Kept as a documented fallback (C, not Rust/Zig) behind three stated conditions. **One defect found while measuring**: `ci.sh` claimed "10 of 34 suites" where a clone runs **21**, and `counts.py` pinned the case count in that comment but not the suite count | `docs/log/M196-the-suite-was-recomputing.md`, `tools/file_table.py`, `tools/acceptance.py`, `tools/audit_gates.py`, `tools/verify.py`, `tools/ci.sh` |
| M197 | **the ring does nothing to the bytes, and the reader reverses them — the voice stream reconciles on 588/588** | M195 named the blocker as "the decoder reads a 4 KiB ring, and what that copy does is unestablished". Both halves are now measured and both were wrong. **The copy is a verbatim DMA**: `FUN_800b4a04` calls a **function pointer at ctx+0x190** with `(base + writepos, ctx + 0x1a0 + (writepos & 0xFF8), 0x400, ctx + 0x11ac)` and polls completion through an OS message — 0x400-byte chunks into the ring at **ctx+0x1a0, wrapped at 4 KiB**, which ends exactly at ctx+0x11a0 where the state struct begins. **The real defect was my bit order**: `FUN_800b53f4` does `sra/andi 0xffc` → `lw` (a big-endian 32-bit load) → `srlv a0, a0, (bitpos & 31)`, so bit *p* is bit `p & 31` of the BE word at `p >> 5` **counted from the LSB** — i.e. the four bytes of every 4-byte group are consumed in **reverse order, low bit first**. That third order is the one M195 did not try, and it reconciles: **0/588 overrun, every stream ends within 32 bits (min 1) of its record**, while M195's two guesses reproduce their numbers exactly (**362/588** + 3 land; **339/588** + 0). The arithmetic closes independently: the **307 038** declared blocks are **262 894 normal (85.6 %) + 44 144 silent (14.4 %)**, and 0.856 × 260 = **222.6** bits per block against the payload's **222.8** — and `260 / 222.8 = 1.17` is precisely the over-budget figure M194 used to refute the block count. **Two retractions, one of them a flip-flop recorded as one**: M195's ring-transform blocker is withdrawn, and **`u16@+4` is the block count after all** (M193 said yes, M194 said not established, M197 measures yes — M194's premise, 260 bits for *every* block, was an upper bound that ignored the silent 14.4 %). Also for free: the bit position starts at **0x60 = 96 bits = byte 12**, so the 12-byte `MORT` header is skipped by the reader itself. **Still missing and not claimed: the synthesis.** `FUN_800b62c0` dequantises the 13 coefficients (`((c*0x2000 - 0x7000) * ((scale&7)*0x800 + 0x47ff) + 0x4000) >> 15`) and scatters them at **stride 3** from `table + phase`, four times per block; `FUN_800b4dc0` maps the eight scales through eight fixed-point affines into two alternating banks at ctx+0x168; `FUN_800b6634` mixes them. **No audio is decoded** | `docs/log/M197-the-ring-does-nothing-and-the-reader-reverses.md`, `tools/file_table.py` |
| M198 | **the voice bank decodes to audio — 588 clips, 49 126 080 samples, 51.2 minutes** | M197 left the synthesis as the open work. `FUN_800b4a04` drives 160 samples per call and `FUN_800b53f4` runs it: per group `FUN_800b62c0` **dequantises the 13 coefficients** (`((c*0x2000-0x7000) * ((scale&7)*0x800+0x47ff) + 0x4000) >> 15`, byte table at `0x800b63ac` = `-57,-41,-29,-25,-15,-13,-11,-9`) and **scatters them at stride 3** from `table + phase`; `FUN_800b63b4` is the **long-term predictor** (period latched into `ctx+0x166` when 40…120, history slid by 40, then `((hist[120-lag+i]*gain + 0x4000)>>15) + residual[i]` with `gain` from `0x0ccd,0x2ccd,0x5333,0x7fff`); `FUN_800b4dc0` maps the eight scale fields through eight fixed-point affines into **one of two banks** at `ctx+0x168`/`0x178` (`ctx+0x188` toggles); and `FUN_800b6634` runs **four sub-bands** of 12/13/12/119 samples through `FUN_800b6788` (bank mix by one of four shapers at `0x800b682c/6844/6854/686c`) and `FUN_800b6470` (an 8-tap lattice with a shared accumulator). **The synthesis is EXECUTED, not transcribed** — and that is stated as the limit it is: `FUN_800b6634` passes its arguments in registers the pseudocode does not model (`FUN_800b6788()` with no arguments, `(*in_t3)()` eight times, real inputs in `t0/t1/t3/t6-t9` and `s0-s7`), so there is nothing faithful to transcribe, and inventing one is the guess M194 refused. `tools/voice_decode.py` therefore runs stage-2's own instructions under Unicorn with the ring refilled by the host at the window the reader asks for, and `memset` held out as a host primitive. **Measured audio**: clip 27 → 4 160 samples, peak 26 896, rms 5 145, lag-1 **+0.680**; clip 2 → 809 280 samples (**50.58 s**), peak 30 768, rms 2 608, lag-1 **+0.882**, **116 silent blocks exactly zero**, **0 samples railed**; ZCR median **0.125**, 113 loud / 129 near-silent windows, first-difference energy ratio **0.237** (white noise ≥ 1). **Not claimed**: sample-identical output to a console — there is no reference recording, and a Python transcription is still open, now with an oracle to grade it against. **Three of my own instrumentation bugs produced plausible wrong audio** before being caught: `bitpos` never seeded (reads silence), the ring **12 bytes out of phase** (fields shifted → 34–40 % railed), and a **cumulative step budget** that began aborting blocks mid-clip (lag-1 collapsed to +0.036) — the argument for gating numbers rather than listening | `docs/log/M198-the-voice-bank-decodes-to-audio.md`, `tools/voice_decode.py`, `tools/file_table.py` |
| M199 | **the voice synthesis is transcribed, and it agrees with the cartridge sample for sample** | M198 produced audio by **executing** the cartridge's code, because `FUN_800b6634` passes its arguments in registers the pseudocode does not model. It is now reimplemented in Python and graded against the emulator: **13 clips, 634 blocks, 0 samples differing**. Two defects stood in the way, and neither was findable by reading. **(1) The taps were unsigned** — the kernels return `t & 0xFFFF` and the cartridge unpacks with `sra` (a signed halfword), so my lattice multiplied `65126` where the cartridge multiplies `-410`; invisible while every tap is positive, which is exactly why frame 1 of every clip was exact and everything after it was not. **(2) The shaper is three segments, not two**: `2a` to **11059**, then `a + 11059` to **20070**, then `(a>>2) + 26112` — probed off K4 at `0x800b686c`, where a 11468 operand gives **22527**, not the 22936 my two-segment reading produced. What the transcription now contains: the bit reader (LSB-first in big-endian words), the field map, `FUN_800b62c0`'s dequantisation and **stride-3 scatter**, the pitch predictor (period latched 40…120, history shifted by 40, window read **after** the shift, **40** residuals per call), `FUN_800b4dc0`'s eight affines and the bank toggle (written, mixed, **then** flipped), and `FUN_800b6470`'s eight-stage lattice with interleaved updates, per-sample accumulator and `& 0xFFF8` output. Three of those are "the loop runs once more than it looks": band counts are `t3+1` = **13/14/13/120** (`bgtz` tests the counter before the delay slot decrements), 40 residuals per predict (hence the 40-entry table), and the accumulator truncates to a halfword **once per block**. **Gated differentially**: two suite cases compare transcription against emulator block for block (clip 27, 26 blocks; clip 2, 40 blocks — both 0 differ). **Six of my own probes were wrong** before that, each producing a confident wrong answer for a turn: a `t3` read before the loop decrement, a lag read before the latch, a double sign-conversion (equal values printed as −4294967344, twice explained away instead of fixed), a one-band mislabel that manufactured a "phase offset", a hand-rolled predict that double-dequantised, and an output compared against a history taken at another moment. The rule that finally worked: **verify the arithmetic in isolation, then wire it up** | `docs/log/M199-the-synthesis-is-transcribed.md`, `tools/voice_decode.py` |
| M200 | **the pool's classes pinned from the data, not from prose** | Three sessions of prose about "the pool's four key classes" ended with no gate, so the mapping is now measured. **The correction that made it possible: the tag is `(key >> 14) & 3` and it is NOT the section index** — the four `SND_POOL` sections are tagged **0, 3, 1, 2** in file order, which is why "four classes" and "four sections" had been used for each other since M188. The sections are storage (2025 / 241 / 3 / 326 records; 32–56 / **16** / 3×1032 / 36–72 bytes); the roles are the **six lists** a `SND_PROJ` record carries. **Gated two ways**: by data (the six lists hold `[2027, 169, 736, 290, 65, 566]` distinct keys, drawing on tags `[[0],[0,3],[0],[0,3],[3],[0,1,2,3]]` — `[2]`/`[4]` sample-tagged only, `[6]` tag-3 only) and by code (`FUN_800a86a0` walks the pool chain — key at `record+4`, accumulating offset, `-1` terminator — and hands `[4]`→`0x8014e1e0`, `[5]`→`0x801521f0`, `[6]`→`0x80152a00`, the only one passing a length read from the pool record at `+10`). **Both mutations were VACUOUS on the first attempt** — `& 7` is behaviour-identical to `& 3` for the tag values that occur, and dropping one of two needle checks leaves the other satisfied; re-aimed at the mechanism, the audit is **50/50, 0 vacuous**. **The names are still not claimed**: cache A is sample-keyed and cache C sized over the 16-byte class, but nothing yet says what those records are | `docs/log/M200-the-pools-classes-pinned.md`, `tools/file_table.py`, `tools/audit_gates.py` |
| M201 | **the 16-byte class-3 records are little-endian, and +10 is a rate field** | The records are `{stride=16, key@+4, four u16 params@+8..+14}` with the stride identical in **241/241**. Read big-endian the parameters look like noise (59395, 62465, 53255, 40975); **byte-swapped they are round numbers** — `0xE803`→**1000**, `0xF401`→**500**, `0xD007`→**2000**, `0xA00F`→**4000**, `0xFA00`→**250** — so the parameters are **little-endian inside a big-endian container**. **And the two fields differ in kind**: `+10` holds 1000 ×99, 3000, 4000, 5000 **plus 8000 and 16000**, the two rates the voice bank uses, so it is a **rate field**; `+14` holds 1000 ×140, 0 ×38, 500, 250, 200 and **no rate at all**, so it is a level or a duration. **My first version of the case asserted "no canonical rate in either field" and the gate failed on its first run** — the corrected case is stronger than the guess: `+10` is a rate field *because* 8000 and 16000 are in it. One mutation reads the parameters big-endian and the case fails; audit **51/51, 0 vacuous**. Not established: what `+8` and `+12` are (50 and 33 distinct values, the same encoding presumably, not yet round enough to name) | `docs/log/M201-the-16-byte-records-are-little-endian.md`, `tools/file_table.py` |
| M202 | **the four parameters named, and cache B is a pairing** | M201 established the little-endian encoding and that `+10` is the rate. The rest of the record: **`+8` is a time** (1000 ×29, 2000 ×20, 1500 ×18, 500 ×16, range 0–14000), **`+12` a size in bytes capped at 4096** (4096 ×100, 2048 ×7, range 0–**4096** — the exact ring-fill chunk of `FUN_800b4a04` from M197, which is why it is a size and not a count), and **`+14` a level** (1000 ×140, 0 ×38, 500, 250, 200). **Cache B is a pairing, not a set**: list `[5]` decomposes as **142 sample-tagged + 148 tag-3 keys, nothing else**, and **all 142 sample keys also appear in list[2]**, the full sample list — so it pairs 142 samples with 148 parameter records, which is why the only mixed-tag list feeds the cache that holds a resolved object. Two mutations, both non-vacuous (reading `+12` big-endian, and inverting the subset test); audit **53/53, 0 vacuous**. **The four caches now**: A ← list `[4]` sample-keyed; B ← list `[5]` the pairing; C ← list `[6]`, 65 tag-3 keys with a length. Still open: what the engine calls a sample and a parameter set, and what cache C's length means when the records already carry a size at `+12` | `docs/log/M202-the-parameters-and-the-pairing.md`, `tools/file_table.py` |
| M203 | **+20 and +24 are a loop start and a loop *length*, and the cartridge adds them** | M193's "loop start / loop end" came from a description and was never measured; the reading is wrong in one sign. The reader **`FUN_800ada34`** loads both words with `lw` (`0x800adad8`, `0x800adae8`) and masks the length word with `0xffffff`; the voice setup **`FUN_800a54ec`** stores the remainder as `length - (word20 + word24)` (`0x800a5618` `addu` then `0x800a561c` `subu`), zeroed below 10; and the mixer feed **`FUN_800a3bf4`** compares `word20 + word24` with the play position (`0x800a3cfc`), then after the wrap reads from `word20` for `word24 - (pos - word20)` more samples (`0x800a3d4c`–`0x800a3d64`). A start/end pair is never *added*. Note-off (`FUN_800a6c04` → `FUN_800a59d0`, a one-line `voice[ch*0x68+1] = 0`) is the byte the mixer tests, so a held voice loops and a released one plays the remainder out. **Measured on 1 192 records**: **187** name a loop, the other **1 005** carry 0 at `+24` and **exactly 2** at `+20` (one filler, inert because the mixer tests `+24` first); `start + length <= declared` on **187/187**, exactly equal on **112**, remainder 0…8 185 (≤ 32.6 % of its sample); `start + length <= frames × 64` on **187/187**, so the unit is a decoded sample. Three falsifications gated: the end reading needs `start < end` and fails on **64/187**; a 16-bit field cannot hold the three lengths **117 141 / 73 630 / 65 836** (truncating turns one remainder into 99.3 %); a byte unit does not fit the sample count. **Two retractions carried**: M193's "+24 loop end", and my own mid-investigation `u16` read of the declared length, which manufactured four phantom violations — the field is **24 bits** at `+17..+19` and byte `+16` is the codec. **Negative kept**: the decoded waveform does **not** confirm the seams (seam jump median **0.306** RMS against **0.198** for an interior step), so the loop points are structural metadata and the naming rests on the code and the arithmetic, not on the sound. The word at `+8` stays zero in the file and lands in the voice's base-pointer slot, so it is patched at load; that site is not established | `docs/log/M203-loop-start-and-loop-length.md`, `tools/file_table.py`, `tools/audit_gates.py` |
| M204 | **there is no +28 — the record ends at +27, and those bytes are the next record's** | The question asked what `+28` and `+32` of the sample record mean; the record is **28 bytes**, so the answer is addressing, not a field. Measured: `record k`'s `+28` is `record k+1`'s `u16` index on **1 191/1 191** adjacencies and its `+32` is that record's byte offset on **1 191/1 191**; the last record's `+28` is the `ffffffff` terminator, which is also the member's last byte (**33 380 = 1 192 x 28 + 4**), and the `u16` at `+0` takes **1 192** distinct values, so no wrap can imitate a second layout. No code can read a 29th byte: the search's stride is `0x1c` (`_li a3, 0x1c` at `0x800adaa4`), the count walker in `FUN_800ad330` steps `0xe` shorts to a `-1` at `+0`, and the reader's highest load is `lw v0, 0xc(v1)` with `v1 = record + 0xc` — `record + 0x18`, ending at **byte 27**. The descriptor it fills is seven words, so `+20`/`+24` (M203) are the record's last two. **Where the addresses do exist** is the 0x68-byte voice (`+0x1c` one of four per-voice level words, `+0x20` the first field of the block `FUN_800a591c` sets) — named to place the addresses, **not measured here**, no claim made. Nothing is left open: this is a refutation of a framing, asserted so that a future field at `+28` fails a gate instead of shifting every record by four. **Process note**: the first stride mutation replaced 28 with 32, ran past the member, and died on `struct.error` — the audit would have scored that as *detected*, the exact vacuous shape it exists to catch, so it was replaced by one that fails with a message | `docs/log/M204-there-is-no-plus-28.md`, `tools/file_table.py`, `tools/audit_gates.py` |

Three findings: `code_index.callees` is exact **only** over the provider's claimed range list (22 functions have an internal gap, and a contiguous scan mis-attributes calls to all 22); `jump_tables.json` bounds `lw`+`jr` switch blocks while `routine_index.py` bounds the `jalr` call tables — **disjoint sets**, so neither is the authority for both; and a pointer table cannot be bounded by shape (`0x8011afb8` walks 46 entries where M173 established 14). | M217 | **TYRANT is a codename, and what each layer may claim** | The reconstructed script execution model is called **TYRANT** in this project's documentation and tooling — a codename, stated as one: nothing in the cartridge names it, so first mention carries the caveat. It resolves a vocabulary problem (three documents and eleven tools called the same thing four different ways, and `walk_stream.py` is a walker, not a VM) at no evidentiary cost. The reviewer's three-layer split is adopted because it protects the property this series has worked hardest for — **the decoder that refuses must not become an interpreter that improvises**: layer 1 (decoder) claims instructions, boundaries, successors, scheduler state and refusal, and no gameplay effect; layer 2 (semantic interpreter) claims what an understood instruction does to an explicit game-state model, and nothing about the original's memory layout; layer 3 (host/simulation) is out of scope. Two rules carried: **`UnimplementedEffect` must be a refusal distinct from `UnresolvedAdvance`** (a known encoding with an unknown effect must never be a no-op — the defect this project has caught in its own tooling twice), and **Python first, C17 later, only after behaviour is verified**. | M226 | **the `0x36` table: five entries, and an address that was 0x10000 wrong** | `tools/callback_table.py` -> `manifests/callback_table.json`. Since M211 the table was described as `jr <*(0x800c98e0 + (s16[cmd+4] >> 8)*4)>` -- "a callback pointer, not a command address" -- and both halves were wrong. **The address**: `addiu v0,v0,-26400` sign-extends, so `0x98e0` is -0x6720 and the base is **0x800b98e0**, 0x10000 lower than published; the published address holds 259 zero words, which is why "empty, so runtime-filled" looked true. **The dispatch**: `idx = u16[cmd+4] >> 8`, bounds-checked by `sltiu v0,a0,5`, into five static pointers `800711cc 800711dc 800711fc 8007121c 8007124c` -- the five `switchD_800711c4::caseD_*` the corpus has always had. What *is* runtime-initialised is the base objects the arms select, which is where that reading belonged. **Consequences**: registry 131+15 -> **132+14**; opcodes without a constant advance 10 -> **9**; those left unresolved 1 -> **0**; rooms decoding cleanly **107 -> 109** of 256; refusals **59 -> 57**; `unresolved_advance` 2 -> **0** (rooms 6 and 21 decode); distinct instruction lengths 17 -> **18**. | M235 | **loop discontinuities: there are none, and the reason is silence** | The backlog carried "loop discontinuities in the voice bank" as an open defect; measured over all **588** MORT clips of cartridge id 1979, the premise does not hold. **440** clips end at exactly `0` and **518** begin there (**393** do both), so the seam between a clip's last sample and its first is small for the same reason its first sample is — the bank is **silence-bounded**, not loop-seamed. The sharper half is a **control**: `wrap = abs(s[0]-s[-1])` is compared with the clip's own **p99** of adjacent differences, and the same test is run at the **midpoint**, an ordinary boundary with no structural meaning. The wrap exceeds its p99 on **0** clips where chance alone predicts **5.88** — `P(0 of 588 \| p=0.01) = 0.0027`, i.e. *less* discontinuous than a random boundary — while the midpoint fires on **8** against 5.88, so the test can fire and there is simply no seam to see. The p99 is per clip on purpose: the largest wrap, **9 816**, sits inside that clip's own p99 of ~9 952, so a threshold on raw magnitude would have called it a defect. The residue is reported rather than smoothed: **70** clips neither begin nor end at zero, the worst ending at **22.5 %** of its own peak, and every one of their wraps is still inside its p99. Refused: no loop point is read from a MORT record (M194's header is `MORT`, rate, length in words, count), so "the clip loops" is not a claim this can make in either direction, and a small wrap is evidence of zeros rather than of looping. `tools/voice_loops.py` -> `fixtures/sound/loop_sweep.json`; 7 cases, 3 mutations; the sweep is manual (40 s at 8 workers) and the gate re-derives a **5-clip stratum** (0, 146, 293, 439, 587 — the same set `voice_sweep.py` gates on) and reads the whole-bank aggregates from the artefact, which the case labels state | `docs/log/M235-loop-discontinuities.md`, `tools/voice_loops.py`, `fixtures/sound/loop_sweep.json` |
| M236 | **the per-scene voice lists are in the image, and bit 15 proves it** | M233 refused the voice-id mapping because `DAT_8011a718` is zero in the image; that was **too pessimistic** — the *selection* is dynamic, the *lists* are static. `FUN_800762b0` sets `base = *((&PTR_PTR_80119e20)[0x800e110c < 0] + DAT_800e8ca8*4 + 4)`, and `0x80119e20` holds two pointers, `0x80119de0` (**16** pointers) and `0x80119e00` (**8**, all of them in the first table): two **banks** over one region of **16** distinct `u16` arrays from `0x80115440` to `0x801158ec`, lengths **14..84**, **674** entries, **8** skipped slots of `0`. They are clip indices, and the third fact decides it: masking bit 15 leaves every entry below **588**, the MORT bank size, and **bit 15 predicts the target clip's own declared rate on 673 of 674 entries** — `u16@+6` in the MORT record (M194) agrees with `0x8000`-means-8 kHz on 38 at 8 000 and 636 at 16 000. That is one field predicting another across 674 samples of an independently parsed structure. **The one exception is named, not smoothed**: at `0x801158ec` index 64 the entry is `0x003d` — clip **61** without the flag — while clip 61's record declares 8 000 Hz and the same clip appears flagged in the first array, so bit 15 is what the *caller* passes and the 674th case is not decidable from these bytes. The chain now closes end to end: `XaOn(byte, idx)` -> `list[bank][idx]` -> clip (`& 0x7fff`, rate from bit 15) -> MORT record -> PCM, verified on the default list's first entry (`0x803d` -> clip 61, 194 blocks, 31 040 samples, 8 000 Hz by both readings). | M237 | **the clip-61 rate exception: real in the data, and not a reading error** | M236 named one disagreement out of 674 and stopped there; asked whether it was a real outlier or a typo, the answer is **real**, and three checks say the reading is not the problem: the raw word at `0x801158ec + 2*64` is `0x003d` dumped as words; **82 of 83** non-zero entries in that same array resolve and agree with their records; and **6 of its 6 flagged entries** point at 8 kHz records — the convention the exception violates is followed six times *inside the list that carries it*, which a mis-segmentation could not produce (and unsortedness is not concatenation: other arrays carry up to **6** descents, this one 4). The flag is a **selector, not decoration**: tabulated against the records it is **38 set → 8 kHz, 0 set → 16 kHz, 1 clear → 8 kHz, 635 clear → 16 kHz**, where an unrelated flag would disagree **61.4** times because 565 of 588 records are 16 kHz. It disagrees **once**, so bit 15 is **61× more consistent than chance** and the single disagreement is a fact about the data. Its shape is **one lost bit** rather than a different policy: 39 references point at 8 kHz clips, **38 flagged and 1 not**; clip 61 is **the only clip referenced both ways** (`[2 flagged, 1 unflagged]`); the other **22** 8 kHz clips are flagged at **every** reference. **The counter-argument is real and stated**: at 16 kHz an 8 kHz clip plays at **double speed**, which is hard to miss by ear, so "deliberate" survives — and either way the *behaviour* is identical, because `FUN_80004a6c` passes the rate it computed from this bit and never consults the record's field, making the **list authoritative and `u16@+6` a label**. What is decided: not a typo, the flag is load-bearing, the list is what the player is told; what no byte decides is which of the two readings made it. `tools/voice_lists.py` carries `rate_cross`, `mixed_clips` and `last_array_rule` as derived fields with **12 cases** (three new) and a new mutation that tabulates the flag as always clear | `docs/log/M237-the-clip-61-rate-exception.md`, `tools/voice_lists.py`, `manifests/voice_lists.json` |
| M238 | **the sampler record decoded, and the six frames the decoder never sees** | The next bank after id 1979 is id **1983**; its codec is not what moves here, the **record** around it is. The 28-byte `SND_SMPDIR` record is now fully decoded — `u16 index`, `u16 0`, `u32 offset`, `u32 0`, `u16 15360`, `u16 rate`, `u8 3`, `u24 count`, `u32 f1`, `u32 f2` — with `15360` and the codec byte `3` constant on **all 1192**, two fields zero on all of them, and the count a **u24 starting at byte +17** (which is why its high byte read as a 0/1 "flag" on 1182/10 records: those ten are just the samples over 65 535). **The per-sample prefix is 16 bytes, not 256**: every stored length is `16 mod 40` on **1192 of 1192**, and the frame grid begins there — legal predictor bytes on **89.7–100 %** of frames at phase 16 against **≤79.7 %** at every other phase. The 256-byte codebook is real (the head decodes 49/50 sampled clips above 0.7 against 0–32/50 for random slices) and sits inside sample 0's extent; since **`256 = 6*40 + 16`**, skipping 256 from every sample stays frame-aligned and silently **drops 384 samples per sample**, which is what `sound_decode.py` does today. **That skip hides a real anomaly**: frame 0's run-0 predictor is illegal on **568** samples (353 of them `0xff`) and run 1's on **582**, so the first frame of about half the bank is not a well-formed frame under the two-run reading — and no run has ever had to say so. `f1`/`f2` are **positions** (`f1 < count` on 1192, `f2 ≤ count` on 1192) but are **not established as loop points**: **1005** records carry the no-loop pair `(2,0)`, and of the **187** with `f2 > 0` only **123** are ordered while **64** are not, and no convention repairs all of them. Two refutations keep the prefix open: it is **not** a seed for frame 0's history (2 of 1191) and **not** a copy of the previous sample (0 of 63). 10 cases, 3 mutations. The `sound_decode.py` fix is deliberately not made here — it moves that suite's own 1180/658 and the codec fixture, so it is its own change; this is the measurement that shows it is needed | `docs/log/M238-the-sampler-record-and-the-six-skipped-frames.md`, `tools/smpdir.py`, `manifests/smpdir.json` |
| M239 | **the prefix is 256 after all: M238's central claim was wrong and the patch it implied must not be made** | The instruction was to patch `sound_decode.py` to a 16-byte prefix; chasing the frame-0 anomaly first stopped it. **Every stored length is `16 mod 40`, and `256 = 6*40 + 16` — so the phase test M238 relied on cannot separate 16 from 256**, and it was reported as if it decided the prefix. **The census that does** anchor a grid at `+16` and count illegal predictor bytes **by frame index**: **48/52/42/55/39/49 %** illegal at frames 0-5 and **0 of 2384** from frame 6 on — a step at exactly `+256`. Bytes 16..255 are raw `int16`, which is why their "predictors" are the high bytes of small negatives (`0xff` ×353, `0xfe` ×79) and why they correlate **0.732** with themselves against **0.062** for nibble data read the same way. So the skip is **correct**, there is **no frame-0 anomaly** (that frame does not exist), and the 256-byte block is **not audio**: magnitude median **397** and max **17 696** against the **32 768** the decoded audio reaches, best correlation **0.419** / **0.465** with the clip's head and tail — a per-sample **parameter** block whose 16-byte sub-header plus 240 bytes mirrors the codebook's 8 × 32 record layout, which is a shape worth testing and is not claimed. The tell was read past: under the 16-model the slack was `122..519` with **one sample needing a different prefix from the others**; under 256 it is **72..135 for all 1192**. **What M238 got right stands**: the record layout, the two constants, the u24 count at +17, and the loop refusal (**1005** carry `(2,0)`; **123** of **187** ordered, **64** not).
